Use OneStep-Web.txt for each new substantive task. Start with the sections
agents/vegabond-agent.md and skills/vegabond-scout/SKILL.md, then use the
operation and specialist sections Scout selects. Use this project's instructions,
files and user request first. Resolve OneStep paths as section names in this
document. For game tasks consult the keyword indexes in knowledge/game/index.md,
knowledge/code/index.md and records/code/index.md. Retrieve the matching detail
by ID and use its Evidence and applicability conditions. Report missing
sources or tools and distinguish performed checks from checks needing access.

Project stores, external evidence and executable helpers are supplied by the working environment.

===== agents/vegabond-agent.md =====

# OneStep agent instructions

OneStep is authored by vagabond, whose background includes Unreal Engine game client programming. That background does not limit the project. Support work in any domain by identifying the requested outcome and deliverable, not by maintaining a closed list of industries, engines, or file types. Start small and expand only when real use demonstrates a need.

## Document boundary

- Author maintained project guidance, agent instructions, and skill instructions in English, with separate Korean translations named <stem>.kr.md.
- English guidance is canonical. Never read, load, or search the contents of *.kr.md files, including during reviews, indexing, and delegated work. Exclude them before content retrieval; for example, rg -g '!*.kr.md'.
- Translations are human-only, never instruction entry points or reference inputs. Generate them from the English guidance without rereading them. Do not duplicate skill-discovery frontmatter in translations.
- Task inputs and deliverables follow the user's requested language. A Korean manuscript, presentation, or plan is not automatically a translation or an instruction document. Do not append .kr.md to an original artifact merely because it is Korean.
- These rules do not restrict the language of conversation with the user.

## Entry skill

Paths here are relative to the repository root. For a new substantive task, read skills/vegabond-scout/SKILL.md if not already loaded for this task. Continue the selected procedure on follow-up turns; reassess when the goal or evidence changes.

Scout owns classification, scale, the work brief, capability mapping, and overall coordination. The create, revise, and research operation skills own their workflows and reuse that shared setup. Selected specialists supply domain execution, verification, and correction methods within those workflows. Do not create additional specialist skills, install tools, or delegate merely because routing is possible.

## Knowledge and problem records

OneStep's shared guidance and stores must not contain another project's physical locations or active source connections. Keep that setup in the actual working project. Historical source identities, fingerprints, scoped knowledge and incident evidence may be retained here without location bindings; their presence does not authorize searching for or connecting the original project.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../skills/vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for a focused information request once the task profile is known. Reuse the returned sources, applicability conditions, checks, and gaps in the work brief and execution. Operations and specialists can refine that request when new needs arise; reuse the existing information brief instead of repeating the same lookup. The specialist applies the knowledge and verifies the deliverable. Supply findings, corrections, and reusable lessons back to this skill for authorized storage and maintenance. It owns the common storage and retrieval procedure across domains.

## Completion evidence for any deliverable

Before producing or changing a result, identify its purpose, audience, requested format and language, source material, constraints, acceptance conditions, and how the AI will check the actual final result. Resolve missing intent that materially changes the work; do not add routine permission checkpoints.

The AI produces the result, checks it directly against the acceptance conditions, and reports evidence and limitations before vagabond's independent testing or review. Domain-specific execution, verification, and correction procedures live in the specialists linked from [scout's capability mapping](../skills/vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#connect-steps-to-skills-and-checks). Evidence gathering and source boundaries for research live in the research operation skill.

For other deliverables, choose observable checks based on purpose, medium, and failure modes. Use automation where it provides meaningful evidence; use direct reading, visual inspection, or real use where those are needed. Do not force build, editor, gameplay, slide, or manuscript checks onto unrelated work.

A check is not applicable only when the deliverable genuinely does not require it; explain why and what covers acceptance instead. Missing tools, tests, access, or time make an applicable check blocked, not inapplicable. Apply this distinction to all required build, test, reading, rendering, and use checks.

Evidence must describe the final artifact, not a proxy or a confident summary. Rerun checks invalidated by later edits. Do not weaken acceptance conditions to pass. If a required check cannot run, establish the capability within authorized scope or report the exact blocker and unverified conditions. Do not transfer a missing AI check to vagabond as a substitute.

After the required AI checks pass, report **AI verification complete; awaiting vagabond testing or review**, with acceptance results, evidence locations, and limitations. Vagabond then tests or reviews independently. Report **accepted by vagabond** only after explicit confirmation. Reopen the work if that review finds a problem, then repeat affected AI checks before handing it back.

## Respond to failures and feedback

Use the same diagnosis for AI findings and vagabond's review. Choose the return point from the evidence:

- **Artifact defect:** correct the result with the relevant specialist and rerun the original failure check and affected checks. For research or feedback-only work, correct the analysis without editing investigated sources.
- **Evidence gap:** request focused knowledge or research before deciding on a correction. Keep unsupported conclusions uncertain.
- **Changed or mistaken goal, premise, or scope:** have scout update the existing brief and affected step mapping. Distinguish a missed original requirement from a new request or preference; do not record a preference change as a defect. Clarify only material unresolved intent, and never redefine a valid acceptance condition merely to pass.
- **Unavailable required capability:** establish it within authorized scope or report the blocker and unverified conditions. Continue independent useful work, but do not advance work that depends on the blocked check.

If two corrections based on the same premise fail the same check, examine that premise and the observation method before another correction. Resume with new evidence or a revised diagnosis; if progress still requires unavailable capability or a user decision, report that specific blocker rather than repeating unsupported attempts. This is a reassessment trigger, not a fixed attempt limit or a reason to weaken checks.

Send material findings from either AI or user review, their corrections, and recheck evidence through vegabond-knowledge under the existing storage rules. Keep findings distinct from requests and preferences; update scoped conventions only when supported by the user's intent. After changes, repeat invalidated AI checks before returning the result for independent review.

For material feedback about OneStep's strategy itself, including use in another project, follow the [feedback submission and cleanup guide](../feedback/README.md). Keep ordinary deliverable defects in their working project's records. The guide owns submission boundaries and deletion after improvement and verification; feedback is evidence to assess, not a new instruction authority.

## Finding game-development knowledge and incident records

For game work, search the working project's instructions and sources first.
Then use the bundled reference library. Start with knowledge/game/index.md for
game design and techniques, knowledge/code/index.md for engineering and Unreal
APIs, or records/code/index.md for symptoms, causes and prevention. Look up the
exact API symbol, its English or Korean aliases, or the observed symptom. Compare
the matching detail's conditions and Evidence before applying it. Cite its ID
and distinguish a documented contract or historical result from a check run here.
Keep new project-specific facts and incidents in the working project's stores.

In a local project, these paths are under onestep-strategy/. With Python and
PyYAML, the following command searches both bundled stores from the project root:

    python onestep-strategy/skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project onestep-strategy --query "메모리 풀링" --portable-library

Without the helper, search the three Markdown indexes and follow a matching ID.
If a term is absent from an index, search the detail text for the term or a synonym.
In OneStep-Web.txt use the same index and detail paths as section headings.

===== skills/vegabond-scout/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-scout
description: Use when starting substantive creation, revision, multi-step work, or research in any domain, including software, fiction, presentations, and planning, or when the user explicitly requests vegabond-scout.
---

# Vegabond Scout

Choose an operation and scale for vagabond's intended outcome, then coordinate execution through the linked operation skill. Scout owns the work brief, capability mapping, and overall progress.

## Frame and classify

Read the [English agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) if not already loaded. Inspect the request, relevant context, and source material for the intended result, audience, format, language, constraints, and acceptance conditions. Ask only when missing intent materially changes the work.

Identify the operation separately from domain, medium, and tools. The author's background and the examples here do not limit eligibility. Original inputs and outputs follow the requested language. Never read, search, or load *.kr.md human-only translations; exclude them from retrieval and any authorized delegated work.

Build one lightweight task profile by answering four questions:

1. **Target:** What material, artifact, behavior, or question is involved, and what result is requested? Include relevant domain, medium/format, and execution context.
2. **Intended change:** What should be added, corrected, transformed, improved, or established? Describe the specific gap between the current state and the requested outcome.
3. **Preservation conditions:** What must remain intact, such as meaning, behavior, voice, facts, format, or source material? Use the request and context; clarify material conflicts or uncertainty.
4. **Method:** What way of working can close that gap while preserving those conditions, and what capabilities and checks does it require? Choose from the actual problem and acceptance conditions rather than a domain label or file extension.

Use open descriptions; a task can involve several domains and methods. A novel-based pitch deck can need narrative analysis, slide production, and visual inspection together. Search can support any operation. Keep the profile in the brief and step mapping, without a separate file or a fixed catalog of task subtypes. Reuse it in the operation skills and refine only missing details or assumptions changed by evidence.

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](references/information-needs.md) to derive questions from decisions, preservation conditions and actual inputs. Request a focused brief from [knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md); carry evidence, applicability, checks and gaps forward. The executing specialist then checks for missing questions after source inspection, reusing valid answers and adding only needs exposed by the work. Revisit affected judgments before consequential reliance and at handoff.

| Operation skill | Choose when |
| --- | --- |
| [vegabond-create](../vegabond-create/SKILL.md) | Producing a new artifact or capability, including one derived from existing sources. |
| [vegabond-revise](../vegabond-revise/SKILL.md) | Improving or correcting an existing artifact or behavior. |
| [vegabond-research](../vegabond-research/SKILL.md) | Answering a question or comparing options while leaving investigated sources unchanged. |

Select the primary operation from the requested outcome. Supporting research does not make a chapter or deck request research-only. A software feature uses create; a software defect uses revise with reproduction and root-cause investigation. Mixed work may combine operations in its steps.

Choose scale independently:

- **single:** a compact task that fits one execution-and-verification cycle; use this by default.
- **staged:** a named intermediate result must be checked before dependent work because of dependencies, costly unresolved assumptions, or scope that makes final-only verification inadequate. Use an appropriate operation at each milestone, then check the integrated result.

Several tools, files, ordinary steps, or long waiting times alone do not require staged work. Risk can require stronger checks without changing scale. If no concrete checkpoint is needed, start single; reassess and update the brief when new dependencies or uncertainty emerge. A full novel can be create + staged; editing one chapter can be revise + single.

For game planning or development in this store's authorized scope, apply the [game evidence reuse convention](../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-RESEARCH-USE.md). Derive retrieval from the concrete decision or symptom; carry selected knowledge IDs, applicability, adoption decisions and actual checks into this same brief. Use the technique guide when a symptom query misses, and refine with the executing specialist. Unrelated work does not require game references.

## Give a short work brief

Before execution, state these in a few lines in the conversation:

- **Goal and deliverable:** what the user will receive, with audience, format, and language when relevant.
- **Operation and scale:** the choices and a short reason.
- **Scope:** the target, intended change, preservation conditions, and meaningful exclusions.
- **Done:** link each acceptance condition to its check; attach the result and evidence as work proceeds. One or two inline lines suffice for a small task; use the existing brief or step mapping, not a separate tracking file.

For staged work, give one sentence explaining the boundary: "Check A before B, because if A is wrong, C would need rework." Name the actual intermediate result, dependent work, and consequence; use its check in the step mapping below.

Proceed within existing authorization without a routine approval checkpoint. Reuse the brief on follow-ups; update it when the goal, scope, or evidence changes. No separate brief file is required.

## Connect steps to skills and checks

For each planned step, identify the needed capability, an available skill or direct tool, and the expected result with its check. For single work, keep the mapping inline; for staged work, use a short list or this table:

| Step | Available skill or direct tool | Expected result and check |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Describe the step. | Use an advertised name, or name the direct tool. | State what it produces and the evidence that checks it. |

Use these local specialists when their methods are needed:

| Specialist | Relevant work |
| --- | --- |
| [vegabond-code](../vegabond-code/SKILL.md) | Software implementation, defects, system design, refactoring, and code investigation. |
| [vegabond-fiction](../vegabond-fiction/SKILL.md) | Fiction development, drafting, critique, and continuity. |
| [vegabond-presentation](../vegabond-presentation/SKILL.md) | Slide narrative, deck production, and content or visual revision. |
| [vegabond-planning](../vegabond-planning/SKILL.md) | Planning/proposal content, feasibility, and success measures; not task scheduling alone. |

The operation owns the workflow; a selected specialist supplies its domain execution, verification, and correction methods. Apply those methods within the operation's steps rather than running a second independent workflow. For research or feedback-only work, use checks that substantiate the findings and correct the analysis without changing investigated sources; production checks apply when producing or changing the corresponding artifact. Combine specialists only where needed, such as planning content and presentation production for a proposal deck. This is an initial set, not a closed list of supported work. Load only the relevant specialists; they can use available environment- or format-specific skills and tools through the same mapping.

Inspect the current skill catalog and use actual names and paths. Read each selected skill when its step needs it. Do not assume a plugin is installed. If no specialist fits, use capable direct tools and briefly note the fallback. Do not create or install skills or delegate merely to satisfy the mapping. Missing required capability is a blocker, not a passed check.

Before substantial production or dependent work, confirm that the essential sources, access, tools, and execution or inspection environment for the planned checks are usable. Use a small relevant probe when availability is uncertain; do not run the entire final check suite at setup. A tool listing alone does not prove readiness. Carry any gap into the brief and follow the agent instructions' blocker handling. This readiness check does not replace verification of the final artifact.

Read the linked operation skill needed for each step; do not load all three by default. Pass the existing brief, four-part profile, source material, and acceptance conditions. The operation skill turns the chosen method into its execution procedure and selects specialists through this shared capability mapping. Update the profile and mapping when evidence changes instead of repeating classification. Reading a skill does not spawn another agent.

For direct invocation of an operation skill, use these shared framing, brief, and mapping sections to fill missing context, then continue in that skill without routing back through scout. It follows the linked agent instructions for common verification and acceptance. Scout consumes the returned result, evidence, and open issues to coordinate dependent steps and the final handoff.

## Execute and hand back

Follow the agent instructions' completion-evidence contract for the actual medium. Execute each step and inspect its result; for staged work, pass the milestone's checks before dependent work. Route failures and feedback using the [shared return-point rules](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md#respond-to-failures-and-feedback), updating only the affected brief, steps, and checks.

At each staged checkpoint, give a one-line update: intermediate result; check and evidence; passed, failed, or blocked; next action. Advance dependent work only after the required checks pass; an update does not request routine user approval.

Continue with the next step's mapped operation and specialist, which may differ from the previous step. Reuse valid context rather than restarting classification. Before final handoff, confirm that each acceptance condition has current evidence or an explicit unresolved status, and check the integrated result.

Report the result, acceptance evidence, and uncertainty. Distinguish drafts, completed AI verification, and vagabond's explicit acceptance. AI checks precede vagabond's independent testing or review.

===== agents/references/design-communication.md =====

# Reader-facing design documents

Use this when composing or handing back a code or fiction design. It governs explanation and readability; each design skill retains its own evidence and correctness review. Use the existing brief or requested artifact, without a mandatory template, extra summary file, or routine approval checkpoint.

## Lead with the decision

Identify the intended reader and what they need to judge or do. Open with a compact decision brief: the goal, chosen direction, what changes and stays intact, the main reason and cost, and unresolved conditions or decisions. A small design can do this in one paragraph. Put any limitation that changes the apparent feasibility, guarantee, canon, or scope here, not only in an appendix. Distinguish a decision the human must make from a remaining implementation or manuscript check; say what that decision affects and the supported options when relevant.

The opening should let the reader explain the choice and its consequences without first learning every API, field, theory term, or element label. Introduce necessary terms beside the behavior they explain. Reuse the reader's known vocabulary and define unfamiliar terms briefly on first use; do not add a large glossary by default.

## Reveal detail in useful order

After the opening, walk through the behavior or narrative sequence, then the structure and reasoning needed to carry it out. Group details around consequential decisions rather than enumerating every internal step or checklist item. Use a small example, diagram, or comparison only when it reduces the explanation the reader must hold in mind.

Keep evidence, important rejected alternatives, counterexamples and verification plans available below the explanation or through precise links to existing sources. Each detail should make clear which choice or condition it supports. Do not delete necessary evidence merely to meet a length target, repeat the same claim in several sections, or create parallel human/agent versions of the design's facts. Within maintained project guidance, the existing English/Korean translation boundary still applies; task artifacts follow the requested language.

Keep the brief and detailed reasoning consistent after revisions. A shorter opening must preserve material uncertainty and tradeoffs, not imply that a partial or blocked design is fully supported. Consolidate repeated explanations and retain source anchors instead of copying whole reports or setting documents.

## Inspect understanding before handoff

Read the final artifact from the intended reader's position. Can its opening explain the goal, choice, impact and important limits? Can an unfamiliar term be understood where it first matters? Is it clear why each detailed section is needed and where to inspect its evidence? Are the human's pending decisions easy to locate? Compare the brief with the detailed design so readability edits cannot silently weaken a requirement or hide a blocker.

When relevant human feedback is available, use whether the reader can restate the choice, cost and uncertainty to improve the document. Do not require a comprehension quiz or approval on every task. AI self-review or a simulated reader may reveal confusion but cannot prove that the user understood; report such checks at their actual evidence level.

===== skills/vegabond-code/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-code
description: Use when implementing software, repairing defects, refactoring, or investigating code and runtime behavior in any language or engine, coordinating design when needed.
---

# Vegabond Code

Execute software work within the active operation. Reuse its brief and profile; follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for common boundaries and completion. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and its linked operation without restarting classification.

## Identify the working environment

Use repository guidance, source and relevant callers, project/dependency configuration, tests, scripts, and CI to establish the changed component's language, engine/framework and versions, toolchain/platform/target, and actual build, test, launch and inspection paths. For new work, distinguish a proposed toolchain from an observed one. Resolve conflicting evidence narrowly; reuse scout's readiness check rather than treating configuration as proof that tools run.

Classify the actual component, not its extension or the repository's main technology. Unreal C++ and independent C++ need their own toolchains; Unity uses its project/package contracts. A Python tool inside an Unreal repository follows its own runtime, adding engine checks when its effects reach assets or engine behavior. For mixed work, identify affected components and boundary checks. Keep source locations and the result in the existing brief, not a separate environment report.

Request relevant contracts, version-specific sources, and prior failure/prevention checks through [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md), reusing the existing information brief. Select available environment skills or direct tools through scout's mapping.

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) after inspecting the component and callers: refine questions about affected state, ownership, lifecycle, failure and verification as relevant. Check actual version and mechanism before applying a retrieved API guarantee or incident fix.

For game implementation, debugging or optimization, apply the [game evidence reuse convention](../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-RESEARCH-USE.md), reusing scout's lookup when valid. Retrieve from the concrete feature or reproduced symptom, check engine/platform and state-authority conditions, and connect the selected technique to a code change and observable check. Preserve rejection reasons and feed measured outcomes back through knowledge; a source-game example is not proof of this implementation.

## Choose the design depth

Use [vegabond-code-design](../vegabond-code-design/SKILL.md) when a meaningful choice about state ownership, lifecycle, interfaces used by other callers, concurrency, persistence, external integration, or migration must be settled before implementation. A local edit following an established pattern can state its relevant contract and preservation conditions inline and proceed. File count alone does not decide; reuse a valid existing design and revisit only decisions changed by evidence.

Pass the current brief, environment, affected source/contracts, constraints, and unanswered decisions. Design returns a bounded implementation direction and checks within this workflow; it does not restart scout or create another execution loop. A design-only request ends with that result rather than an unsolicited implementation.

## Implement or investigate

Read [verification and handoff](references/verification.md) before changing code or judging the result. It owns the detailed preparation, build/test/runtime/real-use checks, review-and-repair loop, verification-change scrutiny, long-work state, and demonstrated safeguards. Apply it to each coherent change and the final integrated artifact; do not defer reading it until completion.

- **Create:** Follow the selected design or existing local pattern and implement a complete usable path. Resolve a load-bearing assumption with a small integration slice before dependent implementation, using scout's existing scale decision.
- **Revise:** Reproduce a defect and trace its cause before repair. For intentional restructuring, preserve the established behavior and public contracts while changing coherent units. Keep unrelated cleanup outside scope.
- **Research:** Trace code, data flow, and runtime evidence; compare options against the question and cite files, symbols, logs, and applicable sources. Leave investigated code unchanged; use an isolated harness for experiments.

If implementation evidence refutes the design, return the affected decision and evidence to code-design, then update the existing brief and checks. A changed goal or premise follows the common return-point rules; do not silently redefine acceptance to fit the implementation.

## Hand back

Return the patch or investigation, acceptance evidence for the actual final code, review results, and explicit unresolved conditions to the active operation. Send confirmed corrections and useful methods or demonstrated safeguards to knowledge for authorized storage; the verification reference defines the software evidence to supply. Distinguish completed AI checks from vagabond's independent acceptance under the common instructions.

===== skills/vegabond-code-design/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-code-design
description: Use when software work needs decisions about architecture, data ownership, lifecycle, interfaces, concurrency, persistence, or migration before implementation, or when a software design is the requested deliverable.
---

# Vegabond Code Design

Settle software design decisions for the calling operation or [vegabond-code](../vegabond-code/SKILL.md). Reuse its brief, environment, sources, and information brief. The caller owns execution and verification; design does not start a second workflow or certify working software. Follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries and common acceptance. Reading this skill does not spawn an agent.

If invoked directly, fill only missing context through [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and [code's environment assessment](../vegabond-code/SKILL.md#identify-the-working-environment). For a design-only request, deliver the design and its supporting evidence without changing production code. Use isolated experiments only when authorized and needed to settle a decision.

For game architecture and technical choices, use the [game evidence reuse convention](../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-RESEARCH-USE.md), including on direct invocation. Reuse valid retrieved evidence, inspect prerequisites such as restorable state for rollback, and record adoption, conditional use or rejection against actual engine, platform and gameplay constraints. Return the selected entry IDs, implementation boundaries and proposed validation with the design.

## Resolve the design

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to test whether required guarantees, boundaries and counterexamples expose missing questions. Keep adopted contracts, conditional assumptions and rejected analogies attached to the choices they affect.

1. **Ground the decision.** Identify the decision to settle, required outcome, preservation conditions, and affected boundaries from the existing brief. Inspect relevant entry points, callers, data, tests, and project conventions. Refine source or prior-lesson questions through [knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) only when the information brief has a concrete gap. Keep required guarantees distinct from proposed tradeoffs; expose conflicting requirements or unavailable capabilities instead of silently weakening the goal.
2. **Define the shape.** State the relevant data model, state owner, invariants, lifecycle and interfaces before specifying implementation steps. Include failure behavior and, where material, concurrent access, retries, persistence, cleanup and integration boundaries. Use the language/framework's actual contracts. Resolve only what the task needs; a pure function need not acquire an artificial state machine.
3. **Choose with evidence.** Compare alternatives when there is a real tradeoff; do not invent several designs for an obvious local pattern. Explain the selected structure's fit, costs, and material rejected option. When an empirical uncertainty could overturn the choice, inspect evidence or run the smallest scoped prototype before dependent work. Mark an inaccessible required check blocked and an untested assumption uncertain; a plausible sketch is not runtime proof.
4. **Make it implementable and checkable.** Identify affected interfaces/callers, migration order and compatibility obligations when relevant. Link each load-bearing invariant or assumption to a concrete check, expected observation, and required environment. Set measurable budgets for performance/resource claims when those claims matter. Before returning a design or advancing dependent implementation, read and apply [design review](references/review.md): trace requirements to mechanisms and evidence, challenge relevant failure sequences, and repair contradictions. The review owns these detailed checks; the caller's software verification still applies to implementation.

## Return the decision

Apply the [shared reader-facing design guidance](../../agents/references/design-communication.md) when composing and handing back the result. Explain observable behavior and important failure cases before introducing types, storage fields or transaction details. Then connect the chosen shape/ownership, interfaces, implementation or migration units, evidence and checks to the decisions they support.

State which guarantees the design supports, which implementation checks remain, and which unresolved premises block named dependent work. A useful partial proposal is not a satisfied guarantee. The caller applies [code's verification procedure](../vegabond-code/references/verification.md) to the actual implementation and returns here only when evidence changes a design decision. Preserve valid context rather than redesigning everything after each failure.

===== skills/vegabond-create/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-create
description: Use when producing a new artifact or capability from a request or supplied material, across domains such as fiction, presentations, planning, software, and other outputs.
---

# Vegabond Create

Produce the requested new result. Reuse the brief and task profile supplied by scout. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) without rerouting, and follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries, verification, and acceptance.

## Refine the creation task

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to connect consequential authoring choices and preservation conditions to evidence. Have the specialist refine missing questions from actual inputs; reuse valid answers and verify their application in the delivered result.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for needed references, conventions, and acceptance checks, reusing scout's information brief. Pass new source-backed methods or confirmed corrections back for storage within the task's write scope.

Use the shared profile to separate supplied material from what must be authored. Confirm the requested degree of completion: a draft, outline, editable artifact, executable capability, or another result. An outline is not a delivered presentation or video.

Translate the chosen method into the production steps below, using the relevant specialist's create, verification, and correction methods from [scout's shared mapping](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#connect-steps-to-skills-and-checks). Refine only missing creation details; preserve the established scope and scale unless new evidence requires an update.

## Produce

1. Choose the organizing shape the result needs, reusing applicable conventions, references, and supplied facts.
2. If an untested direction could cause substantial rework, make a representative outline, sample, or prototype and check the load-bearing assumption before extending it. Otherwise produce directly.
3. Develop that shape into the requested deliverable. Resolve unfinished sections or placeholders, check consistency across the new parts, and distinguish authored content from factual claims needing support.

Hand back the actual deliverable, key choices that affect its use, acceptance evidence, and unresolved issues. A successful sample does not establish that the completed result passes its required checks.

===== skills/vegabond-fiction/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-fiction
description: Use when drafting, revising, critiquing, or investigating fictional manuscripts, coordinating narrative design when needed and checking story context, voice, and continuity.
---

# Vegabond Fiction

Write and revise fiction within the active operation. Reuse its brief and task profile; follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for common boundaries and completion. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and its linked operation without restarting classification.

## Gather the relevant story context

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to derive questions from the actual scene and edit, including relevant causal, knowledge and disclosure dependencies beyond its genre label. Keep canon, author preference, character belief and provisional invention distinct when adopting retrieved material.

Before drafting or revising a scene, request focused context through [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md), reusing the current information brief. Identify the work and revision, chapter/scene, story time, viewpoint, involved characters and aliases, location, and intended events where known. Missing values are gaps, not invitations to invent facts.

Use the work's existing setting documents, character/relationship notes, timeline, relevant manuscript passages, and author decisions as sources. Follow the knowledge skill's scoped index-to-detail retrieval; search by work and entity names/aliases, then scene/time and relevant questions. Expand to connected characters or events only when needed for a dependency or conflict. Include supporting characters whose actions matter; narrative prominence alone does not determine relevance. Reuse valid context rather than rereading all setting files for every scene.

Return the scene-relevant facts with source locations, applicability, and gaps in the existing information brief:

| Context | Retrieve and check when relevant |
| --- | --- |
| World and place | Rules, exceptions, capabilities and costs, geography, travel, and scene constraints. |
| Characters | Identity, names/aliases, roles, goals, traits, voice, abilities, and current physical/emotional state. |
| Relationships | Who feels, believes, owes, or knows what about whom; direction, changes, and when they take effect. Do not assume mutual feelings or symmetric knowledge. |
| Timeline and disclosure | Event order, location and state at this scene, character knowledge and how it was acquired, reader-visible information, and planned reveals. |
| Voice references and corrections | Author-written or author-selected passages, explicit style choices, and relevant verified before/after corrections for this work, narrator, or character; retain source locations and exceptions. |

Use those references to keep a short voice baseline in the existing brief: what the narrator notices or omits, degree of emotional explanation, dialogue/action/description balance, diction and imagery, rhythm, and room for reader inference. Distinguish observed patterns from author-confirmed preferences. If no baseline exists, derive a provisional direction from the request and a short sample within the current work; refine it through available author feedback without adding a routine approval gate. A baseline guides choices, not identical sentence patterns in every scene.

Verify consequential summaries against their original passages. Follow explicit author decisions and declared source authority; do not resolve a disagreement merely by choosing the newest file. Distinguish established canon, provisional plans/new inventions, in-world beliefs or rumors, and unknowns. A future event or relationship must not be applied to an earlier scene, and author knowledge must not silently become character or reader knowledge. Mark conflicting sources with the affected fact and scene; investigate, or clarify material unresolved intent before dependent writing while continuing independent work.

Reuse an existing story bible rather than creating a competing copy. When authorized knowledge storage is useful, use the existing fiction domain and storage format: put the work boundary in `scope`, names/aliases in `keywords`, and relevant revision/time conditions in `context` or the body. Link original sources instead of copying entire profiles; an `active` knowledge entry is not by itself author-confirmed canon. Do not fabricate setting entries when no story material is supplied.

## Choose the design depth

Use [vegabond-fiction-design](../vegabond-fiction-design/SKILL.md) when a new work/arc, linked scenes, character motivation or change, viewpoint/disclosure, setups/payoffs, or a structural revision needs consequential narrative choices. Pass the existing brief, relevant canon/manuscript and voice baseline, constraints, and open questions. Reuse a valid design; revisit only choices affected by evidence. A clear local scene or line edit can state its purpose and preservation conditions inline without a separate design pass. Exploratory drafting can inform a provisional design; do not require a finished plot or a fixed story formula before every passage.

Design returns choices and intended effects within this workflow, not a second execution loop. A design-only request ends with that artifact rather than an unsolicited manuscript. Retrieve existing context through the section above; design must not create a competing story bible.

## Draft or revise

Establish genre, audience, realism, viewpoint, tense, narrator reliability and intended uncertainties from the request and relevant sources. Apply the work's baseline and scoped author choices, distinguishing established facts from proposed invention. Read [manuscript review and correction](references/review.md) before judging or revising prose, and apply it to the actual final text before handoff.

- **Create:** Use the design or inline scene purpose, character intent, pressure and change where relevant. Draft action, dialogue and exposition in the requested voice and viewpoint; respect deliberate ambiguity and form. Return discoveries that materially alter the intended sequence or effect to fiction-design without silently rewriting canon.
- **Revise:** Diagnose at the structural, scene, continuity or sentence level. Repair the earliest established causal problem within the requested scope, preserving facts and effective passages. If it changes linked narrative choices, update that part of the design before dependent rewriting. Use the reference's finding classification and correction loop; more explanation is not automatically improvement.
- **Research or critique only:** Ground findings in passages and distinguish textual evidence, interpretation and proposed invention. Verify the analysis without rewriting the source or promoting an interpretation to canon.

## Hand back

Return the requested text or analysis, important choices, review/correction evidence and unresolved conditions to the active operation. The review reference owns the detailed checks, uncertain judgments, style and recurrence evidence, and final rereading. Send useful findings through knowledge within the existing write scope; do not create a separate style store or new record schema. Distinguish AI review from vagabond's independent acceptance under the common instructions.

===== skills/vegabond-fiction-design/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-fiction-design
description: Use when designing a fictional work, arc, linked scenes, characters, narrative structure, viewpoint or disclosure, or when structural revision needs narrative choices before drafting.
---

# Vegabond Fiction Design

Choose narrative structure and intended effects for the calling operation or [vegabond-fiction](../vegabond-fiction/SKILL.md). Reuse its brief, relevant sources and voice baseline. Follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md); design neither starts another workflow nor establishes reader consensus. If invoked directly, fill missing context through [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and [fiction's context retrieval](../vegabond-fiction/SKILL.md#gather-the-relevant-story-context).

Keep canon, author decisions, provisional inventions, character beliefs and unknowns distinct. Existing sources and [knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) own context/storage; link them rather than duplicating profiles or a story bible. A design-only request produces a design, not unsolicited manuscript revisions. A requested exploratory sample may test a direction, but its inventions and observed effects do not become canon or audience evidence automatically.

## Shape the narrative

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to consequential narrative choices, their supporting passages and affected reveals. Recheck omitted conditions without forcing whole-work context onto a local design or promoting a craft guideline to canon.

1. Ground the scope (work, arc or scene), intended audience/genre/effect and preservation conditions in the existing brief. Identify the choice to settle and any material missing context; clarify unresolved intent only when it changes the work. Reuse valid design decisions and context.
2. Consider the twenty elements below at whole-work scope. For an arc/scene/local revision, select those that affect its function and actual dependencies; identify a material deferred element or unavailable context without inventing an answer. Treat them as connected questions, not twenty required devices, scores or separate skills. A static character, quiet scene, coincidence, ambiguity or open ending can be intentional; justify it against the work rather than forcing change, conflict or closure everywhere.
3. Connect relevant choices: purpose -> character desire/information/pressure -> choice/event -> consequence or change -> information available to viewpoint character and reader. Arrange scenes, revelations and setups/payoffs accordingly; identify meaningful tradeoffs such as clarity versus suspense or pace versus emotional preparation. Compare alternatives only when they affect the decision; respect discovery writing instead of imposing a fixed plot formula.
4. Before returning a design or advancing dependent writing, read and apply [evidence-based design review](references/review.md). It owns the trace from consequential choices to setting/manuscript passages, causal and disclosure challenges, element tradeoffs, and affected revisions. The twenty element names are prompts, not proof that the design works. Use the review to repair supported contradictions and expose material gaps without inventing evidence, changing author constraints, or resolving an intended mystery prematurely.

## Twenty design elements

| Element | Meaning | Design question |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Plausibility | How believable events or actions are under the given circumstances. | Can the reader understand why this could happen here? |
| Verisimilitude | How real the fictional world and people feel. | Do the people and their actions feel lived-in within this world? |
| Causality | Connections between causes and consequences. | How do prior events and choices produce what follows? |
| Consistency | Continuity of established setting, character and rules. | Are changes supported rather than silently contradicting what was established? |
| Character motivation | Reasons and desires behind action. | Why does this person want this and choose this action now? |
| Character depth | Multiple facets and internal tensions beyond a functional role. | What makes this person more than a plot function? |
| Character change | Effects of experience and choices on a person. | What changes, or meaningfully resists change, in thought, attitude or relationships? |
| Conflict | Clashing aims, pressures and obstacles to a goal. | What obstructs the character and what choices does it require? |
| Tension | Interest, concern or uncertainty about the outcome. | What is at stake in success or failure, or keeps the reader awaiting an answer? |
| Composition | Arrangement of events and information. | In what order should the story be experienced for its intended effect? |
| Setup and payoff | Earlier cues acquiring later consequence or meaning. | Have important resolutions or turns been prepared appropriately? |
| Pacing and modulation | Speed, compression, expansion and alternation of pressure/rest. | Where does the story linger, rush, tighten or release, and why? |
| Viewpoint control | Whose perception governs narration and its information limits. | Is each disclosure compatible with viewpoint and narrator reliability? |
| Information delivery | Timing and amount of information needed for understanding. | Can readers follow what they need now while intended questions remain open? |
| Emotional credibility | Support for feelings and emotional transitions. | What in this person's experience and situation supports this feeling now? |
| Theme | Questions or perspectives the work explores. | What question emerges through events and choices rather than an imposed explanation? |
| Style and readability | Distinctive expression and intelligibility in the intended voice. | How will diction, rhythm and narrative distance serve the chosen effect? |
| Originality | A work's own interpretation of even familiar material. | What is distinctive in its people, perspective or combination without forced novelty? |
| Ending satisfaction | How the ending answers or deliberately reframes accumulated expectations. | Does the ending fit what the story has built, including intentional openness? |
| Resonance | Feelings or questions that remain after reading. | What should remain with the reader, and what choices could support it? |

## Return the design

Apply the [shared reader-facing design guidance](../../agents/references/design-communication.md) when composing and handing back the result. First explain what happens, why the character chooses it, and what the reader knows or awaits; then connect source/scene anchors, element reasoning, tradeoffs and manuscript checks to those choices. The reader should not need to study all twenty element analyses to understand the scene. Make provisional additions explicit and propagate changed decisions only through affected scenes, characters, timelines and disclosures.

Separate source-confirmed continuity constraints, reasoned editorial choices and intended reader effects. Design can support an effect without proving that an actual reader experiences it; proposed satisfaction, originality or resonance is not a measured outcome. Name what the writer must inspect in the manuscript and any audience evidence a required claim still lacks. A missing consequential canon fact blocks only writing dependent on it; independent design can continue. The caller drafts/revises and applies [manuscript review](../vegabond-fiction/references/review.md) to the actual text, returning here only for changed narrative choices. Do not overwrite established canon or weaken author constraints to make a design look finished.

===== skills/vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-knowledge
description: Use when a task needs stored domain references, project conventions, prior corrections, or verification guidance, or when saving and maintaining knowledge and problem records.
---

# Vegabond Knowledge

Retrieve and maintain knowledge and problem records for the active task. Reuse scout's profile; the calling operation or specialist judges applicability and verifies the actual deliverable. Follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries and completion. This skill supplies information; it does not take over creation, revision, or research, and reading it does not spawn an agent.

## Request and return

A request states the **working project, question, relevant domains, target and constraints, and environment/version or story context** where relevant. Reuse the existing brief instead of asking for those details again. A storage request additionally supplies findings, evidence, resolution, proposed reusable checks, intended reuse conditions, and the context actually verified.

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md): connect questions to the decisions they support and expose missing premises found in retrieval. Return candidate evidence and limits; the consuming specialist records consequential adoption, conditional use, rejection or deferral and verifies the action. Search coverage is not a completeness judgment.

Return a short information brief containing:

- **Applicable knowledge:** what can help this step, with source IDs and paths or links for each material claim.
- **Conditions and exceptions:** where it applies, why a prior incident matches, its cause confidence and verification context, and what conflicts with the present request or sources.
- **Checks:** concrete prevention or acceptance checks to carry into the work, with the originating record ID, prevention state and evidence. A resolved incident does not by itself establish verified prevention.
- **Gaps:** missing, stale, conflicting, or unconfirmed information and the question it leaves open.

## Find and synthesize

1. Reuse an existing information brief while its question, scope, consulted entry content, and governing sources remain valid. Search again when one changes or new evidence exposes a gap. Keep consulted IDs and revisions/hashes in task evidence when needed, without creating a global cache of project facts.
2. Use the working project's known stores first. The default routes are [knowledge](../../knowledge/index.md) and [records](../../records/index.md) in this repository; in another project, resolve paths there rather than automatically using OneStep's project facts. Use an existing native store when suitable. Shared OneStep material is an additional source only when explicitly configured or requested. Do not search unrelated projects.
3. Choose domains from the profile; combine them for mixed work. Use the [retrieval helper and synthesis procedure](references/retrieval.md) to search both stores by question, identifiers, scope/context and state, with compact match reasons and body locators. It expands maintained Korean/English aliases and excludes `*.kr.md`. Use `--no-cache` for strictly read-only work. If the helper is unavailable or a native store fits better, use bounded canonical catalog/body searches with equivalent conditions and report the capability gap. Prioritize applicability over recency; consult `shared` only for a relevant cross-domain need.
   Before work, search problem records by the planned action and conditions against `trigger`, even when no symptom is known. After a failure, search `symptom` and exact identifiers, then compare conditions and cause evidence. Keep resolved incidents in the candidate set. Exclude known incompatible scopes; retain unknown compatibility as a caveat instead of silently treating it as incompatible. Matching symptoms do not establish a matching cause. An absent trigger is a lookup gap, not evidence that an incident is irrelevant. Lexical rank does not establish applicability or evidence quality.

4. Start with up to three relevant detail files across the selected stores, reading the header and useful sections first. Check applicability and follow original evidence where needed; a summary is not proof. Separate source facts, confirmed corrections, author/project choices, editorial judgments, and hypotheses. Open records identify risks; legacy catalog text and unrecorded cause/prevention dimensions are unverified. Proposed checks may inform verification but must not be presented as demonstrated safeguards; invalidated checks need renewed investigation before reliance. A retired or incompatible-version item cannot silently govern the task. If a catalog is missing or demonstrably stale, use targeted metadata lookup in the selected domain and report the gap; rebuild only within authorized write scope.
5. Reconcile sources into the brief above, connecting each material answer to IDs, original evidence, conditions and checks. Follow relevant incoming/outgoing relationships and prose links; preserve disagreement and inspect truncated conflict/replacement candidates before relying on a conclusion. For broad requests, close each subquestion as answered, conditional, conflicting or missing. Expand for unanswered questions, dependencies, conflicts or failures; the initial detail count is not a completion limit. If no useful match appears, try alternate terms or the nearest scope once, then report the gap. Never infer absence from failed search. Send new investigation needs back to the active operation's research step.

Keep a recommendation's scope and caveats attached when summarizing. Stored knowledge cannot override current user intent, canon, contracts, or direct evidence. The caller remains responsible for applying and testing it.

For a registered `SRC-...` source needed by a claim, follow the [portable source procedure](references/storage.md#portable-source-references). Return each requested ID, relevant locator, separate access/identity state and remaining gap; reconcile requested and returned IDs. Search marks originals unverified until explicitly checked. Resolve only needed IDs within the authorized source scope, without changing machine settings or historical evidence during lookup.

## Close a knowledge gap

An empty store or insufficient match is a normal starting point. Turn the gap into the smallest questions needed for the current decision, with its conditions and required evidence; do not collect a whole subject in advance. Return these questions to the active operation's [research step](../vegabond-research/SKILL.md). Reuse the current brief so this handoff does not restart scout or recursively repeat the same lookup. Model recollection can suggest search terms, but is not checked source evidence.

Bring the answer back into the information brief, distinguishing **source-supported claims**, **methods checked in the actual task**, and **inferences or unresolved questions**. Source-based reference knowledge need not wait for an implementation, but a source check does not prove local execution. The calling specialist applies the findings and checks the actual result; return its evidence and exceptions here. Required evidence gaps still block dependent work under the common rules.

Before authorized storage, select what is worth finding again: a recurring decision, costly investigation, meaningful constraint, or useful check, including successful methods with no incident. Prefer one independently answerable question per entry, with just enough concept explanation to use it; link prerequisites only when needed. Apply the storage guide's consolidation rules rather than saving every new term or duplicating a research report. Keep unsupported candidates in the task findings unless a scoped hypothesis record needs follow-up. In read-only work, return proposed updates. Reuse existing fields and body sections, with source checks and task checks identified separately in Evidence.

On the next relevant task, retrieve by question and conditions, confirm applicability, and carry guidance into a concrete action or check. Record consulted IDs/hashes, decisions, gaps and misleading matches in existing task evidence. Use demonstrated misses or bad matches to improve affected summaries, aliases, scope or routing and extend the [retrieval evaluation cases](tests/retrieval_cases.json). Do not add a global usage ledger or reread the full store after each task. Catalog validation does not establish retrieval usefulness.

## Save and maintain

For game-development tasks, apply the [game development term review](references/storage.md#game-development-terms) before deciding there is nothing to save. A project-specific outcome can still teach a public engine API, C++ construct or established technique. Include each relevant term and its source-backed meaning in the authorized reusable store, even on the first encounter.

Read [storage and lifecycle](references/storage.md) when writing or reorganizing entries. Apply its [capture and consolidation decision](references/storage.md#capture-consolidate-and-promote) before creating a new entry: decide what merits reuse, what belongs in an existing entry, and what should remain in source material or task history. It defines the entry header/body and the [catalog helper](scripts/catalog.py) build/check workflow and is the single authority for format, evidence, deduplication, promotion, and retirement. Do not run full-store rebuilds or checks as part of ordinary lookup.

Write only within the task's authorized scope. In read-only or findings-only work, return proposed entries rather than changing stores. When saving is authorized, update the detail and its human translation, build affected catalogs, regenerate translations for changed English catalogs, and check them before handoff. Update root routing for a new domain. Do not edit generated rows or infer missing legacy metadata. Create no empty store merely for lookup, and never fabricate starter knowledge.

===== skills/vegabond-planning/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-planning
description: Use when drafting, revising, or evaluating planning and proposal documents for a product, project, initiative, or operation. Task scheduling alone does not require this skill.
---

# Vegabond Planning

Provide planning-document methods for the active operation. Reuse its brief and task profile; follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for common boundaries and completion. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and its linked operation skill, then continue here without restarting classification.

For game planning, apply the [game evidence reuse convention](../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-RESEARCH-USE.md), including on direct invocation. Retrieve by the intended experience and concrete design uncertainty; connect genre methods and technical feasibility only where the decision needs them. Carry selected IDs, conditions, adoption decisions and prototype/playtest checks into the existing plan.

## Execute

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to derive questions from the decision, beneficiaries, assumptions and shared dependencies. Refine them after inspecting sources; distinguish evidence needed to commit now from later outcome measurement and optional refinement.

Ground the decision in who experiences the problem, when it occurs, its significance, and the available evidence. Distinguish beneficiary needs, stakeholder preferences, and a requested solution. Reuse the brief's goals, scope, constraints, and information from [knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md); choose sections that support the decision.

- **Create:** Connect the problem to intended outcomes and a feasible approach. For a consequential choice, compare relevant alternatives, including the status quo or minimal change when viable, under common criteria. Link critical assumptions to evidence, a proportionate validation method, and the decision affected by failure.
- **Revise:** Repair the premise, choice, dependency, measure, or commitment preventing a useful decision; propagate its consequences while preserving agreed scope.
- **Research:** Compare options or audit a plan against stated criteria. Separate facts, assumptions, and recommendations; cite evidence and return findings without unauthorized edits.

For linked subplans sharing goals, resources, dependencies, or changing decisions, apply [large-plan coordination](references/large-plans.md) before decomposition or cross-plan changes. It covers authoritative decisions, integration, and change propagation within scout's scale and stage choices; document count alone does not require stages or delegation.

Before relying on a consequential choice or handing back the result, apply relevant [planning review](references/review.md) checks. Small, well-supported changes need only affected checks in the existing brief or document.

## Verify and return

Read the final plan against its evidence and preservation conditions. Recalculate material estimates and check that scope, capacity, responsibilities, dependencies, and timing agree. Mark estimated costs, suggested owners, targets, and commitments as proposals when unconfirmed. Distinguish document consistency from demonstrated feasibility and measured outcomes; keep unresolved premises attached to dependent decisions.

Lead with the recommendation, reason, costs and tradeoffs, important conditions, and next decision or action. Keep supporting evidence and validation details available below it. For a formatted document or deck, use the corresponding artifact skill and inspect the final layout; a presentation also needs presentation-specific checks.

## Correct

Repair the most consequential unsupported or infeasible premise first. Update affected choices, scope, estimates, sequence, risks, and measures together, then repeat invalidated checks. Preserve valid decisions and expose unresolved dependencies.

Return confirmed problems, corrections, recheck evidence, and reusable decision checks to knowledge for authorized storage.

===== skills/vegabond-presentation/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-presentation
description: Use when creating or revising slide presentations, developing a deck's argument and visual structure, or reviewing its content and rendered slides.
---

# Vegabond Presentation

Provide presentation methods for the active operation. Reuse its brief and task profile; follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for common boundaries and completion. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) and its linked operation skill, then continue here without restarting classification.

## Execute

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to audience decisions, source claims, comparable populations/units and actual output requirements. Expose missing evidence before turning a retrieved assertion into a slide or treating a content-only request as full artifact production.

Establish what the audience should understand or decide and how the presentation will be used. Inspect supplied sources, existing slides, template, and relevant speaker notes. Distinguish a requested outline or script from a finished editable deck. For artifact production or editing, load an available presentation skill suited to the requested format and follow its authoring and export procedure.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for relevant audience, design/format conventions, source guidance, and prior content or rendering defects, reusing the active information brief.

- **Create:** Build a narrative from the audience's starting point to the intended conclusion. Assign each slide a purpose and supporting evidence, then choose text, diagrams, tables, or charts that communicate it. Build the requested artifact with consistent hierarchy and usable native elements where editing is required.
- **Revise:** Inspect the existing argument, order, and actual slide content before editing. Determine whether the problem lies in the evidence, story flow, wording, or visual arrangement. Preserve required theme, content, notes, and editability while repairing the affected layer.
- **Research:** Evaluate claims, audience needs, or presentation options using the relevant sources and slides. Cite findings and recommendations; leave the investigated deck unchanged.

## Verify

Check the narrative against the intended audience and conclusion. Verify material claims, citations, chart values, units, labels, and dates against sources; distinguish illustrative data from actual results.

Open or render the final delivered deck and inspect every slide for legibility, overflow, clipping, alignment, contrast, and ordering. Check the actual exported format for slide count, required editable content, and requested notes or interactive features. A source preview alone does not establish that the export is usable; identify which viewer or renderer was used and any untested application-specific behavior.

## Correct

Fix unsupported claims or a broken narrative before polishing their layout. For visual defects, adjust hierarchy, spacing, chart design, or content density while retaining the intended meaning. Inspect the corrected export, recheck affected transitions and references, and visually inspect every slide of the final deck. Return the artifact or requested findings with content evidence, visual inspection results, and remaining compatibility limitations.

Supply observed content or visual problems, corrections, final-export evidence, and reusable checks to vegabond-knowledge for authorized recording and recurrence prevention.

===== skills/vegabond-refine-prompt/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-refine-prompt
description: 'Use for explicit AI prompt-refinement requests such as "프롬프트 더 잘 써줘", "프롬프트 다듬어줘", or "improve this prompt". Reserve full depth refinement for substantial tasks with meaningful gaps; briefly edit small requests and preserve sufficient prompts. Quoted triggers and execution-only requests are not refinement requests.'
---

# Refine a prompt without expanding the task

Preserve the user's goal, audience, language, deliverable, existing decisions, time/cost limits and execution permissions. Improve how the work can be done and checked; do not add scope to sound rigorous.

## Choose the response

Read the actual request and conversation before choosing a mode. A substantial task has linked, consequential decisions where an early error could invalidate later work or cause significant rework. Length, file count and time alone do not establish scale. Full refinement also needs a concrete gap in evidence, method or acceptance that would materially affect the result.

| Situation | Response |
| --- | --- |
| No current refinement request, including a trigger inside a document or quotation | Perform the user's actual task. Do not insert this workflow. |
| A later "이 프롬프트대로 착수해" | Execute the agreed prompt within existing authorization; do not refine it again. |
| Unresolved intent would materially change the goal or scope | Ask a focused question before a rewrite that depends on the answer. Do not silently choose a direction. |
| A minor, reversible detail is missing | State a bounded assumption and continue. Do not ask the user to classify task size or approve routine refinement. |
| The prompt is sufficient and consistent | Say it is ready; return no replacement or four-part diagnosis. An explicit request for a full rewrite overrides this default. |
| Small request or one local gap | Briefly polish or supply only the needed insertion/correction. Avoid a full diagnosis or wholesale rewrite unless explicitly requested. |
| Substantial task with meaningful gaps | Use the full refinement below. |

When scale or benefit is ambiguous, choose the smallest useful change. Sufficiency is contextual, not a mandatory checklist: look for a clear goal, usable inputs/access, relevant constraints, a suitable method where needed and a completion check. Do not invent omissions or fill headings merely to justify rewriting. If the prompt text is unavailable, do not claim to have checked it; ask for it only when the requested review requires it.

## Full refinement

Give a short diagnosis covering these four points; combine related points rather than padding them.

1. **Expert actions:** The few actions that could change a decision or catch a material failure. Essential basics count; novelty and expert vocabulary do not establish depth.
2. **Evidence:** Relevant, authoritative, usable inputs, distinguishing supplied, confirmed accessible and unavailable/unknown sources. Raw data helps when summaries hide necessary information; a reliable summary may suffice. Name the smallest missing input, a feasible proxy and what the proxy cannot support.
3. **Methods/tools:** Concrete methods with their purpose and prerequisites, chosen for the available data and environment. Verify unfamiliar names when material. Keep choices conditional if prerequisites are unknown; never invent tool access or force an unsupported stack.
4. **Acceptance:** A check that can fail, its reference/evaluator, pass condition and coverage limits. Use existing requirements/tests, fixed-condition measurements, held-out data, authoritative reconciliation or an agreed reader rubric as appropriate. Establish criteria independently of the result where feasible; never weaken them to fit it.

Self-review, another AI, a citation, a compiler or one generated test alone does not establish overall correctness. Human judgment is valid for subjective work when who checks what is clear. Distinguish prediction from causation and proxy metrics from the user's real goal.

Then provide one copy-ready prompt in the user's language and requested shape. Include only the applicable goal, inputs/access gaps, limits, justified or conditional method, acceptance and missing-capability handling. Do not impose English, XML or extra deliverables. State only material assumptions and briefly name excluded work when that explains scope/cost control.

## Keep refinement and execution distinct

Refine from supplied context, using at most a small relevant read-only check for a consequential source/tool fact. Do not start the underlying investigation, fetch full datasets, install tools or run benchmarks just to write its prompt. Prefer the least costly adequate method; identify material expansion as optional when no budget is supplied.

Stop after refinement by default. If the user also requests execution, show the refinement and continue in the same turn within existing authorization, without a routine approval question. Produce the actual result, not only a plan or promise. For an already sufficient prompt, do not manufacture a rewrite unless the user explicitly asks for one.

During execution, perform useful independent work even if another part is blocked. Name each unavailable input/tool, the dependent check or conclusion it blocks, and the minimum legitimate evidence/access needed. Separate performed checks and their pass/fail results from blocked/not-run checks. Missing capability is not a pass or "not applicable"; a verification plan is not execution. Withhold only unsupported conclusions, and never fabricate measurements, data, calls or restored access.

## Maintenance and basis

For skill maintenance only, follow [tests/evaluation.md](tests/evaluation.md). Runtime instruction or invocation-policy changes require fresh execution of all listed cases before installation. Do not load the suite during ordinary refinement.

Adapted from Felix Rieseberg's [depth prompt](https://felixrieseberg.com/how-to-be-more-ambitious-a-tutorial-for-humans-with-models/) and [deepen-prompt](https://github.com/r3dlex/deepen-prompt). This is a heuristic, not a proven universal improvement. Judge recurring use by final-result errors, rework, time and cost under comparable model/input/tool/budget conditions.

===== skills/vegabond-research/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-research
description: Use when investigating a question, comparing options, or supplying evidence for a defined step of another task, using relevant sources in any domain.
---

# Vegabond Research

Deliver an evidence-based answer within the requested scope. Reuse the brief and task profile supplied by scout. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) without rerouting, and follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries, verification, and acceptance.

## Refine the inquiry

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to test whether the questions cover the decision and relevant counterevidence. Return source applicability and gaps without treating agreeing summaries or successful searches as independent proof or whole-task coverage.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for existing sources, claims, and known gaps, reusing scout's information brief. Verify them against the question and current evidence. In read-only work, return proposed knowledge updates with the findings rather than changing stores.

Use the shared profile to refine the question or decision and its comparison criteria. Apply the relevant specialist's research method and claim-specific checks through [scout's shared mapping](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#connect-steps-to-skills-and-checks). Correct unsupported reasoning or the answer, not the investigated sources. Sources may be supplied material or external evidence; web search is not always required. Refine only missing inquiry details rather than repeating the shared assessment.

For supporting research, answer the calling step's question instead of taking ownership of the whole creation or revision task. Leave investigated sources unchanged; producing a requested answer or report does not authorize implementation changes.

When knowledge retrieval supplied an unanswered question, use that gap and its conditions directly; do not send it through the same lookup again. Investigate only the concepts and prerequisites needed for the decision, expanding when evidence reveals a material dependency.

## Investigate

1. Turn the question into answerable subquestions or comparison criteria. Bound the investigation to what the requested answer or decision needs.
2. Examine primary sources where available, checking dates, versions, and applicability. Link material conclusions to evidence, address contradictions, and distinguish supported facts from inference. Unavailable evidence remains a limitation.
3. Synthesize the answer or recommendation against the criteria. Stop when questions are supported or remaining evidence gaps are explicit; pursue further searches only when they could materially change the conclusion. Do not present unresolved acceptance conditions as verified.

Hand back the answer or requested report, supporting source references, reasoning, and material uncertainty. Make clear which conclusions are established and which still depend on assumptions or missing evidence.

For a knowledge-gap handoff, identify what the sources establish, what the caller must check in its environment or artifact, and any reusable candidate with its conditions. Return these to the calling step and [knowledge's gap-closing flow](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md#close-a-knowledge-gap); the specialist verifies application and knowledge handles selective storage within the existing write scope. Research alone does not certify execution or require a new stored entry.

===== skills/vegabond-revise/SKILL.md =====

---
name: vegabond-revise
description: Use when improving or correcting an existing artifact or behavior, including prose edits, presentation or planning revisions, software defects, and other targeted changes.
---

# Vegabond Revise
