Improve the existing result while preserving the intended constraints. Reuse the brief and task profile supplied by scout. If invoked directly, use [scout's shared setup](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify) without rerouting, and follow the [agent instructions](../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) for document boundaries, verification, and acceptance.

## Refine the revision task

Apply the shared [information-needs judgment](../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) to the actual change, preserved contract and affected dependents. Recheck whether initial questions missed a material condition; compare a prior incident's mechanism before transferring its correction.

Use [vegabond-knowledge](../vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) for relevant prior failures, preserved contracts, and correction checks, reusing scout's information brief. Return confirmed findings and before/after evidence for authorized record updates.

Use the shared profile and original context to distinguish defect repair from intentional improvement and identify affected dependencies. Apply the relevant specialist's revise, verification, and correction methods within the steps below, using [scout's shared mapping](../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#connect-steps-to-skills-and-checks). Refine only missing revision details rather than repeating the shared assessment.

Distinguish analysis-only feedback from an authorized edit. If only findings are requested, return them without rewriting the source. Keep the established scope and scale; surface new dependencies when discovered.

## Revise

1. Establish the current-to-target gap and a meaningful comparison. Preserve access to the original state through existing history or a non-destructive comparison method where needed.
2. For a defect, reproduce it and trace its cause before repair. For editorial improvement, tie edits to the requested criteria. Make the smallest coherent change; keep unrelated polish outside scope.
3. Compare before and after for the intended improvement and preserved constraints. Inspect affected dependents or surrounding content, and explain any necessary departure from what was intended to remain intact.

Hand back the revised artifact or authorized findings, the meaningful changes, comparison evidence, and unresolved issues. A changed result is not by itself evidence of improvement.

===== skills/vegabond-code/references/verification.md =====

# Software verification and handoff

Use this procedure from [vegabond-code](../SKILL.md) before changing code or judging a software result. The [agent instructions](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) own common completion, acceptance, and failure routing; this reference owns software-specific checks.

## Prepare checks

Before implementation, map the acceptance conditions and preserved contracts to executable checks, including relevant safeguards from the information brief. Establish the baseline for the affected path and distinguish existing failures. For a bug with a practical local test path, capture a regression check failing for the intended reason before the fix. Otherwise retain the closest observable reproduction and state its limits.

Use the project's existing verification commands and harness first. Confirm that the command actually discovers the intended tests and reaches the changed behavior; an exit-zero run with no relevant assertions is not proof. Retain the command, target/configuration, code revision or relevant diff, and observed results in existing task evidence. When a missing repeatable path would otherwise leave required behavior unchecked, add the smallest reusable check within scope and demonstrate it end to end.

## Verify

Retain these four stages where applicable:

1. **Build/preparation:** Run the project's actual build, compile, or equivalent preparation for the affected target and configuration.
2. **Automated tests:** Run relevant existing tests. Add regression or behavior coverage when it provides meaningful protection, using expected results independent of the implementation. Include affected boundary and error paths.
3. **Runtime readiness:** Launch the application or required environment and confirm the changed path can be reached without initialization failures.
4. **Real use:** Exercise the changed behavior through its public interface. For Unreal or Unity games, launch the editor and perform actual play, including relevant multiplayer roles. For a CLI, invoke real commands; for a library, exercise a real consumer or harness.

For a defect, rerun the original reproduction as well as affected surrounding behavior. Record commands, target/configuration, outcomes, and observed runtime behavior. Apply the agent instructions' blocked-versus-inapplicable distinction to each stage. A code investigation without an implementation change uses the checks needed to substantiate its claims rather than requiring an unrelated full build.

## Review and repeat

After each coherent change, run the affected build/preparation and automated checks, then inspect the diff and relevant callers against the requirements. Look for omitted behavior, broken contracts, state/lifecycle errors, and risks specific to the change. Review whether the tests would detect incorrect behavior rather than merely repeat the implementation. Use available review or test skills when they add relevant capability; a review finding needs a concrete mechanism or evidence before it motivates a change.

Review changes to tests and verification configuration as part of the patch: deleted assertions, skips, exclusions, relaxed expected values, lint/type suppressions, and disabled checks need a contract-based reason. Do not use them to hide a valid failure. If a check is wrong, identify the conflicting requirement or evidence, explain the correction, and verify the preserved contract. Keep blocked or skipped required checks visible in the handoff rather than counting them as passes. For a practical regression or consequential safeguard, demonstrate that the check detects the original failure or a controlled broken case; keep expected results independent of the implementation.

Inspect exception swallowing, success-shaped defaults, unconditional retries, arbitrary waits, and compatibility paths for evidence that they handle legitimate states. A fallback is valid only when the contract calls for it and its failure behavior is checked; for example, a permitted missing configuration must not silently turn malformed configuration into success. Unsupported workarounds are hypotheses to investigate, not completed fixes. Remove speculative changes that the evidence rules out without discarding unrelated work.

For a confirmed failure or defect, use the command, assertion, log, or interaction to localize its cause. Correct it, repeat the original failure check and affected surrounding checks, and review the repaired diff. Repeat until required checks pass and no confirmed in-scope defect remains. Do not weaken a valid check to accept incorrect behavior; correct a mistaken check against the requirement with an explicit reason.

Use the agent instructions' [shared failure and feedback rules](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md#respond-to-failures-and-feedback) when diagnosis, evidence, scope, or capability needs reassessment.

After the local loop, run the required integrated and real-use checks on the final artifact. A failure returns to diagnosis and repair; later edits invalidate affected evidence. Separate reproducible pre-existing failures from introduced ones, and do not treat a pre-existing failure as proof of acceptance.

## Continue long or staged work

At phase boundaries and before a handoff or context reset, update the existing brief with the goal and preservation conditions, completed and pending acceptance items, current code state, evidence pointers, decisions that constrain the next step, and next action. Record decisions and checks, not private reasoning or every tool call. On resumption, reconcile that state with the current diff and valid evidence before continuing. Recheck evidence invalidated by intervening changes; do not silently drop pending conditions or rerun all completed work by default.

## Retain useful safeguards

For repeated consequential mistakes, use the existing knowledge/record flow to identify the pattern, then prefer a scoped structural fix, type constraint, lint/CI check, or behavior test over another prose warning. Reuse the project's enforcement path and demonstrate detection of the actual mistake or a controlled equivalent. Report what is automatically enforced versus merely documented, and any remaining limits. Do not claim a rule is enforced just because it appears in this skill, or impose new infrastructure on every small task.

===== skills/vegabond-code-design/references/review.md =====

# Design review

Apply this before handing off a software design or advancing implementation that relies on it. Use the existing brief or design artifact; no separate checklist file is required. The [design skill](../SKILL.md) owns choices, the [common instructions](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) own completion and failure routing, and [software verification](../../vegabond-code/references/verification.md) owns execution checks on implemented code.

## Preserve the contract and evidence boundary

Trace each material requirement and preservation condition to its mechanism, evidence or explicit assumption, and acceptance check. Use a compact mapping when prose would hide a gap. Derive the expected behavior from the request and governing contracts, not from what the chosen architecture happens to support. A successful example cannot replace the required failure or concurrency behavior.

Separate observed source/API contracts, reasoned design conclusions, proposed choices, and untested assumptions. Attach source/version and prototype conditions to claims that need them. A framework name, type, lock, queue or transaction is not evidence that a guarantee holds across every boundary. An isolated model/prototype supports only what it represents; record omitted actors, failures and environment limits. Proposed budgets are not measured performance.

Do not turn a required guarantee into a preference, remove a caller, failure case or compatibility obligation, or add an unstated environmental restriction to make the design appear complete. When constraints conflict or a capability is missing, name the incompatible requirement and the evidence, show a bounded alternative with its changed guarantee, and leave the original condition unresolved. Clarify material product choices under the common rules; independent work may continue without pretending the blocked guarantee is met.

## Challenge the mechanism

For each relevant boundary, identify who owns authoritative state, valid/invalid states, permitted transitions, and the point at which ownership, durability, or acknowledgement changes. Include error propagation and resource release. Describe the mechanism that enforces an invariant rather than repeating the invariant as a promise.

Attempt a concrete counterexample at the important transitions. Choose cases from the design's actual risks; a local pure function does not need an invented distributed-system audit. For example:

- Concurrent or delayed actors: duplicate claim, stale owner, cancellation or object destruction while work continues; distinguish protecting a local record from controlling an external side effect.
- Partial failure: crash before/after persistence or acknowledgement, timeout after remote acceptance, retry after unknown outcome, partial migration and mixed old/new callers.
- Progress and capacity: hung owner, lock held across external work, retry backlog, starvation, bounded memory/storage and cleanup. A safety mechanism must disclose the progress or availability it can sacrifice.

Write the shortest relevant event sequence, expected state/effect, and where the mechanism prevents a violation. If it does not, revise the design or mark the guarantee unresolved. Do not dismiss a reachable failure merely as unlikely. Review fallback success, blanket retry, fixed waits, global serialization and compatibility layers against their explicit contract and costs; do not ban a justified mechanism by style alone.

## Check the checks and handoff

For a load-bearing claim, name the trigger/initial state, observable result and failure predicate, plus the environment and evidence needed. Check that the proposed test could reject a broken design, rather than merely observe an internal flag set by that design. Where authorized and useful, use a minimal counterexample, model or prototype to resolve an empirical uncertainty; distinguish a prediction from an executed result. Do not claim tests, formal proof, performance, or compatibility that was not checked.

Separate a premise needed to choose or safely begin the design from an implementation check that can only run after code exists. An unresolved premise blocks the named dependent decision/work; it cannot be buried in a future-test list while declaring that work ready. Implementation checks can remain pending when the structural reasoning is supported and their gates are explicit. State the supported scope, pending checks, and blocked guarantees without a blanket readiness verdict that conceals a limitation.

Reconcile interfaces, state transitions and migration steps across the final artifact; fix contradictions and revisit only decisions affected by the new evidence. A revised design must identify changed guarantees/assumptions and affected implementation/checks in the existing brief, preserving valid decisions and evidence. A document review establishes design consistency within those bounds, not a working implementation or an automatically enforced rule.

===== skills/vegabond-fiction/references/review.md =====

# Manuscript review and correction

Read this from [vegabond-fiction](../SKILL.md) before judging or revising a manuscript. The [agent instructions](../../../agents/vegabond-agent.md) own common completion and acceptance; [fiction-design](../../vegabond-fiction-design/SKILL.md) owns narrative choices, and this reference owns their assessment in actual text.

## Review and verify

Review story-level problems before polishing expression; a short text can combine these in one pass. Apply the following checks to the requested scope and form:

| Area | Check |
| --- | --- |
| Causality and motivation | What desires, information, pressures, or triggers support a choice and its consequences? Do not supply missing motivation yourself or confuse an irrational character choice with a narrative logic error. |
| Verisimilitude | Do actions, reactions, emotions, and details support belief within this genre, world, and situation? Judge against established conditions rather than personal taste or everyday realism alone. |
| Continuity | Do established facts, character knowledge, chronology, location, world rules, and setups/payoffs agree under the same conditions? Check viewpoint and narrator reliability before treating conflicting statements as conflicting facts. |
| Reader understanding | Using only information revealed by this point, can the intended reader follow the necessary connections? Distinguish a deliberately unanswered question from a missing connection that prevents understanding the scene; later revelations must not be treated as already known. |
| Style | Compare viewpoint, tense, tone, narrator/character voices, and pacing with the work's baseline. Check interchangeable character speech, uniform paragraph patterns, and habitual explanatory scene endings against scene purpose. Preserve deliberate ambiguity, dialect, repetition, and unconventional expression. |
| Sentences and words | Check explanations that repeat what action or dialogue already conveys, generic reactions interchangeable across characters, and imagery or sensory detail without a scene function. Select detail through what this viewpoint character would notice now; preserve meaning and useful rhythm. |
| Orthography and form | Spelling, spacing, punctuation, naming conventions, requested length, and formatting follow the work's language and agreed conventions. Check automated suggestions against context and intentional usage. |

Read the complete final text within scope, not just edited lines, and inspect affected surrounding passages. Identify conclusions that require unavailable chapters. Explain editorial judgments with cited passages and concrete reader effects; distinguish confirmed errors, improvement suggestions, and unresolved hypotheses. Mechanical checks assist this reading but cannot establish literary quality.

Compare the final scene's factual claims and state changes with the retrieved context and governing sources, including character knowledge, relationship direction, and disclosure timing. Flag newly introduced setting facts separately so that a plausible draft does not silently redefine established canon.

After story-level repairs, use these style checks in the same review cycle and reread the final text for flow against the baseline. Treat "AI-like" as a request to diagnose concrete prose effects, not evidence of authorship or a sufficient finding by itself. Judge repetitions by their function and context rather than a universal word blacklist. Do not manufacture naturalness through typos, arbitrary fragments, slang, mechanical sentence-length variation, or forced novelty. Direct emotion and concise exposition remain valid when they serve the scene; do not convert every feeling into an action.

## Judge uncertain findings

For a disputed plausibility or interpretation finding, use a short reasoning chain: **passage -> applicable criterion -> textual evidence -> plausible alternative reading -> judgment and smallest useful change**. Consider a text-supported alternative before declaring an error. Do not invent hidden backstory or canon to excuse a gap, and do not add explanations that disclose a planned reveal prematurely.

- **Confirmed error:** a demonstrated contradiction or violated requirement after relevant context and intentional exceptions are checked. Correct it within the authorized editing scope and recheck.
- **Unresolved hypothesis:** missing context or competing supported readings prevent a firm diagnosis. Inspect the relevant evidence; otherwise state the uncertainty and avoid rewriting as though the diagnosis were established.
- **Improvement suggestion or preference:** explain the intended reader effect and the tradeoff. Apply it when supported by the requested editing direction and preservation conditions; otherwise present it as an option.

Keep confidence separate from impact: a consequential suspicion is not automatically a confirmed error. Explain uncertainty when material without invented numerical scores. Grammar and orthography checks also respect dialogue, dialect, and deliberate stylistic exceptions.

Do not claim universal or majority reader agreement without actual audience evidence. Simulated reader perspectives may expose alternative interpretations, but agreement among AI passes is not a reader survey. If audience response is essential to an acceptance condition, use available relevant reader feedback or report that evidence gap; do not add a routine reader-approval checkpoint to every task.

## Record findings

Keep a compact problem list in the task's existing report or record system. Each finding names the passage, review area, evidence or editorial rationale, finding type, proposed correction, and resolution/recheck result. For disputed findings, retain the material uncertainty, alternative reading, and impact with the rationale above. Group minor instances of the same problem where useful; do not create a permanent lesson for each typo.

Provide these findings to vegabond-knowledge, which owns storage, targeted retrieval, deduplication, and retirement within the task's write scope. Supply story-specific applicability and recurrence-prevention checks for reusable lessons, including the relevant scene and reader-information context. Keep tentative diagnoses in the current task history; an author or story preference must remain scoped to that author or story rather than becoming a universal rule or a defect merely because the author selected another approach.

For a reusable style correction, include before/after passage locations, the diagnosed effect, why the change fits the baseline, preservation/recheck evidence, and any actual author feedback. Keep AI review and author acceptance distinct. Return work-specific style conventions and confirmed reusable corrections through the existing knowledge/record lifecycle, without a separate style store or schema. On later writing requests, retrieve the applicable baseline and correction checks through the context request above; use them conditionally rather than copying wording. If the author prefers the original or changes direction, retain that decision and update the affected convention and brief instead of reapplying the rejected correction.

## Correct and repeat

For drafting or authorized revision, apply the finding classification above before rewriting. Repair the earliest established causal problem at the appropriate level while preserving the agreed intent and constraints. Prefer the smallest sufficient cue, action, dialogue, or ordering change; more exposition is not automatically a better repair. Reread affected scenes and transitions for new contradictions or disrupted rhythm, checking whether clarity improved without damaging tension, reveals, voice, or characterization. When a fact or event changes, trace consequences through relevant character knowledge, relationships, chronology, and later references. Within authorized scope, reconcile established changes with existing setting/continuity sources and pass evidence to vegabond-knowledge for affected entry and catalog maintenance. Keep provisional inventions marked as such; do not silently promote them or overwrite source conflicts. Refresh affected context before the next dependent scene rather than carrying forward a stale brief.

Repeat affected checks after each repair, revisiting expression and orthography after structural changes. Before handoff, read the complete final text within scope again. Expand beyond that scope only to inspect actual dependencies; do not restart the whole manuscript on every local edit. If a problem persists, reassess the diagnosis and correction against the intended effect instead of accumulating rewrites without progress.

Finish when the requested writing goal and constraints are met and confirmed in-scope errors are resolved. Account for material reader-understanding risks with evidence, correction, or an explicit unresolved condition; a preference difference alone does not prevent completion. Report remaining editorial choices and unavailable evidence rather than endlessly polishing or seeking universal agreement. If missing intent or material prevents judging a required condition, report that condition under the agent's completion contract instead of claiming verification complete.

For critique-only or research work, verify and correct the findings rather than rewriting the manuscript. Hand back the requested text or analysis, important editorial choices, problem resolutions and recheck evidence, reusable prevention references, and open questions. The agent instructions govern vagabond's independent review and explicit acceptance.

===== skills/vegabond-fiction-design/references/review.md =====

# Evidence-based narrative design review

Apply this before handing off a narrative design or writing dependent scenes. Use the existing brief or design artifact. The [design skill](../SKILL.md) owns narrative choices; [fiction context retrieval](../../vegabond-fiction/SKILL.md#gather-the-relevant-story-context) owns source preparation, and [manuscript review](../../vegabond-fiction/references/review.md) owns assessment and correction of actual prose. Reuse its [editorial finding rules](../../vegabond-fiction/references/review.md#judge-uncertain-findings), including alternative readings and the distinction between error, hypothesis and preference; do not invent a second scoring system.

## Ground the consequential choices

For each consequential choice, connect **choice/intended effect -> source passage and applicable conditions -> supporting interpretation -> proposed placement or change -> manuscript check or unresolved question**. Group linked elements when they share a cause; a row saying only "plausibility checked" is not a judgment. Quote briefly or give a precise source/section/scene location, retaining the relevant work revision and story time. Do not reread all lore for every choice; expand only for an actual dependency, conflict or material gap.

Separate explicit canon/author instructions, what the manuscript actually shows, editorial inference, and new invention. Check source authority, viewpoint and narrator reliability before treating a statement as fact. A plausible summary, a later chapter, or an interpretation repeated by several AI passes cannot supply missing earlier evidence. If no manuscript exists, ground the proposal in supplied constraints and label what must be established in future prose; do not fabricate passages or treat absence as a contradiction.

When authorized creation needs a new motive, event or rule, present it as a provisional design choice with its setup and consequences. Do not pretend the original text already established it, use hidden backstory to dismiss a demonstrated gap, or overwrite canon to rescue a favored plot. Expose conflicting constraints and identify which dependent choice needs source investigation or author intent. Missing unrelated material does not block independent work; no routine approval of every invention is required.

## Challenge causality, disclosure and interacting elements

Trace important decisions and emotional turns through the person's goals, available information, means, pressures and consequences. Ask what in this work supports the turn at this time and what a text-supported alternative reading would imply. Distinguish irrational or steadfast characterization from a logic defect. If motivation or reaction is only proposed, identify the scene/cue that must establish it rather than declaring it already persuasive.

Check consequential event sequences from the world and each relevant character's position, then from the reader's information at that point. Track when knowledge is acquired, relationship/state changes take effect, capabilities or costs permit an action, and clues receive meaning. Challenge a convenient resolution, unsupported emotional reversal, premature revelation, or payoff relying on an unavailable clue. Give a concrete sequence or passage that exposes the issue, not a generic warning. Preserve intentional uncertainty; a mystery can withhold an answer while still supplying the connections needed to follow the present action.

Examine real interactions among the selected elements: explanation may improve clarity but spoil suspense; a faster transition may lose emotional preparation; a new twist may break established rules; a polished thematic ending may erase intended openness. Explain the chosen reader effect and the supported tradeoff. Do not add conflict, transformation, exposition, novelty or closure merely to fill twenty boxes. Not every detail must advance plot; rhythm, atmosphere and ambiguity can serve the stated form.

## Review revisions and the handoff

Repair the earliest supported structural cause before adding explanatory prose. Trace a proposed change through affected scenes, character knowledge/relationships, chronology, setup/payoff, viewpoint and voice. Reconcile the final design's choices and disclosure sequence; update affected source/context references and checks within scope, retaining provisional status and unresolved source conflicts. Revisit only invalidated decisions; do not restart the whole work or keep rewriting without a diagnosed effect.

For a material intended effect, name what the writer should inspect in actual prose, using the reader-visible information and concrete scene choices rather than a self-assigned score. Compare with the work's voice/author direction, and state any alternative reading or missing chapter that limits the judgment. Apply the shared finding rules to changes; a preference is not a confirmed defect. Design review can support a rationale, not establish majority-reader plausibility, satisfaction, originality or resonance without relevant audience evidence.

Before handoff, read the final design against its cited sources and preservation conditions. Distinguish supported design decisions, provisional additions, unresolved premises blocking named dependent writing, and effects still to assess in manuscript or audience feedback. A completed twenty-element table is not evidence that these checks happened. Return a useful bounded proposal when possible, with those limits visible; do not claim full-work or reader validation from a partial outline. This is a document review, not proof of literary quality or automatic enforcement.

===== skills/vegabond-knowledge/references/retrieval.md =====

# Retrieval and synthesis

Use this reference for search options, related records, multi-source briefs and retrieval evaluation. Markdown details remain authoritative; catalogs and the disposable search cache are projections. Use the catalog's existing Python/PyYAML runtime; no external service is needed.

## Search a concrete question

Invoke the helper by its installed path; `--project` names the working store:

```text
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project . --query "메모리 풀링" --domain code --limit 3
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project . --query "callback shutdown" --store records
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py search --project . --query "ownership" --context "engine=UE 5.8"
```

The default searches both stores, including resolved incidents, while excluding superseded primary hits. Repeat `--domain` or `--status` for alternatives. Explicit `--status superseded` includes that state. `--scope` selects a scope substring. A `--context key=value` filter uses exact case-insensitive text equality on an existing key; it is not a compatibility solver. An absent key remains with an unknown-applicability caveat. Select actual metadata values: unequal descriptions alone do not establish semantic incompatibility.

The helper expands maintained Korean/English aliases from [search_terms.json](../scripts/search_terms.json), then searches metadata and full English bodies. Identifiers, titles/keywords, triggers/symptoms and query coverage contribute to ranking. Repetition and recent dates do not create authority. Scores are lexical heuristics, not truth or applicability. Known Korean alias substrings tolerate particles; general translation and semantic retrieval are not provided. Add aliases after observed misses, rather than collecting an exhaustive vocabulary.

Compact hits show ID, summary, scope/state, path/hash, matched fields/terms, coverage and a bounded snippet with a one-based line locator. Metadata-only matches identify the header. Read useful sections and original evidence before applying consequential advice. `total_matches` and `truncated` expose omitted candidates; vary the query, filters or limit when needed. Empty results mean no lexical match under the filters, not proof of absence.

Use `--offset N` to page primary search or `related` results beyond a result limit; limits range from 1 to 100. Continue until the relevant candidates are examined. Search's automatically attached relationship page starts at zero independently of the primary offset; page a specific ID with `related` for further edges.

Relationship pages retain traversed edges touching a returned candidate, even when the other endpoint ID is on another page. This preserves conflict and prerequisite types across page boundaries; retrieve an off-page endpoint by ID when necessary.

## Original-source state

Every returned primary or related hit includes an `evidence_notice`: its `checked_at` is a recorded evidence date and its `sha256` identifies the local entry, not a fresh original-source check. For actual links to the internal source registry, `source_checks` lists each source ID with separate `access_status` and `identity_status`. Ordinary search reports `unbound` for location-free provenance or `not_checked` for a binding that has not been checked, and adds a caveat; it reads registry metadata without opening external originals or machine root settings. Other evidence links still require appropriate review.

When original identity matters and access is authorized, explicitly check the registered IDs linked by the returned page:

```text
python <search-helper-path> search --project <working-project> --query "bandaging occupied hands" --domain fiction --verify-sources --source-root <project-ID>=<authorized-absolute-path> --no-cache
```

The placeholders above are not default locations. In the actual consuming project, reuse an authorized task path or known project metadata before asking for a needed root. Do not establish external project connections in OneStep; `unbound` provenance does not ask for a root. Repeat `--source-root project=absolute-path` for distinct projects; it requires `--verify-sources` and applies only to this call. It overrides that project's persistent mapping without writing settings. Checks cover only source IDs linked by returned primary and related hits, deduplicated within the call; retrieve omitted candidates or resolve additional needed IDs explicitly with the [source helper](storage.md#portable-source-references). This is not full-evidence or automatic web verification.

The summary `source_verification` gives requested mode, source IDs, completed outcomes, successfully read files and status counts. Reconcile IDs against the evidence needed for the decision. `retrieval_ok` can be true while original verification is incomplete. Exit 0 means retrieval succeeded and any requested registered checks matched; exit 1 means a requested check is incomplete (`unbound`, `unconfigured`, `missing`, `unavailable`, `unversioned` or `mismatch`); exit 2 reports invalid input/registry/settings/ID. With no registered IDs, verification `ok` is null, never proof that all evidence was checked. Default retrieval with unchecked or unbound provenance can exit 0; preserve its caveats.

Access `available` means the bytes were read; identity `unversioned` means no expected digest exists. A current reading can support a current claim within scope, but cannot establish a historical snapshot. A hash match proves content identity only; inspect the source for meaning, authority and applicability. Neither search mode updates historical dates, expected hashes, entries or settings. Original-source outcomes are never cached and are recomputed on each explicit verification, even when the canonical search cache stays warm.

## Follow meaningful relationships

Entries may declare optional `relations` in canonical YAML, mapping these types to nonempty lists of real entry IDs:

| Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `requires` | Target supplies an essential prerequisite or contract. |
| `derived_from` | Target provides originating evidence, not independent corroboration. |
| `prevention` | Problem record points to preventive knowledge; its prevention state is unchanged. |
| `supersedes` | Source replaces target in the stated scope; retain the old detail and set its status appropriately. |
| `conflicts_with` | Reconcile the claims under their conditions; neither automatically wins. |

```yaml
relations:
  requires: [K-EXAMPLE-PREREQUISITE]
  derived_from: [R-EXAMPLE-EVIDENCE]
```

The catalog helper validates shape and target identity, including explicit cross-store targets in filtered runs. Search follows incoming and outgoing declared edges across stores/domains, including superseded candidates. Visited-ID tracking terminates cycles. These are editorial assertions, not inferred associations; follow existing prose links when the question needs them.

```text
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py related --project . --id K-CODE-CONCURRENCY-LIFETIMES --depth 2 --limit 10
```

Search adds a separate one-hop relationship result by default; `--depth 0` disables it. Maximum depth is three per call. Bounded output reports truncation. Related candidates do not inherit primary-query filters or applicability: inspect their scope/state. Retrieve omitted conflicts or replacements before a dependent decision; limits do not justify omitting contradictory evidence. Continue necessary chains with individual IDs.

## Assemble and check the information brief

Use the shared [information-needs judgment](../../vegabond-scout/references/information-needs.md) before fixing the question set and before applying consequential results. It owns question completeness and adoption decisions; this reference owns finding and assembling evidence for those questions.

1. Split broad requests into answerable questions, recording required context/evidence in the existing brief.
2. Search each question across relevant stores. Begin with a few details; expand for unanswered questions, prerequisites, conflicts, replacements or failures. The initial three-detail allowance is not a completion limit.
3. Connect each material **claim/action → entry ID and useful section/line → original evidence → applicability → check**. Preserve cause/compatibility uncertainty, author choices and research limits. Separate proposed checks from demonstrated safeguards.
4. Reconcile overlap without counting a derived summary as independent evidence. Record conflicting claims and the conditions that could resolve them. Replacements govern only their documented scope.
5. Close every question as answered, conditional, conflicting or missing. Explain further lookup needs; reading the top hits alone is not completion.

Use the existing brief, not a global usage ledger. For multiple questions, `Question | Answer/action | IDs/evidence | Conditions/conflicts | Check/gap` is sufficient. Local entry hashes identify consulted bytes, not external-source truth or identity; use the existing source registry where needed.

Before storage, search the proposed question, synonyms and underlying cause across both stores. Review likely existing entries and relationships. Update an existing answer when it gains evidence/exceptions; keep incidents separate when conditions or causes differ. Never auto-merge by similarity. Validate, build affected catalogs and regenerate translations under the [storage procedure](storage.md).

## Disposable index and freshness

`.cache/onestep/search-v1.json` under the working project is Git-ignored here. It contains canonical text/hashes, never translations, generated rows or external originals. Do not commit it. Changed paths, sizes or modification times trigger rereading/revalidation; removed files disappear. Unchanged canonicals are reused without reopening them. Implementation/schema changes invalidate the cache. Atomic replacement avoids partial JSON; concurrent searches may replace each other's disposable snapshots and refresh on the next call.

Use `--refresh` after operations preserving file size and modification time, or when the snapshot is suspect: it rereads/hashes all canonicals, without checking external originals. Corrupt cache JSON is rebuilt. Invalid canonical metadata, duplicate IDs or unresolved declared relations produce explicit errors rather than successful partial results. Unavailable cache storage permits canonical results with a warning. `--no-cache` writes nothing and reads canonicals directly, for strictly read-only tasks.

Each query scans indexed text in memory; this is not an inverted or vector index. Measure representative growth before adding another engine. Timings and fixture results do not establish arbitrary future recall.

## Evaluate retrieval usefulness

```text
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/search.py evaluate --project . --cases skills/vegabond-knowledge/tests/retrieval_cases.json
python -m unittest discover -s skills/vegabond-knowledge/tests
```

Versioned cases declare realistic questions, filters, required/forbidden IDs and result limits. Evaluation reports required-ID recall within limits, forbidden hits, returned IDs, snippet characters and query time; failures exit nonzero. Judgments are incomplete: forbidden-hit checks are not general precision. Extend cases after meaningful misses, bad matches, language failures, incompatible contexts or omitted conflicts. Do not alter expectations merely to fit ranking.

Unit/CLI checks cover body-only matches, resolved/superseded states, known/unknown context, incoming/cross-store relations, cycles/truncation, invalid data, cache refresh/deletion, translation exclusion and read-only use. The synthetic scale test records 1,000-entry cold/warm runs and checks retrieval, bounded output and zero warm rereads. Retain environment and limits in task evidence. Storage validation and retrieval evaluation are separate acceptance checks.

## Korean question expansion and candidate diversity

The maintained `concepts` in [search_terms.json](../scripts/search_terms.json) require a combination of observed fragments: embedded values in code plus an embedding action; implementation details plus concealment; or a mystery, reader, evidence and disclosure action. Fragments start at a word boundary and allow Korean suffixes. A generic word such as number, source or reader alone does not imply the concept. For example, source text must not be inferred from the substring inside resource. `inferred_concepts` reports the matched conditions; it is a query aid, not a confirmed interpretation of intent. Preserve the original question and check the returned evidence. This is bounded lexical expansion, not general Korean understanding. Extend positive variants and nearby negative cases together after an observed miss or misleading expansion.

Default search gives different exact bodies a turn before repeated copies, including distinct answers matching fewer query terms. It retains each candidate, its lexical score and coverage; scores and coverage need not decrease monotonically after this ordering. This trades some raw ranking priority for answer diversity, not semantic certainty. Only identical canonical bodies qualify after the parser's existing line-ending normalization, without additional case/whitespace/code normalization. Store, domain, kind, state, evidence date, cause/prevention dimensions, declared relations and context-caveat classes separate groups. Both endpoints of declared conflicts and replacements remain separate, including incoming edges outside the query filter.

This ordering does not merge records or certify interchangeable evidence. Scope and context may differ even with identical bodies. Hits expose their context and a bounded `similar_content` notice with other-match count, sample IDs and truncation. Inspect relevant variants before a version-, project- or authority-dependent decision. Resolve a sampled ID with an exact-ID query and open its canonical sections. `total_matches` still counts every matched entry; `--offset` reaches all candidates. The summary `diversity` reports eligible groups and variant matches, not semantic uniqueness or completeness.

Use `search --all-variants` to retain raw lexical order when examining individual variants or auditing the ordering. It applies only to search; relationships continue to traverse every declared ID without grouping. Differences elsewhere in a body are not grouped, even if they are near duplicates. No duplicate file, historical date or source fingerprint is rewritten. Snippets are produced only for the returned page, preserving literal locators and their 300-character bound.

For capacity decisions, use the [scale budgets, measurements and migration gates](search-scale.md). A passing 1,000-entry sentinel test is not evidence that natural-language retrieval, mixed-source judgment or tens of thousands of files will stay fast.

===== skills/vegabond-knowledge/references/search-scale.md =====

# Search scale and migration gates

Use this reference when records, text volume, query frequency, duplicate density or relationship degree grows. Markdown remains authoritative; cache and future indexes are disposable projections. Keep the ordinary skill entrypoint short. See [retrieval](retrieval.md) for the current contract and [the checked regression](../tests/information-needs/retrieval-scale-results.md) for actual results and limits.

## What currently grows

Each CLI invocation loads/validates the full JSON cache, enumerates all canonical paths and builds ID/relation information before query filters. A warm `read_files=0` means unchanged details were not reparsed; it does not mean constant-cost lookup. Search scans eligible text and sorts all matches. Returning three hits bounds output, not all matching work. An entry change reparses that entry but rewrites the whole JSON cache. Body fingerprints and page-only snippets reduce repeated work; they are not an inverted index.

Exact-body diversity helps distinct answers survive duplicate competition. It cannot infer authority, interpret arbitrary paraphrases or remove semantic near duplicates. Keep unknown context, conflicting evidence and unavailable originals visible. Relationships around a heavily referenced common prerequisite can cost more than a normal query: the current traversal still scans global edges and uses a list to track selected edges.

## Measure before deciding

Use a fresh isolated project or a read-only copy; never create benchmark records in the live stores. Keep originals and judged targets intact. Exclude human translations before reading and use existence-only stubs where validation needs them. Retain code/assets hashes, dataset provenance, entry/text/cache sizes, state/domain distribution, duplicate density, relation degree, platform/runtime and timing boundaries.

Measure separately: no-cache/first indexing, repeated warm CLI calls, common-term and scoped queries, one-entry updates and peak working set. Include source verification separately when required; default metadata notices do not hash external originals. Report startup versus helper time. Reuse enough representative requests to estimate tails; three repetitions support a median/range, not P95/P99. First access to newly created paths, OS caches, antivirus and other machine activity can dominate comparisons. Do not call a warmer second run an algorithmic improvement.

Evaluate required-ID recall within the actual result limit alongside relevance/precision judgments. Include Korean/English, paraphrases, exact identifiers, multiple required answers, unknown/incompatible contexts, active/resolved/superseded states, incoming conflicts, replacement chains and misleading same-symptom records. Hold out questions from tuning and include adjacent negative cases. A repeated-template experiment tests volume and rank competition, not the semantic accuracy of independent future knowledge. State whether catalogs, source registry size, long documents, graph shape and concurrent use also grew.

## Proposed operating gates

These are adjustable project budgets, not user-agreed SLAs or universal entry limits. Count and bytes both matter.

| Observation | Response |
| --- | --- |
| Any important judged question loses a required source or transfers the wrong context/cause | Investigate immediately, at any size. Preserve the failed input/expectation; refine routing, terms, diversity or the consuming decision and rerun affected positive/negative cases. |
| Around 1,000–5,000 entries, or earlier with long text/frequent queries | Build 50–100 real task questions plus held-out judgments. Watch known-group dominance and scope/context completeness. Compare domain/metadata prefiltering, normalized-text reuse, page-only snippets and indexed graph adjacency. |
| Around 5,000 entries with continuing growth; routine use approaching 10,000 | Start an indexed-search comparison. The measured file/JSON approach already incurs seconds in this range on the audited workload. Do not wait for 30,000 before planning the transition. |
| Warm P95 exceeds 2 seconds across at least 50 representative requests, or repeated lookups materially delay a task | Prioritize a search index. Aim initially for about one second, then set the real budget from use. Do not infer P95 from the small regression sample. |
| First/no-cache operations repeatedly exceed 30 seconds, one-entry updates take seconds, or peak working set exceeds a proposed 512 MiB budget | Compare incremental persistence and candidate loading against whole-JSON reads/writes. Adjust the memory budget for the device and real workload. |
| Thousands of incoming relationships accumulate on common entries | Compare a set for edge deduplication and per-ID/type adjacency. Preserve incoming/outgoing discovery, conflict visibility, bounded pages and continuation. |
| Paraphrase misses survive routing and lexical improvements | Compare Korean tokenization/query reformulation and, if justified, semantic candidate retrieval/reranking. Keep exact-ID and applicability controls; embeddings do not certify evidence or replace required-source checks. |

## Candidate transition and acceptance

Compare a local metadata table plus SQLite FTS5 as a next candidate, keeping Markdown and IDs stable. Separate import/update/delete detection from query execution; index only canonical English. Filter store/domain and established context, retrieve text candidates, then apply source/state checks and diversity. Keep source identity/access status independent and original hashing explicit. Updating an index must not rewrite historical evidence.

FTS5 supplies ranking and tokenizer options; it needs its own language and identifier tests. In particular, trigram full-text queries shorter than three Unicode characters do not match, so two-character Korean terms need a deliberate path. A content-table projection requires index consistency on changes/deletes. See the primary [FTS5 tokenizer documentation](https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html#the_trigram_tokenizer), [BM25](https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html#the_bm25_function) and [external-content consistency requirements](https://www.sqlite.org/fts5.html#external_content_tables). This is a comparison candidate, not an implemented backend or a proven speedup.

Accept a transition only after the same judged questions, exact identifiers, unknown-context caveats, state filters, cross-store/conflict traversal, source notices, freshness and corruption/deletion checks pass, with measured latency/memory gains. Keep before/after evidence and rollback/rebuild instructions. For a busy or changed workspace, use an isolated index/corpus so comparison cannot modify real records. Never lower recall expectations or erase conflicting variants to obtain faster numbers.

===== skills/vegabond-knowledge/references/storage.md =====

# Storage and lifecycle

## Location and domain

Paths below are relative to the working project. Reuse its existing system if it supports scoped retrieval and provenance. Otherwise use:

```text
knowledge/index.md              # Domain routing only
knowledge/<domain>/index.md     # Searchable knowledge summaries
knowledge/<domain>/<id>.md      # One reusable topic or lesson
records/index.md                # Domain routing only
records/<domain>/index.md       # Searchable problem summaries
records/<domain>/<id>.md         # Problem, correction, and recheck history
```

The initial domains are `code`, `fiction`, `presentation`, `planning`, and `shared`; they are not an exhaustive list. Classify by the subject, not create/revise/research or file extension. Add a domain when actual content requires it. Put language, engine, genre, format, component, work identifier, and task subtype in metadata before introducing deeper folders.

Give a cross-domain item one primary home and link to it from other relevant domain indexes. Use `shared` only when evidence supports cross-domain applicability, not as a default catch-all. Project incidents and canon stay in their project; promote a general method to shared guidance only when the evidence supports that scope.

Do not create parallel `lessons/` and `knowledge/` stores. If an existing lesson system is present, reuse it or migrate relevant entries and references within the authorized scope, preserving IDs and evidence.

Maintained store documents include indexes, knowledge details, problem details, and archives. For each new or changed English document, create or update its `<stem>.kr.md` human translation from the English without reading the translation. Do not treat these entries as exempt from the agent's translation rule merely because they are records rather than skills. Before handoff, check English links and status consistency, and verify translation-pair existence without loading translation contents.

## Entry header and body

For the default Markdown stores, create or update one independently reusable topic/check per detail, with a YAML header containing these fields:

| Field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `id` | Stable project-unique ID; letters, digits, dots, underscores and hyphens. Comparison is case-insensitive. |
| `title` | Specific question, method or subject. |
| `summary` | One or two sentences giving the usable point and its essential condition. |
| `kind` | Knowledge: reference, method, convention or lesson. Records: error, suggestion or hypothesis. |
| `scope` | Project/component/work and applicability boundary, as a string. |
| `keywords` | Nonempty list of useful English concepts, synonyms and exact technical identifiers. |
| `status` | Knowledge: active, enforced or superseded. Records: open, resolved or superseded. |
| `checked_at` | Actual evidence-check date as YYYY-MM-DD, or null if unknown; never fill it from the edit date automatically. |

Optional `context` is a string or flat mapping of strings for relevant version, engine, format, genre, viewpoint or other conditions. Quote versions and YAML-reserved words. Avoid irrelevant empty fields. Header data is not skill-discovery frontmatter; human translations present the information for readers without becoming discovery inputs.

Optional `relations` maps `requires`, `derived_from`, `prevention`, `supersedes` or `conflicts_with` to nonempty lists of existing entry IDs, including other stores/domains. Add only relationships that improve a real lookup; see [relationship meanings and traversal](retrieval.md#follow-meaningful-relationships). The helper validates shape and target identity. Edges do not establish causation, independent evidence, compatibility or verified prevention. Preserve prose evidence links and historical dates; adding a retrieval edge is not a new source investigation.

For example, a header might be:

```yaml
---
id: K-FIC-001
title: Check information access in limited viewpoint
summary: Under limited narration, check each claim against the focal character's information access before line polishing.
kind: method
scope: naru-novel; Seorin-limited narration
keywords: [viewpoint, POV, character-knowledge]
status: active
checked_at: null
context:
  viewpoint: third-person limited
---
```

This is a format example, not a live entry. Follow it with four nonempty H2 sections:

- Knowledge: **Guidance**, **Applicability and exceptions**, **Checks**, **Evidence**.
- Records: **Expected and observed**, **Cause and resolution**, **Recheck and prevention**, **Evidence**.

Keep the useful action short, explain exceptions, distinguish source facts/methods/conventions/editorial judgment, and cite original sources with location and verification context. Problem records retain cause confidence, corrections or proposed action, resolution and recheck evidence, and prevention links. Unknown causes remain unknown. Enforced status requires a demonstrated safeguard; saving a lesson does not prove enforcement.

Reference original issues, reports, tests, passages, screenshots or logs rather than copying them. Group minor same-cause findings where useful. Referenced project sources remain authoritative for their facts; the detail is authoritative for its entry metadata, and catalog rows are derived projections.

### Problem retrieval fields

New or normalized `records/` details additionally require all five fields below. These are incident metadata, not additional requirements for `knowledge/` details.

| Field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `trigger` | Short conditions/action under which the problem occurred; null if unknown. Enables lookup before a symptom exists. |
| `symptom` | Nonempty description of the observed failure, with discriminating characteristics. For suggestions/hypotheses describe the observed concern without asserting an unconfirmed defect. |
| `cause_status` | `unknown`, `suspected`, or `confirmed`, supported in Cause and resolution. A confirmed defect can still have an unknown cause. |
| `prevention` | A specific action/check with its essential condition, or null when none is established. Detailed steps, exceptions and evidence stay in Recheck and prevention. |
| `prevention_status` | `none`, `proposed`, `verified`, or `invalidated`. `none` requires null prevention; other states require the action. `verified` additionally requires a real `checked_at` and demonstration evidence in the body. |

Keep these phrases short enough for a useful catalog row; do not repeat logs or every example. Use `keywords` for useful synonyms and exact identifiers, not redundant copies of every phrase. `summary` gives the finding and its essential condition, rather than duplicating the entire header. Use explicit scope/context for versions, canon or format differences that change applicability.

Record example only, not a live incident:

```yaml
---
id: R-FIC-EXAMPLE
title: Inaccessible private decision in limited narration
summary: A focal character asserted a private decision without an information source; revision restored uncertainty.
kind: error
scope: story-alpha; chapter-3
keywords: [viewpoint, POV, information-access, private-thought]
status: resolved
checked_at: null
context:
  canon: revision-B
trigger: Drafting third-person limited narration
symptom: Focal character states another character's private decision without access
cause_status: confirmed
prevention: Trace each private-knowledge assertion to a source available to the focal character
prevention_status: proposed
---
```

`resolved` describes the incident, not the prevention. Use `verified` only after the specified check demonstrably detects the original failure or a controlled equivalent in its stated context; a corrected artifact alone is insufficient. For editorial work, a documented review that detects the relevant defect is valid evidence without pretending to be automated enforcement. Record separate correction and safeguard check dates/results in Recheck and prevention; `checked_at` is the latest actual material evidence check, not an invented safeguard date. A date and valid header cannot prove the demonstration happened: the specialist must inspect its evidence.

Keep the existing four body sections. Expected and observed records the original contract, actual finding, occurrence conditions and location. Cause and resolution distinguishes diagnosis confidence, supporting evidence, correction and unknowns. Recheck and prevention records the corrected result's checks separately from future safeguards, their state, applicability and exceptions. Evidence links the original artifact/version and reports. A suggestion or hypothesis does not become an error merely because it has been recorded.

### Earlier structured records

A record with the common header but none of the five fields remains readable. The helper warns with `legacy_record_fields`, leaves its source unchanged and projects the new dimensions as **unrecorded**. `--strict` treats that migration gap as an error. A partial extension is an error, not a legacy exemption. New and edited records must use all five fields; the helper cannot infer when an old file was edited, so this writing rule also belongs to the skill. Normalize only during authorized maintenance, preserving ID, path, evidence and unknowns. Do not fill fields by guessing from a title or previous catalog row.

## Refresh and validate catalogs

[catalog.py](../scripts/catalog.py) uses Python 3.9+ with PyYAML from an existing runtime; it installs nothing. Commands below are run from the OneStep checkout. In another project, use the helper's absolute path and that working project's `--project` value.

```text
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/catalog.py build --project . --domain fiction
python skills/vegabond-knowledge/scripts/catalog.py check --project . --domain fiction
```

`--store knowledge`, `--store records`, or default `both` selects stores; repeat `--domain` for affected areas. Omitting domains performs explicit full-store maintenance, including project-wide duplicate-ID checking. Filtered runs validate only selected details and explicit related-entry references. Ordinary retrieval still uses bounded catalog searches; do not rebuild after every information request.

The helper generates six-column **ID/title/kind | summary | scope/context | keywords | status/checked date | detail** rows inside managed markers. For record rows, the summary column also exposes trigger and symptom; the status column also exposes cause confidence, prevention state and the short prevention action. Knowledge rows are unchanged. Keep resolved incidents searchable in the active catalog; only superseded entries move to archives. Preserve project notes outside managed markers. Related-domain references keep one primary detail; do not copy its content. Root indexes remain small routing documents maintained by this skill when domains change.

Save in this order: write canonical details and their translations; run `build`; regenerate human translations for the English paths listed in `catalogs_written`; then run `check`. Build is preparation: missing translations/routing are warnings so the English catalog can be produced first. Check requires catalog freshness, valid metadata/states, unique IDs, required body sections, local file references, routing and translation-pair existence. Duplicate YAML keys and malformed structured headers are errors. Metadata or reference errors stop build before writes; each changed catalog is replaced atomically. A filesystem failure can leave some catalogs updated, so rerun check and repair within scope. The helper never writes canonical details or translations.

Links outside the project and section-anchor accuracy require separate verification. The catalog helper reads explicitly related canonical entries and internal source-registry metadata; it checks other local file targets for existence without loading their contents. It checks translation existence, not translation meaning or freshness. Generate every changed translation from English without reopening it. Diagnostics are bounded to the first 20 with total/error counts; narrow the selected domains to investigate larger result sets.

### Portable source references

The [source registry](../../../knowledge/sources.md) separates historical identity from location. Its YAML `sources` mapping gives each `SRC-...` ID exactly `project`, `path` and `sha256` (an existing lowercase digest or null). In OneStep, `project` and `path` are both null: these entries preserve provenance without configuring another project. Do not add real project locations or connections to OneStep. In an actual consuming project, an explicitly authorized binding may provide a lowercase project ID and forward-slash project-relative canonical path; both fields must be present together or both null. Each ID has an H2 heading for a normal local Markdown link. Keep passage/section locators and evidence context in the referring entry. Preserve known fingerprints; never infer missing versions, update historical evidence dates, or certify a historical manuscript from a mutable current file.

A source ID with a recorded digest identifies that content snapshot. Give a different evidentiary revision a new ID rather than repointing existing historical citations; relocate the same snapshot by changing only the local root when possible.

In the actual working project, store authorized persistent roots only in its Git-ignored `knowledge/sources.local.json`, or supply an authorized temporary root with `--root project=absolute-path`. The temporary root overrides that project's persistent mapping for this call and writes nothing. There are no default project IDs or locations. Reuse a user-confirmed task path or known project metadata before asking for a missing root. Do not create such connections in OneStep, commit local settings, fabricate roots, search unrelated projects, or copy source libraries to repair a missing mapping. Each consuming project must exclude its own persistent settings from Git.

After following a registered source link, resolve only the IDs needed for the current claim with the [source helper](../scripts/sources.py):

```text
python <source-helper-path> resolve --project <working-project> --id <registered-source-ID>
python <source-helper-path> resolve --project <working-project> --id <source-ID-1> --id <source-ID-2> --root <project-ID>=<authorized-absolute-path>
```

The command reads the selected original as bytes to compare SHA-256; run it only within authorized source scope. It never prints the content, reads translations, writes settings or entries, or promotes the observed hash automatically. It rejects traversal and resolved paths outside the configured root. A matching digest establishes content identity, not truth, applicability, source quality or author acceptance. Still inspect the relevant original sections when the claim requires that evidence.

Repeat `--id` to resolve multiple distinct IDs. A single ID retains its original result-object shape; a batch returns `requested_ids`, `completed` and one `results` outcome per requested ID in order, including invalid or inaccessible IDs. Reconcile input IDs/counts with results before claiming coverage. Duplicate IDs (case-insensitive) or duplicate root assignments are invalid, never silently discarded. Repeat `--root` only for distinct projects.

Exit 0 means every result is `matched`; exit 1 means at least one is `unbound`, `unconfigured`, `missing`, `unavailable`, `mismatch`, or `unversioned`; exit 2 means invalid registry, ID, settings or path. A bad ID in a valid batch does not discard other outcomes; invalid shared input such as a malformed registry prevents resolution. `unbound` means provenance only and returns before reading machine settings or looking for an external project; it is not a request to configure one. Each result separates `access_status` from `identity_status`: `available` means bytes were read, while `unversioned` means no expected digest exists. Both unversioned and mismatched files can be accessible without verified identity. An accessible unversioned original can support a bounded current reading but cannot certify historical identity. Keep these limits in the brief. A required inaccessible or identity-uncertain source blocks the dependent check; continue independent work. Do not fill old null hashes or update historical dates during lookup. Machine availability is not incident resolution or prevention state.

Catalog build/check validates registry IDs and translation existence, then emits `source_unbound` for historical provenance or `source_unverified` for a binding that still needs a separate source check. It does not load local settings or original content. Registration cannot silently verify evidence. Unknown IDs or malformed registries stop catalog writes. Ordinary lookup resolves only relevant IDs. Update registry data and its human translation together without rereading translations; other external references retain their existing warnings.

Legacy prose entries remain usable: preserve existing catalog rows and their conditions under an explicitly unverified Legacy entries section; unindexed legacy files get an unknown-metadata pointer. Do not infer IDs, dates, rules or states. Migrate a legacy entry during authorized maintenance, preserving its path/ID/evidence, then rebuild. Default runs warn; `--strict` treats legacy entries as errors for scopes already normalized. New or changed entries must use the header even if older neighbors remain legacy. Strict checking is not permission to rewrite historical evidence or all unrelated files.

## Capture, consolidate, and promote

Before saving, use the [retrieval helper](retrieval.md) across both stores for the proposed question, aliases and underlying cause. Inspect close candidates and their relationships before choosing update, separate incident or new knowledge. Similarity proposes candidates; it never authorizes automatic merging.

Before saving, identify the future decision, action, or check the material can support and the conditions under which it applies. Reusable means useful in another task under matching conditions, not universally valid. Choose the smallest useful destination:

- **Knowledge:** state the usable principle or method, its rationale, checks, and exceptions. Make the title and summary about the reusable question or action rather than a project's task chronology.
- **Problem record:** retain the minimum incident evidence needed to distinguish the trigger, symptom, cause confidence, correction, and recheck. Link to the common knowledge entry instead of repeating its full procedure.
- **Existing source or task history:** keep unique project facts, story canon, author preferences, and one-off progress in their authoritative source. Add a scoped reference or convention only when repeated retrieval benefits from it, without copying whole profiles or specifications. Findings with neither reuse value nor a tracking need can stay in the task report; unresolved problems that need follow-up still deserve records.

Search existing indexes for the same principle, question, or underlying cause before writing. Prefer updating applicability, exceptions, evidence, or recurrence in an existing entry over creating a project-specific copy. One knowledge entry can link to several incident records, each retaining its relevant circumstances. Consolidate incidents only when the cause and occurrence conditions support the same prevention; a shared symptom is insufficient. Link uncertain matches without merging their diagnoses. Keep material disagreement explicit; do not overwrite one version merely because it is newer. Preserve the link from a problem to its prevention and from a reusable lesson to its originating evidence.

Generalize the explanation without erasing the evidence boundary. Keep essential version, lifecycle, genre, viewpoint, audience, or other conditions in the existing `scope`, `context`, body, and source references. Retain exact identifiers needed to find or reproduce the incident; omit incidental names and long logs from the reusable guidance. Distinguish the conditions actually checked from broader applicability inferred from a mechanism or rationale, and name what remains untested. A successful case can support a bounded lesson, but cannot establish verification in other environments. Never universalize canon or preferences, or promote a proposed safeguard merely by generalizing its wording. Generalization does not move records across projects or make a domain-specific method belong in `shared` automatically; the location and authorization rules above still apply. Use the existing schema and stores.

Keep recurrence history short: occurrence date or unknown, location/version, evidence link, correction/recheck result, and the prior safeguard's outcome. Distinguish **not executed**, **executed but insufficient**, and **invalidated by changed conditions**; retain unknown outcomes rather than inventing one. When contrary evidence invalidates the recorded prevention claim, mark it `invalidated`, retain the old demonstration context, and investigate a replacement. A missed check does not automatically disprove that check. Add separate incidents when materially different conditions require different remedies. Keep long logs at their source instead of growing the detail into a transcript.

Promote only confirmed, consequential lessons when recurrence, likely reuse, or a useful safeguard warrants it. Store the incident in `records/` and its distilled reusable content in `knowledge/`, with reciprocal links instead of copied narratives. The specialist supplies the diagnosis and domain checks; this skill organizes them and does not invent a resolved cause or certify the deliverable.

Prefer preventing recurrence through structure, types, validation, lint, or regression checks. Retain evidence that the safeguard detects the original failure or a controlled equivalent. For judgment-dependent work, record a specific conditional review check and its rationale. Saving an entry alone does not establish prevention.

### Game development terms

Evaluate each relevant term independently of the game feature that exposed it. A term is relevant when its meaning is needed to explain a decision, mechanism, failure or check in the task. This applies to research, design, implementation and debugging, including work whose entire gameplay outcome stays in its original project.

1. **Establish the term's origin.** Check Unreal Engine APIs, reflection specifiers and settings against Epic's documentation or the matching engine declaration. Check C++ keywords, specifiers and standard library symbols against the applicable language or library specification. For techniques such as object pooling, use an established name and a primary technical reference. An Unreal-style prefix on a game class does not establish engine ownership. If origin or meaning is uncertain, check the source and keep any unresolved question in the task findings.
2. **Extract the public knowledge.** Preserve the exact spelling and qualification in `keywords`. In Guidance, explain what each term means for the reusable question. Give the applicable version or operating conditions where material, a useful check, and an Evidence locator for the claim. Separate documented behavior from what was tested in the game. Keep private classes, asset paths, game rules, tuning values, source code and logs in the originating project. Apply this boundary to metadata, body, examples and evidence alike.
3. **Choose the destination.** Look up each term and the reusable question in the authorized store. Extend the matching entry when an explanation, search term or condition is missing. If a complete entry exists, reuse it. If none covers the question, create a bounded `reference` entry, even on the first encounter and even if there was no incident or runtime test. Group terms that explain one question. A passing mention alone needs no entry. In read-only work, return the same selection and proposed content.
4. **Check the saved result.** Search by each retained canonical term and inspect the detail. The review passes when the relevant entry is findable, its body explains the term and its conditions with evidence, and it is usable without the originating project's vocabulary or data. A tag without an explanation leaves the review incomplete.

| Review case | Expected decision |
| --- | --- |
| A game-specific actor has a mismatched `Tick` declaration. The question is how to catch that signature error. | Keep `AActor::Tick` (the engine virtual function) and `override` (the C++ specifier that requires an override). Record the signature check and its sources. Omit the game's class name and behavior. [Epic API, UE 5.5](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/GameFramework/AActor/Tick?application_version=5.5) · [C++ draft, class.virtual](https://eel.is/c++draft/class.virtual) |
| A project uses a fixed number of objects in a pool. The question is what state a reused object must reset. | Keep `object pooling`, the reuse and reset conditions, and the check on reacquisition. Leave the asset names and chosen pool size in the game. [Game Programming Patterns, Object Pool](https://gameprogrammingpatterns.com/object-pool.html) |
| Only a boss's reward changes. An unchanged C++ file containing `virtual` appears in the diff context. | Keep the reward decision local. The language keyword does not explain the decision or its check. |

## Retire without losing provenance

During normal use, merge duplicates and correct stale applicability. To supersede an entry, set its canonical status and record the reason/replacement; rebuilding routes its row to the domain's `archive.md`, creating that catalog only when needed. Reactivation routes it back. Keep detail paths and evidence stable; do not hand-edit generated rows. Existing native systems use their own archive mechanism. Age alone does not invalidate useful knowledge.

Recheck affected entries when their source, version, canon, or safeguard changes. An old information brief must be refreshed if such a change invalidates it. If targeted searches repeatedly return too much unrelated material, split the affected domain index by actual project area and retain a small routing index. Expand structure only in response to observed retrieval friction; do not schedule full-history rereads after every task.

## First catalog build

For a new store/domain, write valid details and their human translations in the domain directory, and create or update the root routing document and its translation. Root links may point to domain catalogs that this build will create; no index stubs or special initialization command are needed. Run the usual build/translate/check sequence above. The helper checks local file existence after all selected catalog outputs are planned: only a missing catalog in that build plan is allowed. Other missing files and unselected missing catalogs still block writes. `check` remains read-only and requires catalogs to exist, including on the first run.

===== skills/vegabond-planning/references/large-plans.md =====

# Large plans: decomposition, integration, and change management

Use this when linked subplans share goals, scarce resources, dependencies, or decisions whose changes affect other parts. Reuse the [planning method](../SKILL.md), [review checks](review.md), and [scout's scale and stage choices](../../vegabond-scout/SKILL.md#frame-and-classify). This reference coordinates planning content; the calling operation owns execution. Document count alone does not justify more stages, new tools, delegation, or a large template. A local change follows only its actual dependencies.

## Establish the whole and its sources

State the intended experience or outcome, beneficiaries, boundaries, priorities, shared constraints, and whole-plan acceptance conditions in the existing brief. Identify what is required, proposed, deferred, or still unknown. Distinguish decisions needed now from details that can wait for evidence. A complete outline does not make all its choices settled.

Locate the authoritative source for each consequential decision and its current revision or status. Reuse existing design documents, trackers, and decision history.
