# Fit animation contacts with constrained inverse kinematics

## Guidance

Inverse kinematics (IK) solves joint transforms for a desired endpoint or effector. Unreal's Two Bone IK uses a three-joint limb and a Joint Target Location to guide the bend. FABRIK iteratively reaches backward and forward through a chain, with a precision goal and iteration budget. Express the effector, pole and support plane in the solver's declared spaces. Offset the ankle by the calibrated sole or shoe geometry.

## Applicability and exceptions

For static supports, latch a stance contact and release it during swing. Blend from the source pose, then solve pelvis displacement against that blended endpoint and both legs' reach. Fall back if a compatible pelvis shift does not exist. `UPoseableMeshComponent` allows bone-space or component/world pose operations according to the API used. Specify the supported space for each operation. Keep visual contact independent of the gameplay capsule.

## Checks

Compare source and final skinned poses on flat ground, stairs, conflicting contacts, mid-blend, unreachable supports and swing release. Verify heel/toe penetration, knee continuity, previous-frame pose history and unchanged gameplay collision.

## Evidence

Epic documents the solve controls and poseable mesh APIs. Prior Unreal observations covered a static support, calibrated sampling and reach rejection for a skinned biped. Commercial footwear rigs, moving supports, retargeting and a production animation graph were outside that observation.

- [Epic — Two Bone IK](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/animation-blueprint-two-bone-ik-in-unreal-engine)
- [Epic — FABRIK AnimBP Node](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/fabrik-animation-blueprint-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US)

# Evaluate rewards by the behavior they make worthwhile

## Guidance

Proposed design synthesis: define the intended repeated choice, its time/resource cost, failure consequence and alternative. Efficiency upgrades or automation can change the work rather than only increasing numbers. Attack-to-heal and time pressure can reward engagement, but compare them against safe policies and different skill levels. Consult the existing economy and combat entries before tuning.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

Bloodborne producer Masaaki Yamagiwa explicitly describes a short post-hit window in which counterattacks restore part of the lost health, with recoverable health visible in the gauge. A transferable implementation tracks a bounded recoverable-damage pool and expiry, then applies eligible hits without exceeding it; renewal/stacking rules must be authored rather than inferred. This differs from ordinary kill-to-heal. DOOM's GDC presentation is an authoritative discovery lead for push-forward combat, but its description alone does not establish exact resource/drop formulas.

## Applicability and exceptions

Bloodborne's short recovery window is supported by a producer account; exact damage percentages/timing and DOOM drop algorithms remain separate questions. Rewards are not proof of enjoyment or a reason to ban cautious play.

Bloodborne's **Regain System** makes a portion of newly lost health recoverable for a limited time through eligible counterattacks. When adapting this as a risk/reward mechanic, check whether players can read the recoverable portion and whether repeated hits can restore more than the recorded loss. Retain the expiry and recovery cap as game-specific choices. [Masaaki Yamagiwa — Bloodborne combat details](https://blog.playstation.com/2014/08/13/bloodborne-on-ps4-new-combat-details/)

## Checks

Proposed: compare passive/aggressive/efficient policies, record time-to-upgrade and recovery opportunities, then test perceived agency.

## Evidence

- Reuse [resource-flow analysis](K-GAME-ECONOMY.md) for upgrade/automation costs and [combat response design](K-GAME-ACTION-COMBAT.md) for offensive incentives. These existing references retain their source-specific conditions; no new playtest is implied.

- [Ian Schreiber — Probability and Randomness](https://gamebalanceconcepts.wordpress.com/2010/07/28/level-4-probability-and-randomness/) — 2010 designer course; probability distributions and expected values. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V05, V17, V34 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [Masaaki Yamagiwa — Bloodborne combat details](https://blog.playstation.com/2014/08/13/bloodborne-on-ps4-new-combat-details/) — Producer-authored Regain System discussion; historical 2014 design account, not exact frame/percentage data. Checked 2026-11-07.
- [id Software/GDC — Embracing Push Forward Combat](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2KQNpQD8Ayo) — 2018 official talk description only; detailed formulas not verified. Checked 2026-11-07.

# Fake inaccessible interiors with ray-box mapping

## Guidance

Use on windows whose rooms cannot be entered. Transform the view ray into room-local coordinates, intersect an implicit room box, and sample the corresponding wall/floor/ceiling texture. Introduce room variation without authoring full interior meshes.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### interior mapping

Interior mapping uses a ray-box intersection in room-local coordinates to determine which interior surface appears behind a facade. Check grazing views and nearby cameras where parallax errors or the lack of real geometry become visible.

## Applicability and exceptions

The room is a shading illusion: it does not automatically provide real collision, navigation, shadows or interactive furniture. Close grazing views reveal limits.

## Checks

Proposed: compare window edges, corner views and repeated rooms; switch to real geometry for enterable spaces.

## Evidence

- [Joost van Dongen — Interior Mapping](https://www.proun-game.com/Oogst3D/CODING/InteriorMapping/InteriorMapping.pdf) — 2008 author manuscript; abstract and ray intersections. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.

# Bound historical hit resolution for latency compensation

## Guidance

Retain timestamped collision history and resolve eligible shots against a bounded reconstructed past view. Account for clock mapping and interpolation delay. Keep the authoritative current world distinct from temporary historical queries.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Rewinding benefits a shooter's historical view but can hurt a player who has reached cover. 'People always prefer this' is not established; choose fairness rules and maximum rewind explicitly.

With **server rewind**, an authoritative game evaluates an eligible input against stored historical collision poses. The target time depends on how client time maps to server time and how far the displayed remote state lagged behind. Clamp it to available history, choose a missing-history policy, and verify the live collision world is unchanged after a historical query. Riot's account supplies a game-specific example of the latency tradeoff. [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode)

## Checks

Proposed: record both client views and server history for cover transitions, fast targets and asymmetric latency.

## Evidence

- [Riot — Peeking into VALORANT's Netcode](https://www.riotgames.com/en/news/peeking-valorants-netcode) — 2020 developer account; server authority, prediction and server combat resolution; historical implementation. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V28 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Validate perceived scale against traversal and camera constraints

## Guidance

Start with character dimensions, camera envelope, turning radius and encounter sightlines. Block out doors/corridors at usable scale, then tune occlusion, landmarks and route lengths to produce the intended exploration rhythm. Use branching and reconnection where production scope benefits, while preserving meaningful alternative choices.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

This is transferable synthesis, not proof that every game's doors are a fixed multiple of reality. Physical area, travel time, content density and perceived scale differ. See existing exploration and platformer references.

A **blockout** or **graybox** is playable geometry built from simple shapes to test layout before detailed art. Level design metrics express repeatable dimensions such as character clearance, step height and jump reach. Test turns, landings and passages with the actual controller and camera, then vary the metric where the encounter needs a different challenge. [The Level Design Book — Metrics](https://book.leveldesignbook.com/process/blockout/metrics)

## Checks

Proposed: test traversal with production camera and all supported bodies; ask unfamiliar players to find goals without route explanations.

## Evidence

- Related design references: [exploration](K-GAME-EXPLORATION.md), [platformer movement](K-GAME-PLATFORMER.md) and [prototype/playtest](K-GAME-PROTOTYPE-PLAYTEST.md). Scale and route recommendations are synthesis to test in the target game.

- [Itay Keren — Scroll Back](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/scroll-back-the-theory-and-practice-of-cameras-in-side-scrollers) — 2015 creator-authored article; camera-window, framing and smoothing. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Shaker et al. — Constructive Generation Methods for Dungeons and Levels](https://www.pcgbook.com/chapter03.pdf) — 2016 author chapter; representation, BSP, agents and platform levels. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V11, V12, V24 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Select stable lock-on targets before changing movement framing

## Guidance

Filter candidates by range, view cone and line of sight, then score the survivors for acquisition. Hysteresis uses a wider exit band than entry band to suppress boundary jitter. Retaining the current selection avoids changing target whenever two scores cross. Define deterministic ties, switch order and whether occlusion allows grace. Distinguish angular centering from distance in projected screen pixels.

## Applicability and exceptions

For Unreal object lifetimes, `TWeakObjectPtr` holds a non-owning object reference. Test validity before use so a destroyed actor cannot pass selection to a replacement sharing a gameplay ID. Evaluate press edges separately from held directions. Character facing, camera orbit and attack decisions can then consume the selected identity independently.

## Checks

Cross two targets near the aiming center, oscillate at entry and exit boundaries, destroy and replace the selected actor, occlude it, and hold both switch directions. Log selected identity before and after each change. Check physical input and camera comfort in play.

## Evidence

Nintendo's staff interview describes Z-targeting feedback and Navi. Epic's example shows target filtering. Earlier scripted Unreal checks covered selection continuity, switching and actor replacement in a fixed view. They do not validate camera feel or Nintendo's implementation details.

- [Nintendo — Where the Name Navi Came From](https://iwataasks.nintendo.com/interviews/3ds/zelda-ocarina-of-time/1/3/)

===== knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-LOCKSTEP.md =====

id: K-GAME-TECH-LOCKSTEP
title: "Use lockstep only with an exact determinism contract"
summary: "Exchange tick-indexed inputs instead of every object's state. Sharing a random seed alone does not guarantee determinism; floating-point and threading differences can diverge."
kind: reference
scope: "Game development; engine-independent mechanisms with version-dependent engine examples"
keywords: ["deterministic", "lockstep", "RTS", "hash", "결정론", "deterministic lockstep", "playout delay buffer", "state hash"]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-19'
context:
  evidence_check: "External source text checked; source-specific limits appear in Evidence"
  application_status: "Reference research and proposed checks only; no engine implementation or performance validation"
---
# Use lockstep only with an exact determinism contract

## Guidance

Exchange tick-indexed inputs instead of every object's state. Start from identical state and make random streams, processing order, arithmetic and simulation steps reproducible. Use state hashes to locate divergence. A playout buffer absorbs arrival jitter but adds delay.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Sharing a random seed alone does not guarantee determinism; floating-point and threading differences can diverge. Shared simulation can expose information clients should not know.

In **deterministic lockstep**, peers advance tick n only when the required inputs for n are available. A **playout delay buffer** holds inputs briefly to absorb jitter. It costs latency and cannot fill an indefinitely missing tick. Compare hashes of equivalent simulation state after each tick and locate the first divergence, checking seed, update order and arithmetic on each supported build. [Glenn Fiedler — Deterministic Lockstep](https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/)

## Checks

Proposed: compare per-tick hashes across supported platforms/builds and long runs with the same recorded inputs.

## Evidence

- [Glenn Fiedler — Deterministic Lockstep](https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/) — 2014 author article; exact determinism and input synchronization. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V04 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Separate skill estimation from matchmaking policy

## Guidance

Estimate skill separately from selecting a match. TrueSkill represents a distribution with mean and uncertainty and updates from results; Elo uses expected outcomes for rating updates. Match search additionally chooses latency, party, queue time and acceptable skill spread constraints.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Do not generalize 'the highest party member sets the match' to every game. Ratings are estimates, not a promise of 50% win probability in every individual match.

In **TrueSkill**, a Bayesian skill rating represents a player's estimated skill by a mean and uncertainty. A new player's estimate can move more after an informative result than a well-established one. When testing matchmaking rating (MMR), evaluate prediction calibration separately from queue time and region constraints. Microsoft documents how its model updates individual beliefs from team outcomes. [Microsoft Research — TrueSkill Ranking System](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/project/trueskill-ranking-system/)

## Checks

Proposed: evaluate new-player convergence, mixed-skill parties, queue scarcity and latency; compare predicted versus observed outcomes.

## Evidence

- [Microsoft Research — TrueSkill Ranking System](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/research/project/trueskill-ranking-system/) — Official project text; mean, uncertainty, team outcomes; distinct from complete match search. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.

---
# Check buffer bounds and object lifetimes in game data handling

## Guidance

MITRE classifies a write before or after the allocated buffer as `CWE-787` (Out-of-bounds Write). It can corrupt neighboring data or crash a program. When loading save files, assets or network messages in C++, verify each length and offset before forming a pointer or writing. After checking offset ≤ capacity, compare count against capacity minus offset, taking element size and signed-to-unsigned conversions into account.

## Applicability and exceptions

For native C++ builds with supported instrumentation, Clang AddressSanitizer (ASan) detects many executed out-of-bounds accesses and use-after-free errors. A dangling pointer refers to an object whose lifetime has ended. Even an in-range index is unsafe if the owner has released or reallocated storage. A clean run covers only executed paths. Arbitrary code execution (ACE) is a possible consequence of some failures and does not identify their underlying weakness.

## Checks

Exercise empty, truncated, exact-fit and oversized records in a supported instrumented build. Verify that rejection leaves the destination state valid. Check callback and view lifetimes across scene unload and buffer reallocation. Attribute a diagnostic to the first invalid access and its allocation lifetime.

## Evidence

MITRE defines the weakness and Clang documents ASan coverage and instrumentation. A public historical game replay demonstrates an observed outcome, without establishing a particular buffer bug. No sanitizer run or particular game defect was verified in this review.

- [MITRE — CWE-787: Out-of-bounds Write](https://cwe.mitre.org/data/definitions/787.html)
- [Clang — AddressSanitizer](https://clang.llvm.org/docs/AddressSanitizer.html)

# Schedule adaptive music at musical boundaries

## Guidance

Use synchronized stems for vertical layering and compatible sections for horizontal resequencing. Map gameplay state to musical intensity, then schedule transitions on beats/bars or authored cues. Track tempo, meter, entry/exit points, transition segments and crossfade policy.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Randomly concatenating clips does not guarantee musical continuity. Composer terminology varies; describe the actual scheduling operation. A brief combat toggle should not cause constant musical restarting.

In **vertical layering**, synchronized stems share a musical timeline and the mix changes by adding or removing layers. In **horizontal resequencing**, the score moves to another compatible section at a musical boundary. A tempo map converts beat or bar positions into playback time as tempo changes. Request a transition immediately before and after a tempo change and verify the audible handoff, including any pickup or decay tail. [Kent Carter / Guy Jones — Composer interview on adaptive music](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/audio/composer-guy-jones-talks-adaptive-music-for-games)

## Checks

Proposed: trigger every state transition at multiple beat offsets, test rapid reversals and loading stalls, and listen for phase/cut artifacts.

## Evidence

- [Kent Carter / Guy Jones — Composer interview on adaptive music](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/audio/composer-guy-jones-talks-adaptive-music-for-games) — 2021 direct composer interview text; stems, sections and tempo maps. Embedded podcast not played. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.

===== knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-NOISE.md =====

---
id: K-GAME-TECH-NOISE

status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-19'
context:
  evidence_check: "External source text checked; source-specific limits appear in Evidence"
  application_status: "Reference research and proposed checks only; no engine implementation or performance validation"
---
# Generate coherent terrain with gradients and multi-scale noise

## Guidance

Use coherent noise for spatially continuous variation. Perlin noise computes gradient-dot products at surrounding lattice corners and blends them with smooth interpolation; adding the raw dot products is insufficient. Sum octaves with controlled frequency and amplitude for multi-scale detail. Derive biome fields independently from final mesh construction.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

Mojang's 1.18 development notes explicitly separate terrain shaping, biome distribution, noise caves and aquifers. Aquifers give underground regions local water levels; cave carving and noise fields can coexist. For a new generator, treat density d(x,y,z), climate/biome fields and fluid occupancy as separate inputs. Evaluate density including neighbor margins before meshing a chunk, and make cave/biome blending deterministic across boundaries. This is an implementation synthesis, not Mojang's exact density function.

## Applicability and exceptions

A heightfield cannot represent overhangs; density fields can. Exact Minecraft algorithms change by edition/version and are not established from this general reference.

In procedural terrain, an **octave** is one noise layer at a chosen frequency and amplitude. Fractal-noise sums often called fractional Brownian motion (fBm) combine several such layers. **Lacunarity** scales frequency between octaves and **persistence** scales amplitude. Keep the sampled world coordinates consistent across chunk edges. Test neighboring chunks independently, including distant levels of detail where high frequencies can alias. [libnoise — Perlin module parameters](https://libnoise.sourceforge.net/docs/classnoise_1_1module_1_1Perlin.html)

## Checks

Proposed: fix seeds, inspect chunk seams and derivatives, and validate biome transitions and reachable terrain.

# Add lighting detail without changing surface topology

## Guidance

Use normal textures when the lighting needs finer surface detail than the mesh. Decode stored vectors in the expected space, transform through a compatible tangent basis, and normalize before lighting. Keep the normal-map convention consistent across baking, import and rendering.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### tangent-space normal map

In a tangent-space normal map, the texture encodes directions relative to the mesh surface. A normal map convention includes the sign of the tangent-space Y direction. Godot expects OpenGL-style normals. Check a known raised feature under a moving light before changing mesh geometry or lighting.

## Applicability and exceptions

Ordinary normal mapping does not change silhouette, collision or actual surface height. A normal map on a 2D sprite needs a lighting pipeline that uses it.

## Checks

Proposed: rotate the light, mirror UVs and inspect seams; test compression and lower mip levels.

## Evidence

- [Godot — Standard Material 3D](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/stable/tutorials/3d/standard_material_3d.html) — Official stable documentation; normal map, transparency and billboard sections; rolling version. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V26 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

===== knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-PATH.md =====

title: Choose A-star or flow fields by query structure
summary: Choose search by graph costs, goal sharing and agent footprint. For scheduled multi-agent motion,
  model occupancy in time as well as space.
kind: reference
scope: Game development. For changing or shared routes, distinguish traversability from physical closure.
keywords: ["A*", "Dijkstra", "flow", "field", "navigation", "topology", "dynamic-obstacle", "door", "traversal", "reservation", "길찾기", "에이스타", "다익스트라", "플로필드", "bidirectional", "corridor", "radius", "clearance", "deadlock", "교행", "양방향", "반경", "통로", "교착", "경로장", "개체", "lifelong", "sustained", "inflow", "queue", "FIFO", "starvation", "overload", "throughput", "unlaned", "time-expanded", "지속", "유입", "개별", "무한", "대기", "차선", "없는", "지형", "시간", "확장", "예약", "과부하", "breadth-first search", "admissible heuristic", "consistent heuristic", "flow field", "time-expanded graph", "multi-agent path finding", "configuration space", "priority queue", "horizon pruning", "owned paths", "snapshot", "source destruction", "path order", "언리얼", "점유", "문 닫힘", "국소 회피"]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-11'
---
# Choose A-star or flow fields by query structure

## Guidance

Breadth-first search finds shortest paths on an unweighted graph. Dijkstra's algorithm expands lowest accumulated cost for nonnegative weights. A* orders by accumulated cost plus a heuristic. An admissible heuristic never overestimates remaining cost. If closed nodes cannot reopen, use a consistent heuristic for the usual graph-search optimality guarantee. For many agents sharing a goal, reverse search from that goal can produce a flow field of downhill choices. Respect directed edges, movement cost and each body's clearance.

## Applicability and exceptions

For changing or shared routes, distinguish traversability from physical closure. Block new routes first and drain existing traversals before closing a door. A time-expanded graph gives each state a location and time. Reserve vertex occupancy, opposing edges, footprint sweeps and final occupancy under a common clock. A bounded planning horizon can miss a route. Prune a corridor only when the graph proves that every feasible route crosses the relevant cut. In a priority queue, compact keys must preserve the full ordering, tie-breaks and range without overflow.

When a route is saved for replay or read on another thread, take an **owned snapshot** of each complete ordered path, including waits and the terminal state. A non-owning view into planner storage becomes invalid when its source is destroyed or reused. Publish only after copying the complete data. Destroy or mutate the source and compare every saved step and its order against the original. For an occupied door in Unreal, perform a separate gameplay check: update the route field for new entrants, preserve the traversing body's swept clearance until it exits, then close physical collision. A route field alone supplies no local avoidance. [C++ Core Guidelines — ownership and lifetime safety](https://isocpp.github.io/CppCoreGuidelines/CppCoreGuidelines#Rr-ptr)

## Checks

Check reachability and cost against an independent shortest-path solution, including directed, blocked, reopened and narrow edges. For reservations audit simultaneous sweeps, swaps and terminal occupancy. Include a safe deadlock and a valid detour as controls. Compare complete paths and timing at equal offered demand before adopting optimizations.

## Evidence

Patel explains single-goal search and reusable destination fields. LaValle covers configuration space and coordinating fixed paths. The cited multi-agent papers have distinct graph and scheduling assumptions. Prior Unity and Unreal grid observations covered selected closures, radius constraints and finite scheduled routes. They leave arbitrary continuous geometry, lifelong fairness and production capacity open.

- [Amit Patel — Introduction to A*](https://www.redblobgames.com/pathfinding/a-star/introduction.html)
- [Amit Patel — Flow Field Pathfinding for Tower Defense](https://www.redblobgames.com/pathfinding/tower-defense/)
- [Li et al., corridor symmetry](https://mapf.info/index.php/Main.SKoen20k)
- [LaValle footprint/C-space](https://lavalle.pl/planning/node161.html)
- [fixed-path coordination](https://lavalle.pl/planning/node325.html)
- [RHCR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2005.07371)
- [PIBT](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.11282)
- [online pickup/delivery](https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.10868)

# Design pixel clusters and palette storage deliberately

## Guidance

For readable low-resolution sprites, tune clusters, edge rhythm, palette and selective anti-aliasing at the actual display scale. Indexed colour stores palette indices; n colours need ceil(log2(n)) index bits in principle, subject to format constraints. Dithering trades spatial patterns for perceived intermediate tones.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### PLTE

An indexed-colour PNG stores palette indices in its image data. The required `PLTE` chunk holds the corresponding RGB entries. Inspect the importer’s chosen texture format to determine GPU memory use, since a small palette in the file does not require indexed GPU storage.

## Applicability and exceptions

Art conventions are stylistic choices, not universal bans. File size, decoded CPU memory and GPU texture format are different budgets. This reference does not certify a particular SNES sprite's storage layout.

## Checks

Proposed: preview integer and noninteger scaling, filtering and palette swaps; inspect runtime texture memory rather than PNG size.

## Evidence

- [W3C, PNG Specification (PLTE)](https://www.w3.org/TR/png-3/#11PLTE) — terminology and applicability of PLTE, indexed-colour PNG. This is source evidence. The suggested target checks remain to be run.

- [Derek Yu — Pixel Art Tutorial: Basics](https://www.derekyu.com/makegames/pixelart.html) — Creator tutorial; outlines, anti-aliasing and shading. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [W3C — PNG Specification, Third Edition](https://www.w3.org/TR/png/) — 2025 recommendation; indexed colour and PLTE; storage format, not GPU residency. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V35, V58 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

===== knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-RANDOM-DESIGN.md =====

id: K-GAME-TECH-RANDOM-DESIGN

status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: "External source text checked; source-specific limits appear in Evidence"
  application_status: "Reference research and proposed checks only; no engine implementation or performance validation"
---
# Control randomness by decision timing and streak distribution

## Guidance

Classify when uncertainty becomes known and how strongly it changes outcomes. A shuffle bag samples without replacement until refill; a pity system changes conditional odds after failures or guarantees an outcome. Compute P(first success=n)=p_n*product(1-p_i for i<n), then derive expected wait and tail probabilities.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Input randomness is not automatically good and output randomness not automatically bad; the cited designers disagree. Changing odds changes the process and must not be described as independent fixed-probability trials.

**Input randomness** reveals uncertain information before a player chooses an action. **Output randomness** resolves uncertainty after commitment. A new draw may therefore offer an adaptation opportunity in one decision and resolve risk in another. For a pity system, compute the chance of success conditional on all preceding misses and compare its wait-time distribution with the independent-draw baseline. Equal averages can conceal markedly different long waits. [Keith Burgun — Randomness and Game Design](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/randomness-and-game-design)

## Checks

Proposed: compare mean, percentiles and maximum droughts, inspect bag-boundary repeats, and playtest whether players understand their options.

## Evidence

- [Ian Schreiber — Probability and Randomness](https://gamebalanceconcepts.wordpress.com/2010/07/28/level-4-probability-and-randomness/) — 2010 designer course; probability distributions and expected values. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Keith Burgun — Randomness and Game Design](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/randomness-and-game-design) — Designer argument; input/output randomness. Normative position, not consensus. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [A Defense of Randomness in Competitive Games](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/a-defense-of-randomness-in-competitive-games) — Original design argument disagreeing with avoidance of output randomness. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V09, V44 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Select reflections by visibility and update requirements

## Guidance

For flat accurate mirrors, render a reflected camera view with clipping. For approximate surrounding light, use reflection probes/cubemaps; choose static capture or explicit update policy. SSR traces against available screen depth, so use fallback data for rays leaving the image. Combine methods by roughness, distance and importance.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Screen Space Reflections

Screen Space Reflections use data visible in the current view, which can cause reflections to disappear at the edge of the screen. Planar Reflections render the scene from a reflected viewpoint for a plane. Move a reflected object out of the main view and compare completeness and GPU time at the chosen quality settings.

## Applicability and exceptions

A mirrored duplicate scene is one historical trick, not the definition of planar reflection. Cubemaps may be updated dynamically. SSR cannot recover unseen geometry.

## Checks

Proposed: move a reflected object off-screen, change the camera angle, and compare static/dynamic captures and GPU pass costs.

## Evidence

- [Epic — Planar Reflections](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/planar-reflections-in-unreal-engine) — Official documentation; SSR comparison and rendering cost. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V16 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

# Distinguish affine texture transforms from perspective projection

## Guidance

Mathematical derivation: an affine 2D transform is p'=A*p+b and preserves parallelism. Perspective ground rendering needs scale varying with depth; changing affine parameters per scanline can approximate that view. Use for stylized flat-ground effects or historical rendering study.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

The bsnes renderer's renderMode7 loads signed matrix coefficients and centers/offsets, computes a scanline origin with fixed-point clipping/truncation, advances source coordinates by matrix increments, then resolves tile and palette samples plus out-of-range rules. This confirms a concrete raster implementation beyond the earlier README. Perspective-like ground requires the game to vary parameters across scanlines; a single affine mapping is insufficient. For faithful emulation, retain signed widths, intermediate truncation, wrap/clamp behavior and register-latch timing instead of substituting unrestricted floats.

### Mode 7

Mode 7 applies an affine transformation to a background. Per-scanline changes can suggest a horizon. Fixed-point arithmetic quantizes the coordinates, which matters when matching an emulator reference. Compare integer sampling at scanline boundaries before attributing a discrepancy to the projection model.

## Applicability and exceptions

Source-level affine sampling is now checked in bsnes. Hardware bus timing, register-latch fidelity and original Mario Kart per-scanline code are not verified by reading this renderer alone.

## Checks

Proposed: verify the general transform using a checkerboard, then consult emulator/hardware source before implementing authentic Mode 7.

## Evidence

- [Godot — Matrices and transforms](https://docs.godotengine.org/en/latest/tutorials/math/matrices_and_transforms.html) — Official tutorial; 2D translation/rotation/scale math; latest documentation, API version not pinned. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [DerKoun — bsnes-hd README](https://github.com/DerKoun/bsnes-hd/blob/master/README.md) — Emulator author's implementation description; HD Mode 7 and integer-math limits; no emulator run. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V57 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [bsnes — renderMode7 source](https://github.com/bsnes-emu/bsnes/blob/master/bsnes/sfc/ppu-fast/mode7.cpp) — renderMode7; public master source read 2026-11-07, no emulator/hardware run; original Mario Kart code and cycle-exact timing not established. Checked 2026-11-07.

---
# Separate reproducible randomness from security-sensitive randomness

## Guidance

A deterministic PRNG advances internal state into a reproducible sequence. Persist state or use stable independent streams for replay, generation and loot; cosmetic draws must not unexpectedly consume gameplay randomness. Choose a generator by period, quality, speed and reproducibility requirements.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

In pret's Emerald reconstruction, Random advances a 32-bit state by multiplication and addition, then returns its upper 16 bits; SeedRng assigns the state. The header identifies the recurrence s'=(1103515245*s+24691) mod 2^32. Thus an identical seed and identical count/order of calls reproduce this stream, not merely an identical elapsed wall-clock time. Instrument seed initialization and every caller before diagnosing a version-specific manipulation. Keep gameplay and cosmetic streams separate for new games.

## Applicability and exceptions

Predictability is implementation-dependent; not every modern game prevents RNG manipulation. General-purpose PCG is not a substitute for a cryptographically secure generator where secrecy matters.

A **linear congruential generator** (LCG) updates state by a multiply, add and reduction modulo the state range. The PCG family (Permuted Congruential Generator) applies an output transformation to a congruential state generator. For repeatable game simulation compare complete draw sequences under the same algorithm and stream parameters. If visual effects use their own streams, adding a cosmetic draw should leave gameplay draws unchanged. [O'Neill — The PCG Paper](https://www.pcg-random.org/paper.html)

## Checks

Proposed: replay from stored state, change cosmetic activity, check distribution and ensure independent streams remain independent in actual use.

## Evidence

- [Melissa O'Neill — PCG Random Number Generators](https://www.pcg-random.org/pdf/hmc-cs-2014-0905.pdf) — 2014 technical report; abstract, generator state and stream design. Not cryptographic certification. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Glenn Fiedler — Deterministic Lockstep](https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/) — 2014 author article; exact determinism and input synchronization. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V55 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [pret/pokeemerald — random.c](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pret/pokeemerald/refs/heads/master/src/random.c) — Random and SeedRng inspected; community reconstruction, not a vendor patch history. Checked 2026-11-07.
- [pret/pokeemerald — random.h](https://github.com/pret/pokeemerald/blob/master/include/random.h) — ISO_RANDOMIZE1 and declarations inspected; formula transcribed, no game build. Checked 2026-11-07.

# Restore and re-simulate state for rollback networking

## Guidance

Rollback networking predicts missing input, saves restorable simulation snapshots and re-simulates when the received input differs. Cancel or reconcile speculative audio, particles and UI. An external achievement, currency award or other irreversible effect must wait for verified eligibility. Give the confirmed effect an idempotency key that distinguishes session, event and recipient across replay and delivery retries.

## Applicability and exceptions

For a rollback simulation, restored state and input order must reproduce the agreed result within the replay budget. A peer protocol can agree on final inputs and verified state. An authoritative game delegates eligibility to its responsible authority. A display animation or elapsed delay cannot establish confirmation. Reliable cross-restart delivery also requires durable state and a compatible receiver contract.

## Checks

Inject wrong and out-of-order inputs and compare final state hashes. A predicted knockout corrected to a block must produce zero external awards. Replay and retry a confirmed award and expect one delivery effect. Test distinct same-tick events, recipients, lost acknowledgments and restart recovery.

## Evidence

GGPO describes prediction and replay. Photon Quantum distinguishes predicted and verified frames and documents cancellation of predicted events. Earlier synthetic event traces exposed a false award when deduplication was the only guard. They did not exercise network determinism or a durable reward service. External delivery is an engineering design requirement.

- [GGPO — Rollback Networking SDK](https://www.ggpo.net/)
- [Glenn Fiedler — Deterministic Lockstep](https://gafferongames.com/post/deterministic_lockstep/)
- [Photon Quantum 3 — Frames, “Which Frame To Read In The View”](https://doc.photonengine.com/quantum/v3/manual/frames)
- [Photon Quantum 2.1.9 — CallbackEventCanceled](https://doc-api.photonengine.com/en/quantum/v2/class_quantum_1_1_callback_event_canceled.html)

id: K-GAME-TECH-SAVE

kind: reference
scope: "Game development; engine-independent mechanisms with version-dependent engine examples"
keywords: ["save", "serialization", "checksum", "password", "bitflags", "저장", "세이브", "백업", "저장손상", "USaveGame", "UGameplayStatics::AsyncSaveGameToSlot", "save generation"]
status: active

  application_status: "Reference research and proposed checks only; no engine implementation or performance validation"
---
# Design versioned durable saves and compact password states

## Guidance

Serialize persistent state, stable object identifiers and schema version rather than raw runtime pointers. Pack independent flags into bits when useful. Password saves encode a small reconstructible state into a restricted alphabet with error detection. Proposed durability design: validate a new save before replacing the known-good slot and retain a recoverable generation.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

Emerald's public reconstruction writes sector groups using a save counter to select a slot and tracks the last known good write state; loading validates sector data and selects a valid generation. This is a historical reconstruction, not Nintendo-authored source or a tested local save system. For a new implementation, use generation IDs, payload validation and a documented commit/recovery order; do not assume two files alone are atomic. The original Simon's Quest reverse-engineering FAQ describes seven state bytes, a two-byte additive check and a scrambling byte, encoded into a 16-character password. Item/experience/day state is reconstructed, not an exact paused-world snapshot.

## Applicability and exceptions

A checksum detects some corruption; it is not tamper-proof authentication. Eight bits store eight independent yes/no flags, not hundreds of millions of independent events. Two slots alone do not prove atomic filesystem behavior.

For Unreal saves, `USaveGame` represents game-defined persistent data. `UGameplayStatics::AsyncSaveGameToSlot` serializes the object on the game thread, writes the platform data on a worker thread and reports success or failure via a completion delegate on the game thread. A scheduled operation still needs a completion check. Inject a failed write and verify the previous valid generation remains readable on the target platform. [Epic — UGameplayStatics::AsyncSaveGameToSlot](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/UGameplayStatics/AsyncSaveGameToSlot?application_version=5.8)

## Checks

Proposed: interrupt each write stage, corrupt payloads, load older schemas, and verify backups and object references; test on the target platform.

## Evidence

- [Epic — Saving and Loading Your Game](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/saving-and-loading-your-game-in-unreal-engine) — Official documentation; custom SaveGame and asynchronous saving. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [pret/pokered — save.asm](https://github.com/pret/pokered/blob/master/engine/menus/save.asm) — Community disassembly artifact, not Nintendo-authored source; inspected as historical implementation evidence, no ROM built. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V20, V54 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [pret/pokeemerald — save.c](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/pret/pokeemerald/refs/heads/master/src/save.c) — HandleWriteSector, save generation selection and validation text inspected; community reconstruction; no ROM build or fault injection. Checked 2026-11-07.
- [kingshriek — Simon's Quest Password System FAQ](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/nes/587179-castlevania-ii-simons-quest/faqs/38582) — Original reverse-engineering account, generation/decoding sections; exact ROM behavior not independently executed; do not copy apparent transcription errors as a tested decoder. Checked 2026-11-07.

# Choose shadow-map resolution and filtering

## Guidance

Use for moving lights or casters. Render light-space depth, then compare the receiver's light-space depth against it. CSM allocates directional-light coverage across camera-frustum intervals; PCF averages comparison results, not ordinary filtered depth. Tune split distances, receiver/caster bias, filter footprint and stabilization separately. Bake static lighting only when its assumptions remain valid.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### cascaded shadow maps

Cascaded shadow maps partition the camera frustum so directional shadows can use different texel densities over distance. Percentage-closer filtering averages the outcomes of depth comparisons. Inspect cascade transitions while moving the camera, then test filter softness at a fixed split arrangement.

## Applicability and exceptions

Acne, peter-panning, cascade seams and camera-motion shimmer are different failures. Historical Direct3D examples do not establish the best UE renderer configuration.

## Checks

Proposed: inspect grazing surfaces, thin geometry and a slow camera sweep; measure shadow-pass GPU time and memory per quality tier.

## Evidence

- [Microsoft — Cascaded Shadow Maps](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/dxtecharts/cascaded-shadow-maps) — Technical article; frustum partitioning and stability sections. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Bunnell & Pellacini — Shadow Map Antialiasing](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems/part-ii-lighting-and-shadows/chapter-11-shadow-map-antialiasing) — GPU Gems ch.11; sections 11.1–11.2. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V03 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.

# Model distance, occlusion and room response separately

## Guidance

Use attenuation curves for level, air-absorption filtering for timbre, spatialization for direction, and occlusion/reverb for obstruction and room response. Relative radial motion can drive Doppler pitch when appropriate. Author parameters around listener position, not necessarily the camera.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

One ray is only an occlusion approximation; full propagation/diffraction is another system. Pitch and filtering should preserve gameplay cue recognition. Doppler is a proposed physical transfer, not a verified setting in every cited game.

**Sound attenuation** sets level by source–listener distance. **Air absorption** varies spectral filtering with distance, while spatialization supplies directional cues. Under Unreal's occlusion settings, a blocking trace can control both attenuation and low-pass filtering. Vary distance with line of sight unchanged, then add a wall at fixed distance to isolate the two effects. [Epic — Sound Attenuation](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/sound-attenuation-in-unreal-engine)

## Checks

Proposed: compare headphones/speakers, open/closed doors, listener/source motion, room transitions and many simultaneous voices.

## Evidence

- [Epic — Sound Attenuation](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/sound-attenuation-in-unreal-engine) — Official documentation; attenuation, air absorption, occlusion and reverb sends. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V22 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Use speed effects without hiding actionable information

## Guidance

Coordinate FOV changes, camera offset/lag and velocity-based blur with acceleration events. Separate base framing from effects so changing visual intensity does not alter aiming or movement rules. Depth-based camera-motion blur reconstructs pixel motion from view transforms; moving objects need additional motion information.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

## Applicability and exceptions

Motion blur does not restore lost input responsiveness or actual frames. Large FOV shifts and forced camera motion may impair comfort and target reading.

The camera's **field of view (FOV)** is its angular coverage. Changing it alters perceived motion and framing. For camera motion blur, reconstruct a world position from screen depth and the inverse current view-projection matrix, then project it with the previous matrix to estimate screen displacement. Independently moving objects need their own motion vectors or a velocity buffer. Compare a moving camera with a stationary scene against a stationary camera and moving character. [Gilberto Rosado — Motion Blur as a Post-Processing Effect](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems3/part-iv-image-effects/chapter-27-motion-blur-post-processing-effect)

## Checks

Proposed: compare disabled/reduced/full effects in actual driving or traversal tasks, including accessibility preferences.

## Evidence

- [Gilberto Rosado — Motion Blur as a Post-Processing Effect](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems3/part-iv-image-effects/chapter-27-motion-blur-post-processing-effect) — GPU Gems 3 ch.27; depth reconstruction and velocity sampling. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Itay Keren — Scroll Back](https://www.gamedeveloper.com/design/scroll-back-the-theory-and-practice-of-cameras-in-side-scrollers) — 2015 creator-authored article; camera-window, framing and smoothing. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V06 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Model elemental interactions as explicit state rules

## Guidance

Proposed implementation pattern: represent material properties and elemental effects as data, specify transition conditions and precedence, and send resulting events to physics, audio and rendering. Prototype a small interaction matrix before expanding combinations. The original conceptual model is confirmed by the follow-up below; the proposed data architecture is not Nintendo source code.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

The original Nintendo/GDC slides distinguish movement simulation from state transitions. At 40:23 the model separates elemental effects from material objects; at 40:53 effects may alter material state, at 41:08 effects may alter other effects, and at 41:23 material-to-material contact alone does not cause a chemical state transition. This restriction belongs to that game's model, not physical chemistry. Implementation synthesis: collect contacts/exposures, evaluate a directed effect-to-state rule table, resolve simultaneous changes with explicit precedence, commit state, then emit physics/VFX/audio events. For burning wood, a material can emit an elemental fire effect; this preserves the separation instead of declaring that every material directly transforms another.

## Applicability and exceptions

The three conceptual rules are verified from original slides; data structures, scheduling and propagation algorithms below are proposed engineering synthesis. Exact Nintendo source code, update order and simulation coefficients are not public evidence here.

In Nintendo's **chemistry engine** presentation, an element can change a material state or affect another element. Material contact alone has no chemical transition in that model. An **interaction matrix** is a transferable representation: list influencing effect, receiving state, preconditions and resulting state for each directed interaction. Check simultaneous exposures in more than one processing order before choosing a precedence policy. [Nintendo/GDC — original technical presentation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QyMsF31NdNc&t=2423s)

## Checks

Proposed: test simultaneous fire/water/electric effects, event order, cycles, save/load and replicated authority; record unresolved combinations.

## Evidence

- [Nintendo — Breath of the Wild: Breaking Conventions](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=niIVdlavc7I) — Earlier pass: excerpt description only. The original full talk slides were subsequently checked at the timestamps below. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V51 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [Nintendo/GDC — original technical presentation](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=QyMsF31NdNc&t=2423s) — Original slides visually inspected at 40:23, 40:53, 41:08 and 41:23; transcript export unavailable; not a full-video viewing or source-code inspection. Checked 2026-11-07.

# Separate simulation steps from rendering and tune jump integration

## Guidance

An accumulator holds elapsed time not yet simulated. For each fixed step h that is due, update state and subtract h. Decide how to handle backlog when a per-frame catch-up limit is reached. Semi-implicit Euler updates velocity before position, while explicit Euler uses the old velocity. Keeping fractional position allows subpixel motion even on an integer display.

## Applicability and exceptions

For a bounded fixed-step model with residual time below h, interpolate the previous and current transforms with alpha=residual/h. This draws about one simulation tick behind the elapsed-time trajectory. Keep collisions and input on the simulation clock. Sustained overload requires an explicit time-loss, slowdown or capacity policy.

## Checks

Replay the same timestamped commands at different render cadences and through hitches. Reconcile offered time with simulated time, retained debt and discarded time. Compare apex and landing for each flight. A near-floor tolerance must also consider descent before treating a tiny ascent as a landing.

## Evidence

Fiedler derives accumulator scheduling and integrator behavior. Thorson documents fractional movement in an integer collision model. Earlier scripted movement checks covered finite hitch recovery and display interpolation. They leave engine-wide physics determinism, presentation-complete recovery time and network replay untested.

- [Glenn Fiedler — Fix Your Timestep!](https://gafferongames.com/post/fix_your_timestep/)
- [Glenn Fiedler — Integration Basics](https://gafferongames.com/post/integration_basics/)
- [Maddy Thorson — Celeste & TowerFall Physics](https://www.mattmakesgames.com/articles/celeste_and_towerfall_physics/index.html)

# Combine distance, angle and collision queries for perception

## Guidance

For unit forward f and unit direction d to the sample, `dot(f,d) >= cos(halfFOV)` includes the cone boundary. Test squared range first and handle a coincident point before normalizing. A horizontal cone projects both vectors onto the ground plane, whereas a 3D cone also limits elevation. The Lambert cosine term in diffuse lighting uses a surface normal and light direction and answers a different question.

## Applicability and exceptions

For collision-based Unreal perception, `UWorld::LineTraceSingleByChannel` reports the first blocking hit on a channel. Configure `FCollisionQueryParams::AddIgnoredActor` to skip the observer. A trace that expects to hit the target requires its component to block that channel. A clear-to-point test instead allows a clear segment even if the endpoint has no collider.

## Checks

Place a target on the cone edge, directly above, behind a thin wall and partly exposed. Test the observer's own collider, no-hit results, target replacement and multiple sample points. Cross-check angular boundary values against known directions.

## Evidence

Epic's EQS documentation describes Distance, Dot and Trace tests. Earlier Unreal checks covered one known target point and simple first-hit visibility. Multi-point actors, animated meshes and gameplay perception remain separate questions.

- [Epic — EQS Node Reference: Tests](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/eqs-node-reference-tests-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US)
- [Epic — Mover Grappling Hook Movement Mode](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/mover-03-grappling-hook-movement-mode?lang=en-US)
- [Epic — UWorld](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/UWorld?application_version=5.8)
- [Epic — AddIgnoredActor](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/FCollisionQueryParams/AddIgnoredActor?lang=en-US)

# Separate volumetric appearance from simulated smoke gameplay

## Guidance

Render participating media using a density field with scattering/extinction; use noise to shape clouds or smoke, then sample light and transmittance. Low-resolution evaluation with temporal reconstruction is a quality/performance tradeoff. For competitive smoke, share authoritative density/visibility rules rather than relying on each client's final image.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Implementation follow-up — 2026-11-07

Valve's 2023 CS2 patch notes explicitly mention smoke-volume expansion near walls, changes to the stepping function on AMD hardware, and shotgun-created holes. These support an evolving spatial smoke volume, not a published complete solver. Implementation synthesis: separate authoritative smoke occupancy and temporary bullet/grenade perturbations from per-view lighting, then render the shared state with platform-appropriate sampling. Geometry intersection and reconstruction must preserve gameplay visibility across quality levels.

### Volumetric Fog

Unreal's Volumetric Fog uses temporal reprojection to reuse fog samples across frames. Rapidly changing lights can leave trails. Compare moving and static lights, then vary the light's `Volumetric Scattering Intensity` to isolate its contribution to fog from its lighting of opaque surfaces.

## Applicability and exceptions

Valve's dated patch notes confirm smoke-volume behavior; its complete density solver, replication and ray-marching code remain undisclosed. Volumetric appearance is distinct from fluid simulation and equal competitive visibility still needs target-game tests.

## Checks

Proposed: test moving lights, camera cuts, inside-volume views, depth edges and visibility agreement under different quality settings.

## Evidence

- [Epic — Volumetric Fog](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-fog-in-unreal-engine) — Density, scattering, temporal reprojection and performance sections. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [Shaker, Togelius & Nelson — Fractals, Noise and Agents](https://www.pcgbook.com/chapter04.pdf) — 2016 author chapter; terrain and multi-scale noise. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- [u/smilefr — Optimized volumetric clouds](https://www.reddit.com/r/Unity3D/comments/1u8g138/i_finally_have_optimzied_volumetric_clouds/) — Original implementation account; quarter-resolution compute rendering and depth-edge upsample. Self-reported, no benchmark reproduced. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V10, V27 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). The originating short is a discovery lead, not independent proof.


### Follow-up evidence

- [Valve — Counter-Strike 2 June 2023 update notes](https://store.steampowered.com/news/posts/?appids=730&enddate=1689630041&feed=steam_announce%2F1000) — Official dated notes, VOLUMETRIC SMOKE sections; source code and cross-client determinism not disclosed. Checked 2026-11-07.

# Extract and stream editable voxel surfaces

## Guidance

Use voxel fields when editable volume matters. Separate storage, surface extraction, collision and streaming. Dual contouring uses signed samples plus edge crossings/normals (Hermite data) and a quadratic-error fit; density values alone are not its complete input. Rebuild only affected regions and synchronize border samples.

Implementation selection and checks are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### dual contouring

Dual contouring uses Hermite data at surface crossings: intersection positions and normals. A quadratic error function (QEF) scores candidate vertices by their fit to those local planes. Check normals and shared boundary samples across chunks when evaluating sharp-feature preservation or seam cracks.

## Applicability and exceptions

Block-face meshing and smooth contour extraction solve different art needs. Fine grids increase memory and rebuild work; seams, topology and collision lag remain engineering problems.

## Checks

Proposed: edit exactly on chunk borders, remove thin features and stream while moving; check visual/collision agreement.

## Evidence

- [Ju et al. — Dual Contouring of Hermite Data](https://www.cs.rice.edu/~jwarren/papers/dualcontour.pdf) — 2002 paper; abstract, Hermite input and QEF method. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.
- Originating channel shorts: V21 in [the coverage guide](K-GAME-TECH-GUIDE.md). Originating shorts are discovery leads, not independent proof.

# Use summed waves for water surfaces with explicit limits

## Guidance

Represent height as a sum of waves with amplitude, wavelength, direction and phase. Gerstner waves also move surface points horizontally, producing sharper crests. Compute normals from the same displacement field and share a compatible surface query with buoyancy gameplay.

Implementation selection and checks below are engineering synthesis, not a promise of a source game's exact implementation.

### Gerstner waves

Gerstner waves include horizontal displacement, so a displaced vertex's original horizontal coordinates may differ from its rendered location. For buoyancy, compare the gameplay query and visible water height at the same world-space position and simulation time, especially near steep crests.

## Applicability and exceptions

Steepness must be bounded to avoid folding. Wave sums are not full fluid simulation and do not automatically handle breaking waves, shore interaction or flow around obstacles.

## Checks

Proposed: inspect overhead repetition, shallow coastlines, floating objects and different simulation rates.

## Evidence

- [Mark Finch — Effective Water Simulation from Physical Models](https://developer.nvidia.com/gpugems/gpugems/part-i-natural-effects/chapter-1-effective-water-simulation-physical-models) — GPU Gems ch.1; sum of sines and Gerstner waves. Checked 2026-11-07, except items explicitly marked further reading.

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-ABSTRACTION-COST.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-ABSTRACTION-COST
title: Introduce abstractions when they reduce total reasoning cost
summary: Evaluate an abstraction by complexity hidden and changes simplified relative to its interface, indirection, and maintenance costs; similar code alone is insufficient evidence.
kind: reference
scope: Choosing helpers, shared modules, generalization, and refactoring in maintained software; design heuristics rather than universal size or timing rules
keywords: [abstraction cost, deep module, YAGNI, DRY, premature generalization, semantic duplication, refactoring, 추상화 비용, 깊은 모듈, 중복, 성급한 일반화, 리팩터링]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Ousterhout author-hosted course notes and Fowler original essays inspected on 2026-11-07
  application_status: Reference synthesis only; proposed review checks have no demonstrated local productivity or runtime result
---

# Introduce abstractions when they reduce total reasoning cost

## Guidance

Source observation: Ousterhout favors rich functionality behind a simple interface and flags pass-through layers; his generality advice remains tied to present capabilities. Fowler's YAGNI rejects speculative features while allowing refactoring. His Design Stamina essay explicitly labels the productivity payoff a hypothesis without objective proof.

Synthesis: compare what callers must understand before and after extraction. Weigh a stable concept, owned rule, or hidden decision against added contracts, navigation, configuration, indirect control flow, debugging, and compatibility. Moving lines can simply relocate complexity.

Distinguish semantic duplication from coincidental resemblance. Sharing one rule that must change together can prevent divergence; merging independently evolving rules because their syntax resembles each other can couple unrelated changes. This is a proposed criterion, not an empirical finding from these sources. Temporary duplication can preserve freedom while the domain becomes clearer.

Choose the smallest useful generalization of current needs: perhaps a local helper, concrete module, or standard interface. A deep module can contain substantial implementation work if its contract simplifies clients. Depth does not justify unrelated responsibilities or opaque behavior.

## Applicability and exceptions

Apply when extracting repeated code, introducing layers, or designing reusable APIs. Repetition count is a clue, not a universal threshold: neither two implementations nor three copies automatically establishes the right boundary. A single complex integration may warrant abstraction immediately; several similar calculations may deserve separate ownership.

Public APIs, compatibility commitments, expensive migrations, and known variation can justify earlier design. YAGNI allows maintaining changeability. Anticipated reuse alone cannot establish value. These authors emphasize generality differently and provide judgment, not universal size targets, investment formulas, or measured payoff dates.

## Checks

Proposed design review checks:

- Show current callers before and after; list decisions and guarantees each must understand.
- Name the shared rule and why its clients should change together; separate merely similar operations.
- Count introduced concepts and configuration branches as well as deleted lines.
- Walk through a plausible new case and a divergent case; check whether the abstraction stays coherent.
- Reconsider pass-through layers that provide no policy, isolation, or meaningful contract; verify real behavior after refactoring.

## Evidence

- [Ousterhout, Modular Design](https://web.stanford.edu/~ouster/cgi-bin/cs190-winter18/lecture.php?topic=modularDesign), “Classes Should be Deep,” “Generic Classes are Deeper,” and “New Layer, New Abstraction”; author-hosted 2018 notes inspected, not the complete book.
- [Fowler, Yagni](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/Yagni.html), speculative-feature costs and refactoring clarification; 2015 essay inspected.
- [Fowler, Design Stamina Hypothesis](https://martinfowler.com/bliki/DesignStaminaHypothesis.html), pseudo-graph, hypothesis limitation, and design-payoff discussion; 2007 essay inspected. No local implementation was evaluated.

# Make interface obligations and guarantees explicit before replacing a dependency

## Guidance

Before using or replacing an interface, write down what callers must provide and what they may rely on. Proposed contract dimensions are valid inputs and state, result meaning and units, state changes, failure behavior, ownership and lifetime, and relevant ordering or concurrency constraints. Mark unspecified behavior explicitly; an implementation's current habit is not automatically a promise.

Separate preconditions from postconditions. A precondition states what must hold before a call, such as a usable resource or an allowed range. A postcondition describes the result and state after successful completion. Error semantics must also say what remains valid after failure: whether work was partial, state changed, cleanup is required, or retry is safe. An invariant describes a condition that must remain true at the stated observation boundary.

Document the caller's obligations and the provider's guarantees where users encounter the interface. Types can encode some distinctions; validation, documentation and examples cover others. Prefer the smallest useful contract over a duplicate account of implementation internals.

## Applicability and exceptions

Use this comparison for wrappers, library upgrades, mocks, alternate providers and internal refactoring across a module boundary. A shared signature cannot settle semantic compatibility. Resource ownership matters even with automatic memory management: files, locks, subscriptions and asynchronous tasks have useful lifetimes distinct from object reachability.

Not every interface needs every dimension. A pure calculation may have no cleanup obligation. Timing or allocation details belong in the contract only when callers legitimately depend on them. Version-specific examples below illustrate the method; they do not impose Python or Rust semantics on another language.

## Checks

Proposed checks, requiring local application:

- List one normal case, one boundary case and each consequential failure or lifetime transition. State caller obligations and observable guarantees separately.
- Compare old and new providers against those cases, including partial completion and cleanup. Resolve ambiguous guarantees before migration.
- Choose targeted type checks, contract tests or inspection for the identified risk. Record the dependency version and any unverified dimensions.

## Evidence

Source observations, checked 2026-11-07:

- [Python io documentation](https://docs.python.org/3.14/library/io.html), “Overview,” “IOBase,” and “Raw File I/O”: documents data-type errors, context-manager closure and possible partial raw writes. Official English Python 3.14 reference; relevant sections accessible. No Python execution was performed.
- [Rust Vec documentation](https://doc.rust-lang.org/std/vec/struct.Vec.html#guarantees), “Guarantees” and `with_capacity`: distinguishes guaranteed capacity behavior from an unspecified growth strategy. Official English std reference, served as 1.99.1; relevant sections accessible. No Rust build or compatibility test was performed.
