# Apply coding principles through concrete contracts and change risks

## Guidance

Before design, review or refactoring, identify the changed behavior, intended maintainers, likely change, language/runtime/version, callers and constraints. Search the [code catalog](index.md) by that question; start with up to three matching details and expand for a concrete dependency. Preserve the project's established contracts and useful conventions.

- For understanding and modification, combine [readability](K-CODE-READABILITY.md), [maintenance evidence](K-CODE-MAINTAINABILITY-EVIDENCE.md), and [behavior-preserving change checks](K-CODE-CHANGE-TESTS.md) as needed.
- For structure, distinguish [information hiding](K-CODE-INFORMATION-HIDING.md), [dependency direction](K-CODE-DEPENDENCY-DIRECTION.md), [external dependency choice](K-CODE-DEPENDENCY-SELECTION.md), and [abstraction cost](K-CODE-ABSTRACTION-COST.md).
- For values and state, consult [configuration policy](K-CODE-CONFIGURATION-POLICY.md), [data invariants](K-CODE-DATA-INVARIANTS.md), and [API contracts](K-CODE-API-CONTRACTS.md).
- For lifetimes and cost, distinguish [resource ownership](K-CODE-RESOURCE-OWNERSHIP.md), [GC retention and cleanup](K-CODE-GC-LIFETIMES.md), [concurrent/asynchronous lifetimes](K-CODE-CONCURRENCY-LIFETIMES.md), and [performance evidence](K-CODE-PERFORMANCE-MEASUREMENT.md).

Carry the applicable action, source/locator, conditions, tradeoff and proposed check into the existing work brief. A code example, guideline, empirical association and locally reproduced result support different claims. Read the original when the summary cannot substantiate a consequential detail. Record a hypothesis as uncertain rather than converting repeated advice into a demonstrated safeguard.

## Applicability and exceptions

These references support judgment across contexts; they do not impose one language's semantics or one author's style everywhere. Fewer imports do not by themselves prove lower coupling; more interfaces do not prove modularity; moving every constant into configuration does not prove flexibility; GC and smart pointers do not establish all cleanup or concurrency guarantees.

When principles conflict, identify the concrete contract and cost. Compare the smallest viable options under the actual change and lifetime risks. Small local work need not acquire new abstractions, configuration files, experiments or a large review template. A temporary prototype and a long-lived shared API can justify different choices without silently weakening required correctness.

## Checks

- Does the selected advice match the actual language, engine, resource owner, deployment and team context?
- What observable change, misunderstanding or failure would the proposal prevent, and at what cost?
- Which callers, states, build inputs, configuration paths and cleanup obligations are affected?
- Are empirical study limits, untested assumptions, and evidence invalidated by the change visible?
- Do verification steps challenge actual behavior rather than count lines, interfaces or assertions as success?

Use the [code method](../../skills/vegabond-code/SKILL.md) and [design method](../../skills/vegabond-code-design/SKILL.md) for execution and verification. Knowledge validation establishes storage and retrieval, not program correctness, race freedom, memory safety, performance or developer productivity.

## Evidence

- User request in the current chat on 2026-11-07 to collect programming philosophy covering maintainability, readability, dependency minimization, modularity, hardcoding and memory management, within the ongoing knowledge-capture work.
- The [knowledge procedure](../../skills/vegabond-knowledge/SKILL.md) governs scoped retrieval, source boundaries and authorized maintenance. Individual references retain inspected original sources, publication context and proposed-check limits.
- This is a retrieval convention and synthesis, not a new software study, a completed refactor or evidence that a safeguard is enforced.

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-READABILITY.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-READABILITY
title: Review readability through a concrete maintenance question
summary: Judge readability by whether a reader can explain behavior and safely locate a likely change. Treat size, naming and comment metrics as prompts for inspection rather than universal acceptance thresholds.
kind: reference
scope: Source-code review and maintenance; language and team conventions must be checked locally
keywords: [readability, maintainability, code review, comprehension, naming, comments, cognitive load, 가독성, 유지보수성, 이름짓기, 주석]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: Primary English guidance and selected full-text research sections inspected online
  application_status: Proposed review checks; no implementation or reader study performed in OneStep
---

# Review readability through a concrete maintenance question

## Guidance

Use a plausible maintenance task to judge a passage: explain its result, identify its assumptions, and locate the decision that would change for a new requirement. Readability concerns the reader's successful understanding. A shorter expression can conceal state changes; a longer explanation can remove an otherwise necessary investigation.

Proposed practice: make domain distinctions visible in names and control flow. Choose names that distinguish nearby concepts, state units when confusion is plausible, and keep a name's meaning consistent across its scope. Introduce an intermediate value when it gives a significant condition or calculation a useful meaning. Remove indirection when following it adds no useful concept or independent responsibility.

Use comments for information the code cannot communicate economically: a constraint's origin, a surprising tradeoff, or a compatibility reason. Check that the explanation remains true after a change. A comment that merely repeats an operation adds another statement to maintain. Neither comment count nor identifier length establishes understanding.

## Applicability and exceptions

Apply this review to code that someone must inspect or modify. The relevant reader may need domain training; an unfamiliar algorithm is not automatically poorly written. Generated output, mathematical notation, performance-sensitive loops and framework conventions can justify different presentation choices. Check their generating source, explanation or measured constraints instead of mechanically expanding everything.

Do not derive universal function lengths, naming lengths or formatting thresholds from a readability model. Correlation with defect indicators does not establish that editing a metric causes fewer defects.

## Checks

These are proposed checks, not demonstrated safeguards:

- Trace one normal path and one important exceptional path; state the resulting values and side effects.
- Locate the rule for one plausible change. Record any hidden assumption, misleading name or unnecessary cross-file jump that blocked the explanation.
- Compare a candidate revision using that same question. Accept extra lines or a retained comment when they reduce the actual ambiguity.

## Evidence

Source observations, checked 2026-11-07:

- [Google Engineering Practices](https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html), “Complexity,” “Naming,” and “Comments”: review guidance prioritizes understandable code and useful explanation. English, undated living organizational guidance; relevant sections accessible, not an experiment.
- [Buse and Weimer, Learning a Metric for Code Readability](https://web.eecs.umich.edu/~weimerw/p/weimer-tse2010-readability-preprint.pdf), 2010 author-hosted English preprint, sections 2, 5–7: Java-snippet ratings yield predictive associations; the model is descriptive, with participant limitations. Selected full-text sections were accessible; this does not establish a universal metric or causal intervention.

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-RESOURCE-OWNERSHIP.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-RESOURCE-OWNERSHIP
title: Make resource ownership and borrowing explicit at module boundaries
summary: Give each resource an identifiable cleanup owner, express transfers and borrowing in interfaces, and introduce shared ownership only when independent lifetimes require it. C++ RAII, Rust ownership, and Unreal UObject management impose different contracts.
kind: reference
scope: Resource and interface design in C++, Rust, and Unreal Engine; source-based comparison rather than a universal language guarantee
keywords: [ownership, borrowing, RAII, unique_ptr, shared_ptr, weak_ptr, Rust, Drop, Unreal Engine, UObject, 소유권, 빌림, 자원 수명, 순환 참조, "std::unique_ptr", "std::shared_ptr", "std::weak_ptr", "std::weak_ptr::lock", "TSharedPtr", "TUniquePtr"]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-07'
context:
  evidence_check: 'Primary documentation inspected on 2026-11-07; mutable online editions, including Unreal Engine 5.8 documentation'
  application_status: 'Reference synthesis; proposed review checks, with no local implementation or runtime verification'
---

# Make resource ownership and borrowing explicit at module boundaries

## Guidance

Start an interface review by asking who releases each resource, who may temporarily use it, and what event ends that permission. Include files, sockets, locks, and native handles alongside heap memory. Prefer a lifetime contract that a maintainer can understand without tracing every caller.

In C++, the Core Guidelines recommend RAII wrappers for paired acquisition/release and unique ownership unless sharing is needed. Ordinary scope exit and exception unwinding release correctly constructed automatic wrappers; this does not establish asynchronous completion. `std::unique_ptr` transfers exclusive ownership by moving. `std::shared_ptr` shares ownership, so cycles of strong owners can prevent reclamation. A `std::weak_ptr` observes a shared object without keeping it alive; `lock()` obtains temporary shared ownership if it is still available.

Rust makes ownership moves and borrowing visible in types. The Book's `String` example releases its allocation through `drop` at the owner's scope boundary. References borrow rather than own; safe reference rules constrain validity and simultaneous mutable access. These mechanisms help local reasoning, but do not make every resource protocol automatic.

Unreal's `TSharedPtr`/`TUniquePtr` family manages ordinary C++ objects: `TSharedPtr` shares ownership and `TUniquePtr` assigns it to a single owner. Epic explicitly separates this library from `UObject` management. Select the engine's object-reference mechanism for `UObject` lifetimes instead of transferring deletion to an ordinary smart pointer.

## Applicability and exceptions

Use this comparison when reviewing ownership-heavy APIs or moving between these ecosystems. A borrowed view can still become invalid if its contract is violated. Legacy raw pointers may encode ownership despite a recommended convention; inspect their actual contract. Custom allocators, arenas, foreign interfaces, and framework-managed lifetimes need their own release rules. RAII does not promise cleanup after abrupt termination, or prove thread safety.

## Checks

Proposed review checks:

- Trace one acquisition through success, early return, failure, and transfer; identify exactly one cleanup responsibility for each owning path.
- Inspect returned views, callbacks, and stored references against their backing owner's lifetime. Explain each shared owner and inspect strong cycles.
- Confirm whether each Unreal target is a `UObject` before selecting its pointer family. Test cleanup behavior in the actual project before claiming prevention.

## Evidence

Source observations checked on 2026-11-07:

- [C++ Core Guidelines](https://isocpp.github.io/CppCoreGuidelines/CppCoreGuidelines): R.1, R.21, R.24; recommendations, not a compiler-enforced ownership system.
- [C++ working draft, smart pointers](https://eel.is/c++draft/smartptr), unique, shared and weak ownership semantics.
- [Rust Book: ownership](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch04-01-what-is-ownership.html): Ownership Rules and Memory and Allocation; live Book edition.
- [Rust Book: references](https://doc.rust-lang.org/book/ch04-02-references-and-borrowing.html): Mutable References and Dangling References; safe-reference examples.
- [Epic: smart pointers](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/smart-pointers-in-unreal-engine): introduction and Tips and Limitations; served as UE 5.8.

Pages were readable. No project cleanup scenario or sanitizer run was executed; checks above remain proposed.

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-ASSET-EXPORT-LAYOUT.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-ASSET-EXPORT-LAYOUT
title: Where an Unreal package export's tagged properties start when reading it outside the engine
summary: In versioned UE 5 packages at object version 1010 or later, seek to the export's script serialization start, and treat a start and end of 0 as "no tagged properties". Older or unversioned packages need per-class rules, such as the import data JSON written before the tags.
kind: reference
scope: Standalone readers of uncooked Unreal Engine 5 packages (.uasset, .umap) that parse export data without the engine
keywords: [Unreal Engine, uasset, package format, export, tagged properties, ScriptSerializationStartOffset, ScriptSerializationEndOffset, SCRIPT_SERIALIZATION_OFFSET, UAssetImportData, URigVMLink, UInterchangeBaseNode, SerializeDefaultObject, class default object, CDO, sparse class data, 언리얼 에셋, 패키지 형식, 직렬화, "FObjectExport", "FObjectExport::ScriptSerializationStartOffset", "FObjectExport::ScriptSerializationEndOffset", "EUnrealEngineObjectUE5Version::SCRIPT_SERIALIZATION_OFFSET", "UAssetImportData::Serialize", "UClass::SerializeDefaultObject", "SerialOffset", "SerialSize"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-10"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.8 stock serialization
  package: uncooked, versioned properties, object versions 1004 to 1018
---

## Guidance

An `FObjectExport` describes one exported object. Locate its tagged property range in the archive before interpreting object-specific bytes.

1. **Versioned package, object version 1010 or later** (`EUnrealEngineObjectUE5Version::SCRIPT_SERIALIZATION_OFFSET`): the export table entry holds `FObjectExport::ScriptSerializationStartOffset` and `FObjectExport::ScriptSerializationEndOffset`, relative to its `SerialOffset`. Seek to the start. If both are 0, the class wrote no tagged properties, because the saver records the range only when it writes tags (`Class.cpp`, `LinkerSave`). Do not read tags in that case.
2. **Older or unversioned package:** apply class rules.
   - `UAssetImportData` and its subclasses (`Fbx*ImportData`, `InterchangeAssetImportData`, `SpeedTreeImportData`, `AbcAssetImportData`, every `Datasmith*ImportData`) write an FString of source-file JSON before `Super::Serialize`, unless the package is `FilterEditorOnly` (`UAssetImportData::Serialize` in `AssetImportData.cpp`). The class default object does not.
   - `URigVMLink` (two FStrings) and `UInterchangeBaseNode` subclasses (attribute storage) do not call `UObject::Serialize`, so they have no tags.
3. **Class default object:** saved with `UClass::SerializeDefaultObject`, so no object GUID follows the tags. A Blueprint class's CDO is followed by sparse class data: at UE5Main custom version 33 or later, a struct PackageIndex, then that struct's tagged stream if it is not null (`BlueprintGeneratedClass.cpp`).

## Applicability and exceptions

Checked against stock UE 5.8 source and one project's editor content with object versions 1004 to 1018. Other engine versions, engine forks that change serialization, and cooked or unversioned packages are untested; step 1 does not apply to unversioned packages. Further classes with a custom `Serialize` that writes before `Super::Serialize`, or skips it, can exist; a sweep finds them.

## Checks

- Sweep every export of a large content folder and count the exports read exactly to their end, grouped by class. A class that fails consistently is the signal of another custom `Serialize`.
- Check that the offsets are ordered and fit within the export's `SerialSize` before seeking. Check that each import data JSON string starts with `[` or `{`.

## Evidence

Historical UE 5.8 source reading on 2026-11-10 covered `AssetImportData.cpp`, `RigVMLink.cpp`, `InterchangeBaseNode.cpp`, `Class.cpp` and `BlueprintGeneratedClass.cpp`. A read-only sweep of uncooked editor content supported the range and class rules. Pre-1012 RigHierarchy and RigVM exports retained unsupported custom layouts. Cooked, Zen/IoStore and other engine versions were not tested. Epic documents the offset origin in the FObjectExport fields. [Epic, FObjectExport](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/CoreUObject/FObjectExport?application_version=5.8)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-ENGINE-CHECKOUT.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-ENGINE-CHECKOUT
title: Confirm which engine source checkout is current before reading a custom Unreal engine
summary: An engine path environment variable can point at a stale checkout whose active source lives in a git worktree; check worktrees, branch and Build.version before drawing conclusions from engine source or history.
kind: lesson
scope: Projects built on a source-built or forked Unreal Engine located through an environment variable or registry association
keywords: [Unreal Engine, custom engine, engine fork, git worktree, Build.version, EngineAssociation, engine source, stale checkout]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-06"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5 source build
  vcs: git with worktrees
---

## Guidance

Before reading engine source, engine history or engine modification markers, confirm which checkout holds the engine version the project actually uses:

1. Run `git worktree list` in the path the environment variable names. Another worktree may hold the release branch.
2. Check that `Engine/Build/Build.version` exists and that its major and minor version match the version the project documents.
3. Check `git status` and the branch. An unexpected branch or mass of staged deletions is a reason to verify the path against the build inputs.
4. When the project names the engine by a registry GUID (`EngineAssociation` in the `.uproject`), resolve the GUID to a path before assuming the environment variable is the build input.

Only then search the engine for modification markers or renderer changes.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies to any project that resolves a custom engine by path or by registry association. It does not apply to a launcher-installed engine, which has no git history to misread. A worktree layout is one cause; other causes include several engine clones for different releases side by side.

## Checks

- `git worktree list` lists more than one path: decide which one is the release branch before reading.
- `Engine/Build/Build.version` is missing: the checkout is not a usable engine tree.
- The editor executable that the project's run scripts build from the environment variable does not exist: fix the variable before the first run.
- The `.uproject` `EngineAssociation` GUID is missing from `HKCU\Software\Epic Games\Unreal Engine\Builds`: the current user's association cannot be confirmed there.
- Before running a registration script that removes other GUIDs pointing at the same engine path, list the `EngineAssociation` of every local `.uproject`. A checkout whose `.uproject` was locally rewritten to an auto-generated GUID loses its engine association when that GUID is removed. A lower-impact alternative is to add the project GUID without removing others and to set the engine variable at user scope; both are reversible without administrator rights.
- History depth alone is inconclusive in a shallow clone. Compare the commit, branch and executable selected by the actual build or launch command.
- Restrict engine modification searches to the relevant modules or use `git log -- <paths>`, especially on large Windows checkouts.

## Evidence

A Windows UE 5.8 source-build investigation on 2026-11-05–06 found an environment variable selecting an incomplete checkout while a linked worktree contained the engine version and editor executable. The project association and per-user registration also disagreed. Inspecting those inputs corrected a source-history conclusion. Registration and launch were not retested end to end. [Git worktree manual](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-LOC-MERGE.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-LOC-MERGE
title: Merge conflicting Unreal localization manifest and archive files semantically and recompile locres with a parity proof
summary: Flatten Game.manifest and each Game.archive to (namespace path, key) entries, three-way merge the entries, write the result in the kept side's own format, then recompile Game.locres with the GatherText commandlet only after proving that the same engine reproduces the kept side's committed locres byte for byte.
kind: method
scope: Unreal Engine projects whose Content/Localization/<target>/ manifest, archive and locres files conflict in a git merge between two long-lived branches
keywords: [Unreal Engine, localization, Game.manifest, Game.archive, Game.locres, Game.po, GatherText, GenerateTextLocalizationResource, Game_Compile.ini, UTF-16LE, three-way merge, semantic merge, locres parity, "localization manifest", "localization archive", "Portable Object", "localization resource"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-06"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.3 project data compiled with a 5.8 source build
  platform: Windows desktop
  format: manifest and archive are UTF-16LE BOM JSON with tab indentation and LF
---

## Guidance

A localization manifest contains gathered source text and identity contexts. Each culture archive records translations; PO (Portable Object) exchanges text with translators and a locres file stores compiled runtime resources.

1. Extract the three stages of every conflicted manifest and archive with `git show <base>:<path>`, `git show <ours>:<path>` and `git show <theirs>:<path>` into a scratch tree. Do not resolve them as text: the files are large JSON trees and git's line merge produces duplicate or broken namespaces.
2. Parse each file as UTF-16LE JSON after the 2-byte BOM. Reject a duplicate identity rather than overwriting its entry. For this schema, flatten to a map keyed by (tuple of nested `Namespace` values, `Key`). In the manifest the value is the `Source` object plus the key's other fields; in an archive it is the child entry without `Key`.
3. Three-way merge the maps: equal on both sides keeps; changed on one side only takes that side (add, modify or delete); changed differently on both sides is a conflict to list for a person, kept as ours meanwhile.
4. Apply the merged map as a delta onto a deep copy of the kept side's tree. Insert new entries in the order the tree already uses (detect whether children are sorted by source text and keys by key before inserting) and remove empty `Children` or `Subnamespaces` arrays. Flatten the result again and assert it equals the merged map.
5. Serialize with the kept side's format: BOM, `json.dumps(ensure_ascii=False, indent='\t')`, LF, and a trailing newline if the kept side has one. Prove the serializer first: parse and re-serialize each input and compare bytes. A side that differs only by a missing trailing newline is still safe to merge.
6. Recompile every `Game.locres` instead of choosing a side. Make a dummy project: a minimal `.uproject`, the project's `Config/Localization/<target>_Compile.ini`, and `Content/Localization/<target>/` holding the merged manifest and archives. Run `UnrealEditor-Cmd.exe <dummy>.uproject -run=GatherText -config=Config/Localization/<target>_Compile.ini -unattended -nullrhi -nosplash -NoShaderCompile`.
7. Prove parity before trusting the output: compile the kept side's committed manifest and archives first and require byte identity with its committed locres for every culture. Only then compile the merged inputs and stage those locres.
8. Determine which files supply the translations. If `Game.po` is a generated export, it may lag the authoritative archive; compare it to that archive for omissions. If translators deliver changes in PO files, reconcile and import those edits first.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies when both branches touched the same localization target and the archives are JSON (the default UE format). An engine of a later minor version compiled the locres identically here, but that must be re-proven per engine build and per project with the parity step. A plugin listed in the dummy project's engine that fails to load must be disabled in the `.uproject` `Plugins` array, otherwise the commandlet exits before compiling. If the project's custom engine is available, prefer it for the compile. The method does not handle entries that both sides changed differently; those stay a human decision.

## Checks

- Round trip: `serialize(parse(file)) == file` for the kept side; report any other side's difference and confirm it is only the trailing newline.
- Entry counts: merged entries == kept side entries + the other side's additions - deletions, checked independently per manifest and culture against its own base and edits.
- Parity: compiled locres of the kept side's committed inputs == committed locres, all cultures, before compiling the merge.
- Content: parse or byte-search the merged locres for every key the other side added and for every key the kept side added since the merge base; both sets must be present. Prefer a format-aware reader; a byte match alone cannot associate a key with its translation.
- `.po` cross-check: entries present in the `.po` but absent from the archive on all three sides are pre-existing staleness, not merge loss.

## Evidence

A historical merge of UE 5.3 localization inputs on Windows (2026-11-06) matched the retained culture outputs byte-for-byte when compiled with a particular UE 5.8 source build. Merged outputs retained changes from both sides, and stale PO exports differed from the authoritative archives. Other compilers, legacy schemas and divergent same-entry edits remain untested. Epic describes the manifest, archive, PO exchange and compiled resource stages. [Epic, Localization Overview](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/localization-overview-for-unreal-engine?application_version=5.8)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-LOCAL-ONLY-WORK.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-LOCAL-ONLY-WORK
title: Keep Unreal editor work local by checking the shared DDC and the distributed compile paths
summary: A local Unreal build or editor session can still reach team infrastructure through the shared DDC, UBA/Horde remote compile and Incredibuild shader compile; disable the shared DDC per checkout with a User*.ini override and confirm each path from the logs.
kind: method
scope: Unreal Engine 5 projects on a studio network with a shared DDC and distributed compile agents, when work must stay on the local machine
keywords: [Unreal Engine, DDC, Derived Data Cache, shared DDC, UserEngine.ini, UnrealBuildTool, UBA, Horde, Incredibuild, XGE, distributed compile, local only, "Unreal Build Accelerator", "LogDerivedDataCache", "BuildConfiguration.xml", "bAllowUBAExecutor"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-06"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.8 source build
  platform: Windows desktop
---

## Guidance

When a task must stay on the local machine, check each path by which an editor build or editor session touches team infrastructure. Do not assume that a local command stays local.

1. The Derived Data Cache (DDC) stores regenerable engine/asset data. For a filesystem-backed shared DDC, disable it for one checkout only by creating `<Project>/Config/UserEngine.ini` with `[DerivedDataCacheStores]` and `Shared=(Type=FileSystem, Path=None)`. Use the store name the project's `DefaultEngine.ini` uses. Keep the file out of version control with `.git/info/exclude` (`<Project>/Config/User*.ini`) so nothing changes for the team. On editor start, `LogDerivedDataCache` must print `Shared: Disabled because the path is configured to 'None'`.
2. C++ compile. Unreal Build Accelerator (UBA) can execute compiler actions dispatched through Horde. UnrealBuildTool reads the per-user `%APPDATA%\Unreal Engine\UnrealBuildTool\BuildConfiguration.xml`. A configured `<Horde>` server and enabled `bAllowUBAExecutor` can allow remote actions. Confirm actual execution using the build output. Look for `RemoteExecutor:` and `Horde URL:` in the build output.
3. Shader compile. XGE is a separate distributed execution controller used by Incredibuild. The editor log line `Using XGE Controller for shader compilation` together with an installed Incredibuild (`xgConsole.exe`) means shader jobs can go to Incredibuild agents. `LogUbaHorde: ... Not Enabled` covers only UBA, not Incredibuild.

Report every path that is still active before claiming that the work is local only.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies to studio setups with a shared DDC, Horde/UBA or Incredibuild. Check the backend and executor actually selected; other versions and installations can use other services. The `User*.ini` layer (GameDirUser) affects only that checkout. The local DDC (engine `DerivedDataCache` folder and the local ZenServer) is still shared with other projects on the same engine on this machine. Treat host-local sharing separately if the requirement calls for per-project isolation.

## Checks

- Find the DDC proof line in the editor log by the `LogDerivedDataCache` category. Plain `Shared:` also matches `LogEOSShared:` and other categories.
- `git status` stays clean and `git check-ignore -v` names the exclude rule for the override file.
- The build output has no `RemoteExecutor:` lines when remote compile must be off.
- Check executor settings and logs before authorizing a build that must stay local. A selected remote controller and an actual remote action support different conclusions.

## Evidence

In a Windows UE 5.8 source-build session on 2026-11-06, the checkout override disabled its filesystem DDC store. The build output still identified remote C++ actions and the editor selected the XGE shader controller. Actual remote shader execution and a per-build local-only UBA configuration were not verified. Log categories and executor settings are version-dependent. [Epic, Horde and Unreal Build Accelerator](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/horde-unreal-build-accelerator-and-remote-compilation-tutorial-for-unreal-engine?application_version=5.8)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-NATIVE-STRUCT-DECODING.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-NATIVE-STRUCT-DECODING
title: How to decode an Unreal struct property that may or may not use native serialization
summary: A struct with a native serializer is still saved as tagged properties when its Serialize returns false. Decode known native layouts by the custom version gates in their Serialize source; otherwise try a tagged stream and accept it only when it consumes the value size exactly.
kind: method
scope: Standalone readers of uncooked Unreal Engine 5 packages that decode StructProperty values and pre-1012 Set or Map struct elements without the engine
keywords: [Unreal Engine, uasset, StructProperty, native serialization, STRUCT_SerializeNative, Serialize returns false, tagged fallback, exact size, custom version, MovieSceneFloatChannel, MovieSceneDoubleChannel, SmartName, PerQualityLevelInt, UniversalObjectLocatorFragment, FontData, FrameRate, FTimeline, Set, Map, 구조체 직렬화, 언리얼 에셋, "FStructProperty", "UScriptStruct::UseNativeSerialization", "UScriptStruct::SerializeItem", "RawStructPropertyData", "UAssetAPI", "PerQualityLevelFloat", "AlphaBlend", "InputAlphaBoolBlend", "GameplayEffectModifierMagnitude"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-10"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.8 stock serialization
  package: uncooked, versioned properties, object versions 1004 to 1018
---

## Guidance

`FStructProperty` describes a reflected structure property. `UScriptStruct::UseNativeSerialization` reports whether a native serializer exists. `UScriptStruct::SerializeItem` serializes an instance; the archive, custom versions and native serializer's return determine the path used. `STRUCT_SerializeNative` alone cannot identify the bytes on disk.

1. **Known native layout:** decode it with the custom version gates read from the struct's `Serialize` source, not from the property tag. Layouts checked in UE 5.8:
   - `SmartName`: FName, then uint16 if AnimPhys custom version < 5, then a Guid if AnimPhys < 10.
   - `PerQualityLevelInt` and `PerQualityLevelFloat`: bool32 cooked, Default, then `TMap<int32, T>`.
   - `UniversalObjectLocatorFragment`: FName type, then the payload as a tagged stream.
   - `FontData`: at Editor custom version 9 (AddedFontFaceAssets) or later, bool32 cooked, then tagged; earlier, tagged only.
   - `MovieSceneFloatChannel` and `MovieSceneDoubleChannel` (`MovieSceneCurveChannelImpl.cpp`), when Sequencer custom version ≥ 12 or FortniteMain ≥ 53: uint8 pre- and post-extrapolation; int32 element size, int32 count and raw key times; int32 element size, int32 count and raw key values; the default value; bool32 has-default; int32 tick resolution numerator and denominator; bool32 show-curve if FortniteMain ≥ 53. Below both gates the channel is tagged.
2. **No known layout:** try reading a tagged stream inside the value's size, and accept it only if it ends exactly at the size; otherwise keep the raw bytes and report the value as unsupported. Structs whose `Serialize` returns false are tagged even though they declare a native serializer, for example `FTimeline`, `AlphaBlend`, `InputAlphaBoolBlend`, `GameplayEffectModifierMagnitude`. `FrameRate` is tagged.
3. **Pre-1012 Set and Map elements** do not record their struct name. Try candidate layouts (a tagged stream, a 16-byte Guid, then any domain layout such as the Niagara variable) and accept a candidate only when independent element boundaries and version/type evidence support it. If several fit or boundaries cannot be established, retain the bytes as unresolved.

## Applicability and exceptions

The exact-size test is evidence, not proof: another layout can fit by chance. Tagged streams carry low risk because they must end with a `None` tag; raw fixed-size candidates carry more. Gates and layouts change between engine versions; recheck them in the target engine's source. Big-endian packages with raw arrays need byte swapping.

## Checks

- Never accept a partial fit; leftover bytes are a decoding error.
- Sweep content and count unsupported values by struct name; frequent ones deserve a native decoder.
- When wrapping another parser, propagate its opaque struct types as unsupported/partial even when the outer export read succeeds. For UAssetAPI 1.1.0, `RawStructPropertyData` retains raw bytes; it does not establish a decoded value. Exercise nested arrays in a real engine-saved asset as well as scalar fixtures.

## Evidence

Historical source reading and an editor-content sweep on 2026-11-10 (UE 5.8, versioned uncooked objects 1004–1018) improved exact boundary consumption; some old container elements remained unidentified. A separate wrapper check on 2026-11-13 found that nested raw struct payloads in an engine-saved animation had been counted as decoded. Treating those properties as unsupported and their exports as partial corrected the reported coverage. This did not verify individual native struct values against the Editor. Other versions and byte orders require new comparisons. [Epic, UScriptStruct](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/CoreUObject/UScriptStruct?application_version=5.8) · [UAssetAPI, RawStructPropertyData](https://github.com/atenfyr/UAssetAPI/blob/master/UAssetAPI/PropertyTypes/Structs/RawStructPropertyData.cs)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-PIE-VERIFICATION.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-PIE-VERIFICATION
title: Verify actual Unreal PIE through the native Editor automation API
summary: Use the installed engine's Editor automation API for authorized PIE tests,
  then independently check WorldType, the requested consumer, rendering, runtime results
  and build identity. Standalone game execution and a successful process exit do not
  establish PIE.
kind: method
scope: Unreal Engine 5.8.3 installed Windows C++ Editor and Development Game verification;
  adapt and recheck APIs for other versions or projects
keywords: ["Play In Editor","PIE","UnrealEd","FEditorLoadMap","FStartPIECommand","FEndPlayMapCommand","UWorld::WorldType","EWorldType::PIE","Target.bBuildEditor","latent automation command","rendered viewport","orthographic camera","snapshot lifetime","backpressure","에디터 자동화","플레이 월드"]
status: active
checked_at: '2026-11-11'
context:
  engine: "UE 5.8.3 Windows D3D11, Editor automation and separately cooked Development Game"
  verification: "Historical world, rendering, runtime-policy and owned-output checks; other projects and engine releases require rechecking"
---

## Guidance

Use the Editor automation commands in the installed engine's `AutomationEditorCommon.h`. In UE 5.8.3, `FEditorLoadMap` loads the test map, `FStartPIECommand(false)` requests Play In Editor (PIE) and `FEndPlayMapCommand` requests its end. Latent commands can span frames. Wait until the play world exists and the tested consumer finishes before teardown. Gate the `UnrealEd` module dependency on `Target.bBuildEditor` and use the Editor/automation compile guards appropriate to the target.

Inspect the test world's `UWorld::WorldType` against `EWorldType::PIE`. A standalone `-game` launch has a game world and exercises a separate contract. Match the launched Editor module and its source, configuration and test driver to the intended build. Require both the automation result and the consumer's runtime result. Rendering claims require a rendering backend, the expected viewport, and actual captured content. Fixed-time video and ordinary packaged frame timing answer different questions.

## Applicability and exceptions

Historical checks exercised native C++ Editor automation and separately cooked Development Game builds on UE 5.8.3, Windows/D3D11. Verify the latent command signatures, play-world creation, camera path, teardown and assets for each receiving project. Networked PIE, other graphics backends and live autosave during simulation need independent checks.

## Checks

- Reject a standalone world, wrong consumer, stale result, unfinished activity, nested failure, missing viewport or unexpected dimensions. Check rendering flags and test mode against the running implementation. Deliberately altering a label, policy or trace launcher must invalidate its evidence.
- Inspect pixels at the relevant screen position and the visual mesh component's world transform alongside the collision component. In an orthographic camera, automatic view-origin or near/far-plane adjustment can shift intended framing. Compare actual pixels and projected positions, including HUD occlusion. Test the first, intermediate and terminal states before despawn.
- Reconstruct original request IDs, arrivals, admissions, refusals, completions and oldest waiting request from individual records when checking service. Compare implementations at identical simulation times and validate the observed settings and results of both runs.
- For snapshot verification, compare value types, escaping, precision, member/array order when contractual, and omitted/duplicate members. Mutate and destroy source data before writing. Detach all inputs before background work, including metadata and strings, keeping live `UObject` and world access on the game thread. Exercise bounded input ownership, refusal and retry, FIFO completion, write failure, and pending work at exit. Normal polling can be nonblocking while shutdown drains. A temporary-file rename does not establish crash durability.

## Evidence

The historical reports below recorded actual PIE and packaged results, including a standalone-world rejection, detection of displaced visual geometry and incorrect runtime settings, output parity, and draining an accepted save during shutdown. A partially owned snapshot still read live world data. A later path deep-owned all writer inputs and tested them after source destruction. The original reports establish these observations within their tested conditions. No new Editor, performance or gameplay run was performed in this review.

- [Epic, EWorldType::Type](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/EWorldType__Type?application_version=5.8) · `UnrealEd/Public/Tests/AutomationEditorCommon.h` (UE 5.8.3)
- [PIE automation](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-editor-gate-results-md) · [Orthographic framing](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-traffic-results-md) · [Mesh transforms](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-stream-results-md) · [Queue and policy identity](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-admission-results-md)
- [Viewport and trace settings](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-hitch-results-md) · [Planner comparison](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-planner-results-md) · [Snapshot parity](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-snapshot-results-md) · [Worker completion](../sources.md#src-ue-lab-async-save-results-md)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-RENDER-ORIENTATION.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-RENDER-ORIENTATION
title: How to map an Unreal project's rendering setup before studying or optimizing it
summary: Classify the rendering path from renderer settings, find engine renderer changes by marker or history, and locate project render hooks by searching for view extensions, global shaders and shader files, before planning study or optimization.
kind: method
scope: Unreal Engine 5 game projects, especially ones with a custom engine and in-project rendering plugins
keywords: [Unreal Engine, rendering, renderer settings, Nanite, Lumen, Virtual Shadow Maps, distance field shadows, static lighting, SceneViewExtension, ISceneViewExtension, RDG, FRDGBuilder, IMPLEMENT_GLOBAL_SHADER, usf, vertex factory, scalability, PSO, "Render Dependency Graph", "FRDGBuilder::AddPass", "FGlobalShader", "FDeferredShadingSceneRenderer::Render", "pipeline state object"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-05"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.x
  platform: Windows desktop
---

## Guidance

Map the rendering setup in this order. Each step is a short read or search.

1. **Classify the rendering path** from `Config/DefaultEngine.ini` `[/Script/Engine.RendererSettings]`: `r.Nanite`, `r.DynamicGlobalIlluminationMethod` (Lumen), `r.ReflectionMethod`, `r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable`, `r.AllowStaticLighting`, `r.GenerateMeshDistanceFields`, `r.CustomDepth`, forward or deferred shading. Nanite is virtualized geometry, Lumen provides dynamic global illumination/reflections, and Virtual Shadow Maps (VSM) provide paged high-resolution shadows. Read the forward-shading switch separately. Availability of static lighting or mesh distance fields alone does not establish their use by any particular pass.
2. **Read quality tiers**: `DefaultScalability.ini`, `DefaultDeviceProfiles.ini` and the game user settings class that applies scalability, anti-aliasing and upscalers. Settings repeated at every tier (for example `r.ForceLODShadow`) deserve a look.
3. **Measure the engine renderer delta**: in the engine checkout (see [K-CODE-UE-ENGINE-CHECKOUT](K-CODE-UE-ENGINE-CHECKOUT.md)), search the studio's modification marker under `Renderer`, `RenderCore`, `RHI`, the RHI backends and `Engine/Shaders`, or filter `git log` to those paths. Use the ownership of the measured pass to choose where to investigate; engine edits also carry a merge cost.
4. **Find project render hooks**: search project `Source/` and `Plugins/` for `SceneViewExtension`, `FGlobalShader`, `IMPLEMENT_GLOBAL_SHADER`, `FRDGBuilder`, `AddPass`, scene proxies, vertex factories, and `*.usf` and `*.ush` files. Separate first-party code from third-party upscaler plugins (DLSS, XeSS, FSR), which are useful RDG reading material but not owned code. `ISceneViewExtension` supplies callbacks in the view lifecycle. `FGlobalShader` is a shader type independent of a material and `IMPLEMENT_GLOBAL_SHADER` registers its implementation. RDG (Render Dependency Graph) describes passes and their resource dependencies; `FRDGBuilder::AddPass` adds a pass to that graph. A vertex factory supplies mesh vertex data to a shader. A pipeline state object (PSO) bundles graphics pipeline state; PSO creation or compilation is one possible source of hitches.
5. **Find existing measurement**: in-game telemetry of render views, pixels, GPU memory or PSO hitches, and any profiling workflow the team already uses. Use it to rank candidates before changing structure.

Editorial judgment, not verified as a learning outcome: for study, start with a GPU frame capture (RenderDoc, `ProfileGPU` or Unreal Insights GPU tracks) and name each pass against `FDeferredShadingSceneRenderer::Render`; then follow one primitive from component to scene proxy, mesh batch, mesh pass processor and mesh draw command, using a project plugin with its own vertex factory as the concrete example. The smallest first-party view extension is a good first read for the render thread and game thread boundary.

## Applicability and exceptions

Steps 1 to 5 apply to any UE5 project. Step 3 needs engine source; with a launcher engine, treat the renderer as stock. For a Lumen and Nanite project, step 1 points to a different study and cost set (Lumen scene, Nanite rasterization, VSM page cost).

## Checks

- Every optimization candidate taken from this mapping is a hypothesis until a capture or telemetry measures it. Do not report a setting as a cost without measurement.
- Confirm that config values are not overridden by platform ini files, device profiles or user settings before treating them as effective.
- Large counts of lights or casters noted in code comments are inputs to measure, not evidence of cost.

## Evidence

A Windows UE 5.8 source/configuration review on 2026-11-05 identified renderer settings, engine modifications and project-owned rendering hooks. Effective runtime overrides, GPU cost and the proposed study sequence were not validated. Epic's RDG and FRDGBuilder references describe graph construction and pass scheduling. [Epic, Render Dependency Graph](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/render-dependency-graph-in-unreal-engine?application_version=5.8) · [FRDGBuilder](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/RenderCore/FRDGBuilder?application_version=5.8)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-RENDERDOC-ATTACH.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-RENDERDOC-ATTACH
title: Attach RenderDoc to an Unreal editor and get past the startup blockers
summary: "Attach the engine's RenderDoc plugin, verify readiness in the current Editor log and inspect modal warnings when startup stalls. NGX initialization and DLSS availability depend on the plugin and capture environment."
kind: method
scope: Unreal Engine 5 editors on Windows D3D12 where a frame must be captured with RenderDoc, especially projects that ship the NVIDIA DLSS plugin
keywords: [Unreal Engine, RenderDoc, RenderDocPlugin, AttachRenderDoc, renderdoc.CaptureFrame, renderdoc.CapturePIE, DLSS, NGX, frame capture, editor startup hang, D3D12, "renderdoc.AutoAttach", "Render Hardware Interface", "EventGPUDuration", "ProfileGPU", "GetRootActions"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-06"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.8 source build
  platform: Windows desktop, D3D12
  tool: RenderDoc 1.47
---

## Guidance

1. Configure the RenderDocPlugin to locate `renderdoc.dll`, using its installation path or the plugin settings supported by that engine version.
2. Start the editor with `-AttachRenderDoc`. Without it, the log shows `RenderDoc plugin will not be loaded`. A ready plugin logs `RenderDoc plugin is ready!`.
3. In the observed combination of NVIDIA DLSS (Deep Learning Super Sampling) and RenderDoc, initialization of the NVIDIA NGX SDK failed with `NVSDK_NGX_Result_FAIL_PlatformError`, and in the editor the plugin opens a modal Yes/No/Cancel warning titled `Warning -- DLSS incompatible with plugins and/or API capture tools`. Startup stops until it is answered. Yes continues and changes nothing. No continues and hides the warning. Cancel exits the editor. DLSS stays off for that run, so captures show the engine upscaler path instead of DLSS. Verify support separately when the requested capture must include DLSS.
4. Capture with `renderdoc.CaptureFrame` or the viewport toolbar button, or with `renderdoc.CapturePIE` for a game frame. Files go to `<Project>/Saved/RenderDocCaptures/`. On first launch RenderDoc asks about anonymous analytics. Choose no submission when work must stay local.

5. To list the pass tree without driving the UI, run `qrenderdoc.exe --python <script.py>`. The script runs in the embedded Python 3.8 with the `renderdoc` module. `__file__` is not defined there, in the observed context, so pass explicit input and output paths. Arrange orderly closure and an explicit failure exit. An uncaught error leaves the UI open and the process waiting. Walk `controller.GetRootActions()` recursively, and name each action with `action.GetName(controller.GetStructuredFile())`.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies to editor builds. The DLSS dialog is compiled only `WITH_EDITOR`, and it is skipped when unattended or when the project or local DLSS setting disables it. Other capture tools that NGX reports as incompatible behave the same way.

## Checks

- Editor log stops growing for minutes while the process still responds: check the main window title for a modal dialog before calling it a hang.
- When waiting for a line in the editor log after a restart, first confirm that the log header shows the new start time. The old log stays in place until the new process rotates it, so a wait loop can match stale lines.
- `Total Editor Startup Time` includes the time a modal dialog waited for the user.
- Do not split cost by marker range alone. Unreal GPU markers can nest wrongly in a capture, so one marker may span most of the frame. Count draws and dispatches per leaf pass instead.
- If `FetchCounters([EventGPUDuration])` returns an empty list in a scripted replay, measure pass times in the engine (`ProfileGPU`) instead.

## Evidence

In a UE 5.8 source-build Editor on Windows D3D12 (2026-11-06), RenderDoc API 1.7.0 attached, NGX initialization failed, and an Editor modal held startup. Acknowledging it allowed an .rdc capture to open in RenderDoc 1.47 with DLSS inactive. A stale log caused a false readiness result. Scripted replay enumerated actions; a missing script-path variable stalled one run, and GPU-duration queries yielded no usable timings. Other plugin versions and DLSS-enabled capture were not tested. [Epic, Using RenderDoc with Unreal Engine](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/using-renderdoc-with-unreal-engine?application_version=5.8)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-UE-SKELETAL-LOD.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-UE-SKELETAL-LOD
title: How Unreal picks a skeletal mesh LOD, and why a mesh with its tick off keeps a stale LOD
summary: "The render thread computes a desired skeletal mesh LOD and the component tick applies it. When suspending ticks, check the applied level and allow any forced selection to take effect before suspension."
kind: reference
scope: Unreal Engine 5 skeletal mesh components (USkinnedMeshComponent, USkeletalMeshComponent), non-Nanite skeletal meshes
keywords: [Unreal Engine, skeletal mesh, LOD, level of detail, MinDesiredLODLevel, UpdateMinDesiredLODLevel, FSkeletalMeshObject, UpdateLODStatus, TickComponent, SetForcedLOD, ForcedLodModel, r.SkeletalMeshLODBias, LOD bias, r.SkeletalMeshLODRadiusScale, ScreenSize, LODHysteresis, ComputeBoundsScreenRadiusSquared, MaxDistanceFactor, EngineShowFlags.LOD, bThumbnailRendering, GetIsInFocus, FSceneView, FSceneViewFamily, view relevance, double buffering, one-frame latency, render thread, game thread, mutex, race condition, clamp, hysteresis, LOD popping, scalability, "FSkeletalMeshObject::UpdateMinDesiredLODLevel", "USkinnedMeshComponent::TickComponent", "USkinnedMeshComponent::SetForcedLOD", "SetComponentTickEnabled"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-19"
context:
  engine: Unreal Engine 5.8 source
---

## Guidance

**Flow (who computes, who applies).**

1. **Render thread computes.** While the scene proxy gathers view relevance, `FSkeletalMeshObject::UpdateMinDesiredLODLevel` (`SkeletalRender.cpp`) runs once per view per frame.
   - It skips thumbnail views (`View->Family->bThumbnailRendering`) and view families that are not in focus (`GetIsInFocus()`; only editor viewports set this false, the default is focused).
   - It computes the screen radius squared of the bounds sphere (`ComputeBoundsScreenRadiusSquared`) and multiplies it by `LODScale` squared. `LODScale` is `r.SkeletalMeshLODRadiusScale` clamped to 0.25 to 1.0, so this cvar can only lower detail.
   - If `EngineShowFlags.LOD` is on, it walks LODs from worst to LOD1 and picks the first whose `(ScreenSize * 0.5)^2` exceeds the screen radius squared; LOD0 is the fallback. `ScreenSize` is a projected screen-size threshold.
   - Hysteresis: for LODs equal to or better than the current one, `LODHysteresis` is added to `ScreenSize`, so returning to a better LOD needs a larger on-screen size than leaving it. This prevents LOD popping at a boundary.
2. **Double buffering across views.** On the first call in a new frame number, the previous frame's working values are copied to the values the game thread reads (`MinDesiredLODLevel`, `MaxDistanceFactor`), and the working values restart. Later calls in the same frame (split screen, stereo) keep the minimum LOD and the maximum screen size. A mutex guards this only when the family has more than one view, because relevance runs in parallel.
3. **Game thread applies.** `USkinnedMeshComponent::TickComponent` calls `UpdateLODStatus` (`SkinnedMeshComponent.cpp`), which sets the LOD to `Clamp(MinDesiredLODLevel + LODBias, 0, MaxLOD)` unless a forced LOD is set. The result is one frame behind the render-thread computation.

**Pitfall.** If anything disables the mesh component tick (a custom sleep or idle optimization, `SetComponentTickEnabled(false)`), step 3 stops while step 1 keeps running for a visible mesh. The applied LOD freezes at its last value, so a frozen character seen far away stays at a coarse LOD when the camera approaches. A sufficiently negative global bias can mask stale selection at the cost of detail reduction for other meshes. `USkinnedMeshComponent::SetForcedLOD` takes a one-based selection (0 clears the override) and Epic documents application on the next tick. If a fixed LOD is appropriate, request it while ticks still run, observe it being applied, then suspend ticking. Clear it and verify automatic selection after resuming.

## Applicability and exceptions

Applies to skeletal meshes rendered through `FSkeletalMeshObject`. Nanite skeletal meshes call the same function from their own proxy path, but their geometry detail is decided by Nanite, so verify separately. Scene captures do vote like normal views. Animation update rate optimization and visibility-based anim tick are separate systems: they can change pose update frequency without the component tick being off.

## Checks

- To see the effective LOD in game, use `a.VisualizeLODs 1` and temporarily set the bias cvar to 0.
- Search the project for `SetComponentTickEnabled(false)`, `PrimaryComponentTick.bCanEverTick`, and custom freeze or sleep states on actors with skeletal meshes; for each, check whether a forced LOD is set.
- Check each asset's LOD Settings (`ScreenSize`, `LODHysteresis`) before re-enabling LOD; once a global bias is removed, also check effective bias, minimum LOD, parent/leader synchronization and forced overrides.
- Measure GPU and CPU before and after; a LOD change is a hypothesis until a capture shows the saving.

## Evidence

UE 5.8 source inspection on 2026-11-19 covered `SkeletalRender.cpp` (view filtering and accumulation), `SkinnedMeshComponent.cpp` (component update and clamp), `SceneView.h` and `EditorViewportClient.cpp` (focus). The stale selection was inferred from code. Neither the symptom nor the proposed ordering was tested in a running game, and no performance saving was measured. [Epic, FSkeletalMeshObject::UpdateMinDesiredLODLevel](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/FSkeletalMeshObject/UpdateMinDesiredLODLevel?application_version=5.8) · [USkinnedMeshComponent](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/USkinnedMeshComponent?application_version=5.8) · [Set Forced LOD](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/BlueprintAPI/Components/SkinnedMesh/SetForcedLOD?application_version=5.8)

===== knowledge/code/K-CODE-WPF-LARGE-TREE.md =====

---
id: K-CODE-WPF-LARGE-TREE
title: How to keep a WPF property tree with thousands of rows responsive
summary: Build child rows on first read, bind a row's children only while it is expanded, and show the section headers and top-level rows as one flat virtualized list. Expanders that each hold a plain ItemsControl inside a ScrollViewer create every row.
kind: lesson
scope: WPF views that show large hierarchical data (property grids, Details panels, tree-like lists) with ItemsControl templates
keywords: [WPF, virtualization, UI virtualization, VirtualizingStackPanel, CanContentScroll, ScrollUnit, VirtualizationMode, ItemsControl, Expander, ScrollViewer, lazy loading, property tree, tree view performance, 가상화, 지연 로딩, "VirtualizationMode.Recycling", "IReadOnlyList<T>", "ItemsSource", "DataTemplate", "data virtualization", "container recycling"]
status: active
checked_at: "2026-11-10"
context:
  framework: .NET 10 WPF
  platform: Windows desktop
---

## Guidance

UI virtualization creates visual containers only as items enter the visible range. Data virtualization and lazy construction determine when backing objects exist. Container recycling (`VirtualizationMode.Recycling`) can reuse a visual while scrolling; keep per-item state in the bound item.

1. **Lazy children:** give each row a child list that reports `Count` without building its children (an `IReadOnlyList<T>` that builds on first index or enumeration). An expand arrow needs only the count.
2. **Bind children on expand:** in the row's `DataTemplate`, leave the child `ItemsControl` collapsed with no `ItemsSource`; a trigger on the toggle's `IsChecked` sets `ItemsSource` and `Visibility`. A collapsed row then creates no child containers.
3. **One virtualized list:** flatten the section headers, notices and top-level rows into one list in the view model and show it in a single `ItemsControl` whose template has a `ScrollViewer` with `CanContentScroll="True"` and whose panel is a `VirtualizingStackPanel` (`VirtualizingPanel.IsVirtualizing`, `ScrollUnit="Pixel"`). Use a header item with an `IsExpanded` property that rebuilds the list, instead of an `Expander` per section.
4. **Cheap row text:** stop building a one-line summary of a nested value once it exceeds the display width, and fall back to a type name.

## Applicability and exceptions

An outer `ScrollViewer` around `ItemsControl`s, or an `ItemsControl` without a virtualizing panel, measures every item, so virtualization silently does nothing. Nested child lists in expanded rows are still not virtualized; cap their length or flatten them too. A `TreeView` with virtualization enabled is an alternative that was not compared.

## Checks

- Time the view from data assignment to layout complete (`UpdateLayout` after the dispatcher settles) on the largest real data set, against a budget.
- Count created rows or containers before and after, not only elapsed time.

## Evidence

A Windows .NET 10 WPF asset-inspection view (Debug, 2026-11-10) built rows faster and became usable sooner after deferred row construction and a single virtualized host. A heavily loaded run showed little margin in another view. Release latency, scrolling and memory were not measured. Microsoft describes conditions that disable virtualization and the difference between container recycling and data virtualization. [Microsoft, Optimizing performance for WPF controls](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/advanced/optimizing-performance-controls)

===== records/code/R-CODE-GIT-INDEX-LOCK-RENAME.md =====

---
id: R-CODE-GIT-INDEX-LOCK-RENAME
title: "Verify index and refs after an interrupted Windows checkout"
summary: "An interrupted Git for Windows switch can leave updated files and a pending index while HEAD still names the old branch. Inspect the actual state before deciding how to recover."
kind: error
scope: "Git for Windows, interrupted checkout in a clean linked worktree with Git LFS filters"
keywords: ["Git for Windows","index.lock","GIT_INDEX_FILE","reflog","symbolic-ref","HEAD","git worktree","Git LFS","인덱스"]
status: resolved
checked_at: '2026-11-18'
trigger: "An index replacement prompts for retry during checkout and the terminal cannot accept input"
symptom: "The new worktree files existed, the temporary index remained and the expected branch reference and HEAD update were absent"
cause_status: suspected
prevention: "Use an input-capable terminal and minimize concurrent status polling for the duration of large checkouts"
prevention_status: proposed
---

## Expected and observed

The checkout reached its index replacement step but the retry prompt could not receive input. The worktree, index, branch ref and symbolic HEAD had reached different stages. A transient open handle was suspected. Its owner was never identified.

## Cause and resolution

Git's index stages paths and objects. `index.lock` may hold its pending replacement. `GIT_INDEX_FILE` selects an alternate index and permits read-only comparison on a **copy**. The reflog records local ref movements, so it can disambiguate the commit intended before a remote-tracking ref moved. `HEAD` normally refers to the checked-out branch. Identify the owning worktree, any live writer, local changes and intended commit before interpreting any lock as stale.

## Recheck and prevention

In the recorded clean-worktree recovery, both index files were backed up, the copy of the pending index matched the intended commit, the writer was stopped and the checked index and branch/HEAD were finalized. The resulting status, branch, materialized LFS content and lock state were verified. When the retry prompt is usable, allow the original operation to finish. A manual completion can omit checkout hooks, and matching a commit tree is insufficient when local changes must be carried. Reduced polling remains a proposed precaution.

## Evidence

One historical Windows worktree observation, 2026-11-18. Public references establish Git terminology, not the transient handle's identity. [Git glossary](https://git-scm.com/docs/gitglossary) · [git-reflog](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-reflog) · [Worktree isolation](../../knowledge/code/K-CODE-GIT-ISOLATED-MERGE.md)

===== records/code/R-CODE-UE-EVIDENCE-PROXY.md =====

---
id: R-CODE-UE-EVIDENCE-PROXY
title: "Self-consistent event values can hide an incorrect transition"
summary: "A transition validator accepted compressed wall time and mutually consistent fabricated event values. Bind elapsed time and the values on both sides of an event to independent expectations."
kind: error
scope: "Native real-time or fixed-step transition validation, historically UE 5.8.3 on Win64"
keywords: ["test oracle","monotonic clock","std::chrono::steady_clock","simulation time","negative control","easing","transition","wall age","event continuity","검증"]
status: resolved
checked_at: '2026-11-12'
trigger: "Accepting transition output from its own reported time, counters or neighboring event samples"
symptom: "Shortening wall time, zeroing elapsed duration or substituting equal but incorrect before/after values left the original check green"
cause_status: confirmed
prevention: "Compute expected event values independently and check elapsed time against the clock governing the contract"
prevention_status: verified
---

## Expected and observed

The samples agreed with each other even when the transition ran at the wrong speed or both sides of an event were changed to the same wrong value.

## Cause and resolution

A test oracle supplies the expected outcome independently of the output it evaluates. Derive event values from the schedule and curve. For a real-time contract, compare the elapsed interval with a monotonic clock and account for sampling uncertainty. For a fixed-step simulation, compare with simulation time. In portable C++, `std::chrono::steady_clock` supplies monotonic intervals. It is an implementation option, not an assertion about the historical timer.

## Recheck and prevention

The historical negative controls shortened the observed interval, discarded duration and fabricated both event values. Each change exposed the original blind spot and was rejected after repair. Keep those failure classes separate. Measurement correctness alone does not establish a frame-rate budget or perceptual quality.

## Evidence

Transition-validator behavior was historically checked under UE 5.8.3. The clock reference establishes the C++ contract only. [C++ working draft, time.clock.steady](https://eel.is/c++draft/time.clock.steady) · [Historical transition evidence](../../knowledge/sources.md#src-ue-lab-easing-results-md) · [Fixed-step checks](../../knowledge/game/K-GAME-TECH-TIMESTEP.md)

===== records/code/R-CODE-UE-PARSER-NAME-INDEX.md =====

---
id: R-CODE-UE-PARSER-NAME-INDEX
title: "Check serialized name indices before accepting a decoded property"
summary: "UAssetAPI 1.1.0 can return decimal text for an invalid name-map reference. A consumer must validate the name reference and distinguish opaque export data from decoded properties."
kind: error
scope: "Versioned, single-file uncooked packages read with UAssetAPI 1.1.0"
keywords: ["FName","name map","UAssetAPI","AssetBinaryReader.ReadFName","UAsset.GetNameReference","RawExport","serialization","uncooked package"]
status: resolved
checked_at: '2026-11-13'
trigger: "Treating successful library reads as sufficient proof of a valid serialized property name"
symptom: "A name outside the package name map became decimal property text and the consumer reported full decoding"
cause_status: confirmed
prevention: "Validate the serialized name index and instance number, preserve opaque data as partial and recheck valid controls"
prevention_status: verified
---

## Expected and observed

A consumer reported an apparently named property where the serialized name reference was invalid. Other readable properties made the result look complete.

## Cause and resolution

`FName` is Unreal's compact name representation. In this package format the serialized name uses a name-map index and a number. `AssetBinaryReader.ReadFName` reads those fields. At the cited revision, `UAsset.GetNameReference` can turn an out-of-range reference into decimal text. When export data is preserved as a `RawExport`, its availability as bytes does not establish property-level decoding.

## Recheck and prevention

The historical reader check distinguished invalid references from valid uncooked UE4 and UE5 controls, and reported opaque output as partial. Retain both checks when changing backend versions. Cooked and split packages and independent editor comparison require separate evidence.

## Evidence

The originating reader project's verification report records the consumer checks and their coverage. The upstream references below explain the library behavior separately.

The upstream source pins the observed fallback and read operation. The scoped consumer regression and controls were recorded on 2026-11-13. [Epic, FName](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/fname-in-unreal-engine) · [UAsset.GetNameReference](https://github.com/atenfyr/UAssetAPI/blob/73530811a9d91ffa8959a1a4cb74cc3b9c5c6bed/UAssetAPI/UAsset.cs) · [AssetBinaryReader.ReadFName](https://github.com/atenfyr/UAssetAPI/blob/73530811a9d91ffa8959a1a4cb74cc3b9c5c6bed/UAssetAPI/AssetBinaryReader.cs)

===== records/code/R-CODE-UE-TRANSFORM-TOLERANCE.md =====

---
id: R-CODE-UE-TRANSFORM-TOLERANCE
title: "An exact easing endpoint can yield a tolerance-limited component position"
summary: "A scalar easing calculation finished at its target while a scene component retained a slightly different position. Compare mathematical convergence and the published transform independently."
kind: error
scope: "UE 5.8.3, collisionless single-axis translation with a scene component"
keywords: ["USceneComponent::SetWorldLocation","SetWorldLocation","USceneComponent::InternalSetWorldLocationAndRotation","TVector::Equals","UE_KINDA_SMALL_NUMBER","easing","floating-point tolerance","endpoint","정밀도"]
status: resolved
checked_at: '2026-11-12'
trigger: "Asserting the same endpoint tolerance for the scalar curve and the observed scene transform"
symptom: "The curve reached its endpoint while the remaining native displacement fell below the setter's comparison threshold"
cause_status: confirmed
prevention: "Check the engine version and compare policy value, time and published component position separately"
prevention_status: verified
---

## Expected and observed

A strict comparison of the scalar curve confirmed completion, yet the world-space position read back from the component still differed from the target. Repeating the identical setter call did not guarantee a bit-exact endpoint.

## Cause and resolution

`USceneComponent::SetWorldLocation` requests world-space translation. In the historically inspected UE 5.8.3 path, `USceneComponent::InternalSetWorldLocationAndRotation` compared relative location using `TVector::Equals`, a per-component tolerance comparison. The default vector tolerance there was `UE_KINDA_SMALL_NUMBER`. A sufficiently small change could therefore leave the stored transform alone even after the scalar reached its target.

## Recheck and prevention

Historical negative controls rejected a wrong scalar value inside the native tolerance and independently rejected an incorrect published position. Retain a strict curve check, a time check and a native position check with a bound justified for the selected axes and parent transform. Reassess swept movement, scaled parents and other engine versions separately.

## Evidence

The API references identify the public types. The setter's internal comparison and the two rejected substitutions are historical UE 5.8.3 observations. The source experiment was not rerun in this review. [Epic, USceneComponent](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Engine/Components/USceneComponent?application_version=5.5) · [Epic, TVector](https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/API/Runtime/Core/TVector) · [Easing evidence history](../../knowledge/sources.md#src-ue-lab-easing-results-md)
